# Native parity checklist

This file is generated by `build/fusion_rebuild --checklist` from
the automatic source inventory and the canonical human annotations in
`data/native_parity/annotations.tsv`. Do not edit it directly; edit the
TSV (one row per feature, tab-separated columns, `|`-joined lists) and
regenerate this view.

> Scope warning: this static inventory indexes C/assembly functions, header
> declarations, top-level data, named aggregate types, object-like constants
> and function-pointer table dispatch when it is lexically provable. Dynamic
> callbacks and runtime observations remain open. Counts are discovery
> coverage, not game fidelity percentages.

## Status legend

- `[ ]` not implemented
- `[~]` partially implemented
- `[+]` ported, unit-tested and checked in game
- `[x]` behaviorally compared against an emulator
- `[?]` research or identification required
- `[!]` blocked or known problem

## Metroid: Zero Mission

### Startup and initialization

### Title screen

[?] **`mzm.title.inventory-boundary` — source coverage.** The current classifier assigns 0 discovered routine(s) to this category. Category completeness has not been established.

[+] **`mzm.title.intro` — Intro.** "The runtime boots on the intro without arguments or with `--intro`, and returns to it from the file select (B) and from the game over's quit, as GM_INTRO. It runs IntroHandler frame by frame on the native screen's PPU with the ROM bank's graphics, tile tables, palettes and OAM frames: the fade from black, ""EMERGENCY ORDER"" typed letter by letter with its letter sounds, palette flashes and blinking marker (IntroProcessText's action tables), the ship flying towards the camera as double-size affine sprites (ProcessComplexOam, CalculateOamPart4, the intro music and ship sound), Samus in her ship with the shaking camera, ""EXTERMINATE ALL METROID ORGANISMS..."", the view of Zebes with the ship and its heat blended, ""AND DEFEAT THE MECHANICAL LIFE-FORM, MOTHER BRAIN"", Mother Brain's jar scrolling with its music, and the fuzz (the OBJ palette rewritten from scattered ROM data by IntroFuzzVBlank). It then leads to the title with its comets; A, B or START fades all sounds and the music (FadeAllSounds and FadeMusic, 10 frames) and leads to the title past its comets. The test runs the whole intro on the exported bank and checks the stage order and timings, the sounds, the letters, the affine parameters, the fuzz palette and the skip. Checked by capture: every stage, the end on the title's comets and the skip."

Native evidence: `mzm:c:src/complex_oam.c:CalculateOamPart4`, `mzm:c:src/complex_oam.c:ProcessComplexOam`, `mzm:c:src/intro.c:IntroDefeat`, `mzm:c:src/intro.c:IntroEmergencyOrder`, `mzm:c:src/intro.c:IntroExterminate`, `mzm:c:src/intro.c:IntroFuzz`, `mzm:c:src/intro.c:IntroFuzzProcessOam`, `mzm:c:src/intro.c:IntroFuzzVBlank`, `mzm:c:src/intro.c:IntroHandler`, `mzm:c:src/intro.c:IntroInit`, `mzm:c:src/intro.c:IntroMotherBrain`, `mzm:c:src/intro.c:IntroProcessText`, `mzm:c:src/intro.c:IntroSamusInHerShip`, `mzm:c:src/intro.c:IntroShipFlyingTowardsCamera`, `mzm:c:src/intro.c:IntroShipFlyingTowardsCameraProcessOam`, `mzm:c:src/intro.c:IntroTextProcessOam`, `mzm:c:src/intro.c:IntroVBlank`, `mzm:c:src/intro.c:IntroViewOfZebes`, `mzm:c:src/intro.c:IntroViewOfZebesProcessOam`.  
Local implementation: [`src/runtime/mzm_intro.c`](../src/runtime/mzm_intro.c), [`src/runtime/mzm_audio.c`](../src/runtime/mzm_audio.c), [`src/runtime/mzm_sfx.c`](../src/runtime/mzm_sfx.c), [`src/runtime/room_runtime.c`](../src/runtime/room_runtime.c).  
Tests: [`tests/test_mzm_screens.c`](../tests/test_mzm_screens.c).
Dependencies: `mzm.technical.non_gameplay_screens`, `mzm.technical.ppu_and_rom_bank`, `mzm.audio.sound_engine`, `mzm.title.title_screen`.  
Known divergences: English text only: the other languages' intro text (TextStartStory) is not ported. ProcessComplexOam and CalculateOamPart4 are local copies inside the intro module (the sprite engine does not use them yet). The V-blank writes are applied right after the frame's handler, one frame earlier than on hardware. Not compared with an emulator.  
Next action: Compare the timing and the affine ship with an emulator, then port the game start sequence and the other cutscenes.
Evidence: in game: `runtime capture`.

[+] **`mzm.title.title_screen` — Title screen.** The runtime boots on the intro (mzm.title.intro) without arguments or with `--intro`, and on the title screen with `--title` (Return or the gamepad's Start is START). It runs TitleScreenHandler frame by frame on the native screen's PPU: TitleScreenInit (sTitleScreenPal for BG and OBJ, the two pages of sTitleScreenPageData with their LZ77 tile tables, the title, space, decoration and sparkle graphics, the ground kept in EWRAM, the trademark symbol), the fade in from black through ApplySpecialBackgroundFadingColor, the title music (UpdateMusicPriority 2, PlayMusic MUSIC_TITLE_SCREEN), the comets view (two comets with the menu OAM animations of sTitleScreenOam, the ground scrolling in with TitleScreenTransferGroundGraphics), START skipping it (brightness down, the ground copied, brightness up), the title fading in by alpha blending, the animated palettes (sky decorations, title shine after 4 seconds, the PRESS START prompt), the two sparkles after 6 seconds, and START (the pressing sound, the fast prompt palette, the fade out). Checked by capture: the comets, the scrolled title and PRESS START, then the game after START. START now leads to the file select screen (mzm.menus.file_select), which leads back to the intro with B (GM_INTRO). After a skipped intro (gSubGameMode2 1) the title starts past its comets, as in TitleScreenInit.

Native evidence: `mzm:c:src/menus/title_screen.c:TitleScreenCallProcessOam`, `mzm:c:src/menus/title_screen.c:TitleScreenCheckPlayEffects`, `mzm:c:src/menus/title_screen.c:TitleScreenCometsView`, `mzm:c:src/menus/title_screen.c:TitleScreenFadingIn`, `mzm:c:src/menus/title_screen.c:TitleScreenFadingOut`, `mzm:c:src/menus/title_screen.c:TitleScreenHandler`, `mzm:c:src/menus/title_screen.c:TitleScreenIdle`, `mzm:c:src/menus/title_screen.c:TitleScreenInit`, `mzm:c:src/menus/title_screen.c:TitleScreenLoadPageData`, `mzm:c:src/menus/title_screen.c:TitleScreenProcessBottomSparkle`, `mzm:c:src/menus/title_screen.c:TitleScreenProcessComets`, `mzm:c:src/menus/title_screen.c:TitleScreenProcessOam`, `mzm:c:src/menus/title_screen.c:TitleScreenProcessTopSparkle`, `mzm:c:src/menus/title_screen.c:TitleScreenResetOam`, `mzm:c:src/menus/title_screen.c:TitleScreenSetBGCNTPageData`, `mzm:c:src/menus/title_screen.c:TitleScreenSetCopyrightSymbol`, `mzm:c:src/menus/title_screen.c:TitleScreenSetIdleStage`, `mzm:c:src/menus/title_screen.c:TitleScreenTransferGroundGraphics`, `mzm:c:src/menus/title_screen.c:TitleScreenUpdateAnimatedPalette`, `mzm:c:src/menus/title_screen.c:TitleScreenUpdateOamId`, `mzm:c:src/menus/title_screen.c:TitleScreenVBlank`, `mzm:c:src/menus/title_screen.c:unk_76710`, `mzm:c:src/menus/title_screen.c:unk_767a4`, `mzm:c:src/menus/title_screen.c:unk_76978`, `mzm:c:src/menus/title_screen.c:unk_76a98`.

The idle demos are not ported: when TitleScreenCheckPlayEffects starts one after 17 idle seconds, the title screen is entered again past its comets, as after a demo. The V-blank writes are applied right after the frame's handler, one frame earlier than on hardware. Not compared with an emulator.  
Next action: Port the demos, then compare with an emulator.

### Menus and options

[?] **`mzm.menus.inventory-boundary` — source coverage.** The current classifier assigns 240 discovered routine(s) to this category (240 by source path). Category completeness has not been established.

[+] **`mzm.menus.easy_sleep` — Pause easy sleep.** "L on the map screen plays the opening sound and runs PauseScreenEasySleepInit: the map fades out by four BLDALPHA levels a frame, the L prompt and the visor overlays change, the easy sleep tilemap replaces the map overlay and the equipment names graphics are backed up and replaced by the text, drawn a few steps a frame in the mask mode (TextDrawEasySleep with TextProcessCurrentMessage and TextDrawMessageCharacter: ""Activate Easy Sleep?"" and the SELECT, L and R hint, plus the YES/NO prompt drawn by TextDrawYesNoEasySleep at PauseScreenInit), then it fades in with the cursor on NO. LEFT and RIGHT move the cursor with their sound, A on NO refuses, B, L or R closes (PauseScreenQuitEasySleep restores the overlay and the graphics); A on YES waits half a second, blanks the screen and sleeps until SELECT, L and R are held, then waits for every key to be released with the cursor back on NO. Checked by capture in brinstar_000."

Native evidence: `mzm:c:src/menus/pause_screen.c:PauseScreenEasySleepInit`, `mzm:c:src/menus/pause_screen.c:PauseScreenQuitEasySleep`, `mzm:c:src/menus/pause_screen_sub_menus.c:PauseScreenEasySleepHandler`, `mzm:c:src/text.c:TextDrawEasySleep`, `mzm:c:src/text.c:TextDrawMessageCharacter`, `mzm:c:src/text.c:TextDrawYesNoEasySleep`.  
Local implementation: [`src/runtime/mzm_pause_screen.c`](../src/runtime/mzm_pause_screen.c), [`src/runtime/mzm_text.c`](../src/runtime/mzm_text.c), [`src/runtime/room_runtime.c`](../src/runtime/room_runtime.c).  
Tests: [`tests/test_mzm_screens.c`](../tests/test_mzm_screens.c).
Dependencies: `mzm.menus.pause_map_screen`, `mzm.menus.status_screen`.  
Known divergences: The Stop syscall (SYSCALL 3 with the keypad interrupt on SELECT, L and R) is reproduced as a sleeping state: the runtime stops presenting the screen (black, the LCD being off) and pauses the audio device instead of SoundBias0; the game keeps polling the keys each frame. gDisableSoftReset is not modeled (no soft reset exists). The English text is always used. Not compared with an emulator.  
Next action: Compare the screen and the timings with an emulator.
Evidence: in game: `brinstar_000`.

[+] **`mzm.menus.file_select` — File select screen.** "START on the title enters the US file select screen on the native screen PPU: the Chozo background, the menu and panel tilemaps from the ROM bank, the three files with their energy and tanks (HudDrawEnergy into the file's tiles), timer or dashes, difficulty and area, the Metroid logos of completed files, the music, and the fade in. UP and DOWN move between the files and the enabled entries (with the maintained input repeat) with the file markers and Samus's head cursor; B fades out to the intro (GM_INTRO). A on a file opens its ""Start game"" panel (the window, the alpha fade and the BG2 scroll), whose text is drawn line by line with the native font; A there continues a saved file (the start animation, then the save is loaded), or on a completed file offers continue / new game / time attack and the overwrite confirmation, and on a new file opens the difficulty panel (hard once unlocked, completed difficulties highlighted) before the start; B closes the panels back. Checked by capture: the files, the panels, a new game and a continued file. Copy (with at least one file) chooses a source among the used files (a time attack file is refused with its message) and a destination among the other files (the first empty one, else A/B), shown by the green markers and the arrow, confirms the overwrite of a used destination, copies the file (SramCopyFile) and redraws it; erase chooses a used file, confirms it (NO by default) and deletes it (SramDeleteFile) before redrawing; B leaves each step. The runtime copies and deletes the save files themselves." The Time Attack code is ported (FileSelectCheckInputtingTimeAttackCode: UP, LEFT, RIGHT, DOWN one key at a time in a completed file's Start game panel once gFileScreenOptionsUnlocked.timeAttack bit 0 is set, a wrong key restarting it after half a second): it opens Continue / New / New Time Attack on New Time Attack, and a new Time Attack game keeps SaveFile.timeAttack (save version 4) for gTimeAttackFlag. A bank-backed test enters the code.

Native evidence: `mzm:asm:src/menus/pause_screen.s:CheckForMaintainedInput`, `mzm:c:src/hud.c:HudDrawEnergy`, `mzm:c:src/menus/file_select.c:FileScreenProcessText`, `mzm:c:src/menus/file_select.c:FileScreenSetEnabledMenuFlags`, `mzm:c:src/menus/file_select.c:FileScreenUpdateMessageInfoIdQueue`, `mzm:c:src/menus/file_select.c:FileSelectApplyFading`, `mzm:c:src/menus/file_select.c:FileSelectApplyMenuSelectInput`, `mzm:c:src/menus/file_select.c:FileSelectCheckInputtingTimeAttackCode`, `mzm:c:src/menus/file_select.c:FileSelectDisplaySaveFileHealth`, `mzm:c:src/menus/file_select.c:FileSelectDisplaySaveFileInfo`, `mzm:c:src/menus/file_select.c:FileSelectDisplaySaveFileMiscInfo`, `mzm:c:src/menus/file_select.c:FileSelectDisplaySaveFileTimer`, `mzm:c:src/menus/file_select.c:FileSelectEraseFileHandler`, `mzm:c:src/menus/file_select.c:FileSelectFileCopyChooseBaseDestinationFile`, `mzm:c:src/menus/file_select.c:FileSelectFindFirstNonEmptyFile`, `mzm:c:src/menus/file_select.c:FileSelectInit`, `mzm:c:src/menus/file_select.c:FileSelectInitFading`, `mzm:c:src/menus/file_select.c:FileSelectMenuHandler`, `mzm:c:src/menus/file_select.c:FileSelectPlayMenuSound`, `mzm:c:src/menus/file_select.c:FileSelectProcessFileSelection`, `mzm:c:src/menus/file_select.c:FileSelectProcessOam`, `mzm:c:src/menus/file_select.c:FileSelectResetOam`, `mzm:c:src/menus/file_select.c:FileSelectUpdateCopyArrow`, `mzm:c:src/menus/file_select.c:FileSelectUpdateCopyCursor`, `mzm:c:src/menus/file_select.c:FileSelectUpdateCursor`, `mzm:c:src/menus/file_select.c:FileSelectUpdateEraseCursor`, `mzm:c:src/menus/file_select.c:FileSelectUpdateFading`, `mzm:c:src/menus/file_select.c:FileSelectUpdateSubMenu`, `mzm:c:src/menus/file_select.c:FileSelectVBlank`, `mzm:c:src/menus/file_select.c:unk_7e3fc`, `mzm:c:src/menus/file_select.c:unk_7eedc`, `mzm:c:src/save_file.c:SramCopyFile`, `mzm:c:src/save_file.c:SramDeleteFile`.

Tests: [`tests/test_mzm_screens.c`](../tests/test_mzm_screens.c).
Dependencies: `mzm.title.title_screen`, `mzm.technical.save_file`, `mzm.technical.non_gameplay_screens`.  
Known divergences: The corrupted file handling and the supported options (stereo, sound test, gallery, time attack records and Fusion link) are not ported: the options entry is shown but A on it does nothing. The bundled NES game is deliberately outside the project scope. The Japanese text menu is skipped as in the US version. A new game plays the gunship's landing in Crateria (mzm.environment.gunship) and the intro text cutscene (mzm.cutscenes.story_text), then starts in the runtime's first room. FileSelectCopyFileHandler is ported from its NON_MATCHING C version. The text buffers' fills follow the US binary, which adds their offsets in bytes where the decompiled source's u32 pointers would scale them (checked in the disassembly of mzm_us.elf). Not compared with an emulator.  
Next action: Port the supported options screen (stereo, sound test, gallery and time attack records), then compare the screen with an emulator.
Evidence: in game: `runtime capture`.

[+] **`mzm.menus.map_download` — Pause screen map download.** With PAUSE_SCREEN_MAP_DOWNLOAD, PauseScreenInit flags the screen as on the map and downloading, keeps the explored map on BG3, marks the area's map downloaded, recomputes gDecompressedMinimapVisitedTiles with the downloaded tiles (PauseScreenGetMinimapData, MinimapSetDownloadedTiles), uses the download BG layout (the visor on BG0 at the highest priority, BG3 with the mosaic bit, BG1 scrolled by 4 pixels), shows only the area name (priority 0), Samus's and the boss icons at priority 1 (the boss icon hidden until the line passes it) and no scroll arrows or viewable areas. PauseScreenMapDownload waits 8 frames, starts the line from the BG3 scroll, copies one map row every 8 frames for 21 rows while the line and its four trails move, transfers the whole map, redraws the minimap, removes the line and waits half a second; then A, B or START leaves. Checked by capture in brinstar_032.

Native evidence: `mzm:c:src/menus/pause_screen.c:PauseScreenCallStateHandler`, `mzm:c:src/menus/pause_screen.c:PauseScreenInit`, `mzm:c:src/menus/pause_screen.c:PauseScreenLoadAreaNamesAndIcons`, `mzm:c:src/menus/pause_screen.c:PauseScreenUpdateTopVisorOverlay`, `mzm:c:src/menus/pause_screen_map.c:PauseScreenInitMapDownload`, `mzm:c:src/menus/pause_screen_map.c:PauseScreenMapDownload`, `mzm:c:src/menus/pause_screen_map.c:PauseScreenMapDownloadHandler`, `mzm:c:src/minimap.c:MinimapDraw`.  
Local implementation: [`src/runtime/mzm_pause_screen.c`](../src/runtime/mzm_pause_screen.c), [`src/runtime/mzm_hud.c`](../src/runtime/mzm_hud.c), [`src/runtime/room_runtime.c`](../src/runtime/room_runtime.c).  
Tests: [`tests/test_mzm_screens.c`](../tests/test_mzm_screens.c).
Dependencies: `mzm.menus.pause_map_screen`, `mzm.interface.hud_and_minimap`.  
Known divergences: BG1 and BG2 show screens 24 and 23, which this mode never loads: the game's leftover VRAM there is not kept (the screen PPU is cleared). The game's fade into the pause is not played and the music is not lowered (as in the game, only START lowers it). The HUD minimap is redrawn and transferred over the next frames instead of the HUD graphics reload of the return to the game. The unused instant download types 1 and 2 are not ported. Not compared with an emulator.  
Next action: Compare the screen with an emulator, then port the Chozo hint screen.
Evidence: in game: `brinstar_032`.

[+] **`mzm.menus.pause_map_screen` — Pause screen map.** START in the game (when movement is not prevented and no power bomb is out) lowers the music and opens the pause screen on the native screen's PPU: PauseScreenInit loads its palettes (sTankIconsPal, sMinimapTilesPal, sPauseScreen_3fcef0), graphics and tile maps (the minimap tiles, the visor overlays, the map and world map overlays, the area and menu names) from the ROM bank, puts the current area's explored map on BG3 (gDecompressedMinimapVisitedTiles) and every area's map (PauseScreenGetMinimapData with the obtained items, boss icons and the skipped Varia tile, then MinimapSetDownloadedTiles) in EWRAM, centres the view on Samus (PauseScreenMapSetSpawnPosition), sets the BG controls and alpha blending, and fades in by two color steps a frame. The map screen scrolls with the D-pad (CheckForMaintainedInput repeats), shows the scroll arrows, Samus's icon, the boss icon or its cross, the area name, animates the minimap palette, toggles the world map with A or B (its overlay, palette, area names and Samus's icon, with their sounds), switches between the viewable areas with SELECT (MapScreenChangeMap with sMapScreenAreasViewOrder) and opens the status screen with R (mzm.menus.status_screen) or easy sleep with L (mzm.menus.easy_sleep) and leaves with START or B: the fade out, then ResetMusicVolume and the game resumes. Checked by capture in brinstar_000 (pause, scroll, resume). The map stations open it in its map download mode (mzm.menus.map_download). Chozo statue hints (PAUSE_SCREEN_CHOZO_HINT): the hint screen (sChozoHintBackgroundGfx and its tile table, the area name, BG1's tint blended over the map), the statue's target found by its map position (sChozoStatueTargets), its icon travelling to the item (ChozoStatueHintMovement: the speed by distance, the trail every 8 frames) while the map scrolls to it (ChozoStatueHintScrolling), through the elevators to another area (sChozoStatueTargetPathPointers, sElevatorRoomPairs: the map fades out and in on the next area), then A or START fades the music (2 2/3 seconds) and leaves. The pause screen's map then shows the activated targets (the items still to collect after their hint, Kraid's and Ridley's flames), the elevators towards the first one and the world map's targets; the areas with a target become viewable. Checked by capture: the Long Beam hint in brinstar_002 (the statue's grab, the screen, the target and the scrolling, A, the statue sitting down) and the target on the pause map afterwards. The test runs that hint on the exported bank. ProcessPauseButtonPress: START (and a map station's or a hint statue's request) first fades the game to black in 8 frames (COLOR_FADING_CANCEL, every BG and OBJ row) while the sprites go on and Samus waits; the pause screen then opens, and on leaving it the game fades back in over 8 frames, Samus still waiting, before the pause's music volume is reset (UpdateMusicAfterPause). Checked by capture in brinstar_000.

Known divergences: The pause from the game, the map download and the Chozo statue hints are ported: the item acquisition and suit screens, the debug menu and the completion info and its tank counts (shown only after the game is completed) are not. The hint screen skips StatusScreenDraw and TextDrawYesNoEasySleep (their EWRAM tilemaps are never shown there); the boss flames' up and down movement (PauseScreenUpdateBossFlameAnimation) is never started by the game and is not ported. The game's fades around the pause (COLOR_FADING_CANCEL) darken Samus's and the HUD's frames by colour modulation; the room is not reloaded after the pause (the game keeps its state). Not compared with an emulator.  
Next action: Port the item acquisition screens, then compare with an emulator.
Evidence: in game: `brinstar_000`, `brinstar_002`.

[+] **`mzm.menus.status_screen` — Pause status screen.** R on the map screen runs PauseScreenStatusScreenInit: the R prompt and its visor overlay, the opening sound, the map fades out through BLDALPHA (PauseScreenInitFading / PauseScreenApplyFading, two levels a frame), the status BG2 with its blend, the headers and Samus's wireframe (sSamusWireframeData, sSamusWireframePal by suit, an OBJ window copy showing only BG3 and the objects) slide in, fade in, become their linked versions, the equipment rows are revealed one a frame (StatusScreenDrawItems) and the cursor and text BG0 appear. PauseScreenInit draws the status tilemap from the runtime equipment (obtained and activated items, the plasma beam, gravity suit and space jump as unknown items before the fully powered suit, the bombs and missiles with their ammunition, the pistol when suitless) and the energy and ammunition amounts. The D-pad moves the item cursor between the enabled slots (StatusScreenGetDestinationSlot with the remembered left and right slots), each slot writes its description with the native font into the text tiles (TextProcessCurrentMessage, TextDrawCharacter with the ROM character widths and graphics), A skips the delay and turns the lines, and B, L or R goes back to the map (PauseScreenQuitStatusScreen). Checked by capture in brinstar_000 (long and charge beams, missiles, bombs, misc items). The fully powered suit's items open the status screen on its own (BG2, OBJ and the OBJ window; BG2 blended at 22/16 on BG2, BG3, OBJ and the backdrop) after UpdateSuitType(SUIT_FULLY_POWERED) refills Samus and deactivates every item: Plasma Beam, Gravity Suit and Space Jump stay unknown rows and the missile rows inactive; PauseScreenSuitChangingStart shows the upgrade headers, reveals the rows and puts the cursor on gCurrentItemBeingAcquired's slot; the suitless wireframe fades to the full suit (PauseScreenFadeWireframeSamus) after 50 frames; the normal items are enabled one by one with the cursor's destroy animation and SOUND_ENABLING_NORMAL_ITEM; each unknown item gets its overlay object (MISC_OAM_ID_*_UNKNOWN on OBJ row 12), two partial and one full white flash, SOUND_ENABLING_UNKNOWN_ITEM, its name row written, the cursor, SOUND_TOGGLING_ITEM_ON, the known overlay, the flash fading back, then its description with the names' idle palette until A (the Gravity Suit activates and fades the wireframe to its palette); every item is then activated and the screen leaves after 12 frames. A unit test runs it on the ROM bank; captures in chozodia_042 (`--ruins-test-end` with test items) show the Plasma Beam and the Space Jump unknown, then revealed and described ("Somersault continually"). The item acquisition (PAUSE_SCREEN_ITEM_ACQUISITION, after a new item's or first tank's banner) and suitless (PAUSE_SCREEN_SUITLESS_ITEMS, after the post-Tourian monologue, with UpdateSuitType(SUIT_SUITLESS)) sequences open the status screen on its own: StatusScreenGetSlotForNewItem deactivates the new item, PauseScreenSuitChangingStart puts the cursor on it, StatusScreenSuitlessItems toggles it back on after 16 then 8 frames (SOUND_TOGGLING_ITEM_ON, SOUND_UNKNOWN_ITEM_ACQUISITION for the unknown items; a suit fades the wireframe; the item's missile or bomb row lights up), then describes it and leaves on any key. As natively, StatusScreenToggleItem's 0xFF result stored in an s32 is never below 0, so the toggle stage always runs. Unit tests run the Varia Suit, a first missile tank and the suitless sequence on the ROM bank; captures in brinstar_027 (the Varia Suit taken from its statue: the banner, the status screen with "Decreases damage taken from enemies.", then the in-game suit flash) and after the Tourian escape (the suitless status screen with the pistol described, then chozodia_000 with the Power Grip, Long and Charge Beams active).

### Controls and input

[?] **`mzm.input.inventory-boundary` — source coverage.** The current classifier assigns 9 discovered routine(s) to this category (9 by source path). Category completeness has not been established.

[+] **`mzm.input.gamepad` — Keyboard and gamepad input.** UpdateInput's exact held, previous and rising-edge semantics are shared by gameplay and tested without a device. Keyboard and hot-plugged SDL3 gamepads feed the same ten GBA bits; the default layout, remapping file, analog dead zone, impossible opposite-direction cancellation and debug chord are covered. The intro, title, file select, gameplay and pause/status screens all consume this path, and recorded gameplay captures were driven through it. An in-game controls menu is an optional host feature, not a missing native routine.

Native evidence: `mzm:c:src/update_input.c:UpdateInput`.  
Local implementation: [`src/runtime/gba_input.c`](../src/runtime/gba_input.c), [`src/runtime/room_runtime.c`](../src/runtime/room_runtime.c), [`src/runtime/mzm_screen.c`](../src/runtime/mzm_screen.c).  
Tests: [`tests/test_gba_input.c`](../tests/test_gba_input.c), [`tests/test_mzm_screens.c`](../tests/test_mzm_screens.c).
Known divergences: SDL3 supplies an active-high host mask instead of reading the GBA's active-low KEYINPUT register. Screen-specific menu repeat remains in each native screen handler, as in the game.  
Next action: Add a graphical host control-remapping menu if desired.
Evidence: in game: `brinstar_000`.

### HUD and interface

[?] **`mzm.interface.inventory-boundary` — source coverage.** The current classifier assigns 41 discovered routine(s) to this category (41 by source path). Category completeness has not been established.

[+] **`mzm.interface.area_banner` — Area and room name banners.** SpriteUtilInitLocationText: when a room is entered through a door flagged DOOR_TYPE_DISPLAYS_ROOM_LOCATION (exported as Y lines) the area's location text (LocationTextGet*: BRINSTAR, KRAID, NORFAIR, RIDLEY, TOURIAN, CRATERIA, CHOZODIA, MOTHERSHIP, PLANET ZEBES, CHOZO RUINS, the map rooms...), and in a save or map room spriteset always its name, put an area banner in sprite slot 0 at an absolute screen position: an area's name scrolls in at the bottom for 2 seconds (sAreaBannerLocationTextPal), a room's name in the middle for 1.5 seconds (5/6 of a second for the save and map rooms, none of those while the alarm rings), then scrolls out. TextDrawLocation's text (mask mode over 0xFF, its two tile rows copied to the graphics slot) is drawn by the exporter with the native font. Checked by capture: BRINSTAR at a new game's start and SAVE ROOM in brinstar_033. The tests run both banners and the alarm case.

The banner is drawn after the front backgrounds (BG priority 0) at its GBA screen position, the GBA screen centred in the wider view. Not compared with an emulator.  
Next action: Port the falling Chozo pillar with the Ruins Test, then compare with an emulator.

[+] **`mzm.interface.hud_and_minimap` — HUD and minimap.** HudDraw runs after ProjectileUpdate every frame and writes the native OBJ VRAM and OAM of a GbaPpu: sCommonSpritesGfx at VRAM_OBJ + 0x800 and sCommonSpritesPal at OBJ palette rows 2 to 7 (the in-game init), the energy digits and the energy tank row (sEnergyTanksGfx_* by full tanks, the low health flag under 30), the three refill animation stages of the energy, missile, super missile and power bomb counters (gEnergyRefillAnimation and the others, set by the Chozo statue refills), the missile, super missile and power bomb counters composed from 4-pixel digits (sMissileDigitsGfx, sPowerBombDigitsGfx) with their Inactive, Selected or Active symbol (HudUpdateGfx follows gSamusWeaponInfo.weaponHighlighted and missilesSelected), the suitless charge bar, the native escape countdown objects, and the OAM entries of HudUpdateOam (health bar, counters only when their maximum is non-zero, the minimap and its square blinking every 8 frames). The minimap decompresses the area's sMinimapDataPointers map (LZ77), adds the obtained items, hides the unexplored tiles unless the map is downloaded (MinimapSetDownloadedTiles), follows Samus's screen (MinimapCheckForUnexploredTile with the room entry's mapX and mapY read from sAreaRoomEntryPointers), marks it explored, records the area names, and redraws one 3-tile line a frame through the sPauseScreen_40d74c / _40d6fc color tables with the four flips. Checked in brinstar_000 (`--spawn 212 496`): energy 99, missiles, super missiles and power bombs with their symbols, the minimap with the Morph Ball room tile and the blinking square.

Known divergences: Not compared with hardware or an emulator. The runtime view is 320x224, wider than the GBA screen: the HUD keeps its native positions from the view's top-left corner and the minimap is placed 26 pixels from the view's right edge (SCREEN_SIZE_X replaced by the view width). Only the HUD is drawn by the PPU (its OAM slots; the other sprites keep the runtime's own drawing), so the HUD is always in front. Without a save system the minimap progress (gVisitedMinimapTiles, gMinimapTilesWithObtainedItems, downloaded maps) lasts one session; the Hiragana tiles, the debug room info and the boss icon updates by Kraid, Ridley and the ship (MinimapUpdateChunk has no caller yet) are not wired.  
Next action: Port the pause screen map and status screen on the same PPU, then save the minimap progress.
Evidence: in game: `brinstar_000`.

[+] **`mzm.interface.message_banner` — Message banner and its text.** The exporter transliterates TextProcessMessageBanner, TextProcessCurrentMessage and TextDrawMessageCharacter on the banner's OBJ VRAM (sMessageBannerGfx, color 15 fill, glyphs of sCharactersGfx with sCharacterWidths, eight characters a frame, the US line overflow) for every English message of sMessageTextPointers, renders the banner's six OAM tables with each message, and records the TextProcessMessageBanner calls each message takes. The runtime runs MessageBanner frame by frame: the nine graphics frames, the text processing with the animation held, the shown banner (items and first tanks back up player 2 and insert the item or unknown item jingle; the fully powered suit plays the Brinstar remix behind its jingle; tank messages back up player 2, play the tank jingle and retrieve player 2 at removal; maps and save complete play the tank jingle; one-line tables for items, first tanks, maps, save complete and the fully powered suit), 100 static frames before A, B or a direction closes it, the removal animation, gPreventMovementTimer held at SAMUS_ITEM_PMT meanwhile (SamusUpdate skipped, sprites stopped except uninitialized ones, no Samus contact); its removal finishes a tank collection, and the end of an item message's text (stage 3 of the text processing) runs BgClipFinishCollectingAbility, which registers the ability in gItemsCollected and on the minimap. Once removed, the banner keeps its last pose, as its sprite slot does natively, for the map station that waits for MESSAGE_BANNER_POSE_REMOVAL_ANIMATION. The save prompt spawns its YES/NO cursor (SaveYesNoCursor, drawn at its absolute position shifted like the banner): LEFT and RIGHT move it with their sound, A answers (work1), NO plays the refusal and closes the banner, YES hides the cursor and saves on its next update (MZM_BANNER_SAVE); the save complete message re-enables the pause when it goes. The Zebes and Chozodia escape messages emit EscapeStart when their spawning animation reaches its static table. The fully powered suit message's removal starts the suit animation stage and spawns the falling Chozo pillar (MZM_BANNER_START_SUIT_ANIM). A new item's or first tank's banner now opens the item acquisition status screen once removed (gPauseScreenFlag = PAUSE_SCREEN_ITEM_ACQUISITION, MZM_BANNER_ITEM_ACQUISITION), and player 2 is retrieved at the end of the game's fade back in (UpdateMusicAfterPause). Checked in brinstar_027: the Varia Suit banner, then its status screen.

### Player state machine

[?] **`mzm.player.inventory-boundary` — source coverage.** The current classifier assigns 126 discovered routine(s) to this category (126 by source path). Category completeness has not been established.

[+] **`mzm.player.samus_controller` — Samus pose controller, physics and collisions.** A transliteration of src/samus.c: the 67 poses of sSamusCollisionData with their hitboxes and standing statuses, every handler of sSamusPoseFunctionPointers and sSamusPoseGfxFunctionPointers, SamusSetPose with SamusSetMidAir, SamusSetLandingPose, the hurt and knockback poses and SamusCheckCarryFromCopy, SamusUpdatePhysics, the velocity update with DIV_SHIFT and the slope speeds, and the native point-probe collisions of SamusUpdateHitboxMovingDirection (the side, top, landing and ground probes, the corner nudges, slopes, the walk-off-ledge rule, the Power Grip ledge grab with the non power grip blocks). SamusAimCannon gives the running diagonals (Up or Down with the direction) and the no-aim run (ACD_NONE); the hanging poses turn to aim on Up, Down, L or the outward direction, climb after ten frames of Up without A, hide the arm cannon, pull into a morph ball tunnel under a block and shoot up or down. The animations time with sSamusAnimPointers_PowerSuit[pose] (or the suitless and the special tables) and the runtime draws the body animation of SamusUpdateGraphicsOam by its library key, whose arm cannon offsets place the shots (the Running [ACD_NONE - 2] rows are now exported); the shots take the offsets of the unarmed key, since the armed graphics variants have none and placed missiles at Samus's feet. The screw and speed booster block damage runs at the start of SamusCheckCollisions. Checked in brinstar_009 (--spawn 466 192: hanging, the Up+toward climb without A, a shot up from the hanging cannon) and brinstar_005 (a diagonal shot while running left). The hazards of ClipdataUpdateCurrentAffecting (the water, lava and acid blocks, and the room effect's hazard above or below its surface from the exported sHazeData) drive the liquid slowing of SamusUpdatePhysics (halved speeds and gravity, no Gravity Suit) and SamusTakeHazardDamage (the per-suit damage rates, the submerged sound, the cold knockback, the death). Checked in crateria_007: Samus runs at the slowed cap in the water. Elevator blocks work in the rooms of sElevatorRoomPairs (ClipdataCheckCantUseElevator, from the ROM bank; the second Crateria to Tourian route only after EVENT_ESCAPED_ZEBES): Up or Down on one turns Samus to the foreground, rides at the elevator speed with its sound, takes the area connection through BgClipCheckTouchingTransitionOnElevator and RoomLoad moves her three blocks on in the new room until the opposite elevator block stops her. Checked by capture: brinstar_026 to norfair_000. SamusDying's timer reaching 120 (after the animation's frame 23) flags the game over (gMainGameMode = GM_GAMEOVER). The shinespark and ballspark collisions start the shared screen shake (mzm_screen_shake), whose horizontal timer still drops Samus from a ledge.

`mzm:c:src/clipdata.c:ClipdataUpdateCurrentAffecting`, `mzm:c:src/samus.c:SamusAimCannon`, `mzm:c:src/samus.c:SamusAimingWhileHanging`, `mzm:c:src/samus.c:SamusApplyXAcceleration`, [+] **`mzm.player.speed_booster` — Speed booster, shinespark and ballspark.** With the Speed Booster, running above 96 subpixels a frame counts a timer to 160 (the cap rises to 140 at 120 and 160 at 140); from 160 subpixels a frame SamusCheckShinesparking starts the speed boost (sound, Running_Speedboosting, the speed boost palette rows by gFrameCounter8Bit % 6, the 160 ground and mid-air caps kept through jumps and the speed-boosted landing), cancelled below it and on death. Releasing the direction skids (10 subpixels a frame, the skidding sound); Down alone while skidding, or during the six frames after a speed-boosted roll in the Morph Ball, stores a shinespark for 180 frames (the palette and the charge sound every 16 frames). Speed boosting breaks speed blocks (DESTRUCTING_ACTION_SPEED and SPEED_ON_GROUND) and kills weak sprites as DCT_SPEEDBOOSTER with its particle and sound. Checked in tourian_010 with --item speed-booster: the speed boost palette and running animation after about 160 frames of running. With a stored shinespark, A (without a direction on the ground, with half a block of room, Samus rising by it) or A in the air starts the charge (50 frames of glow, the shinesparking sound, no gravity); when its animation ends the spark goes up by default at 192, sideways at 192 with the direction held, or diagonally (160, 96) with L or the direction and Up, through SamusSetPose with the speed boost on, killing weak sprites as DCT_SHINESPARK (its particle and sound). A ceiling or a wall ends it in the collision (48 frames of invincibility, the collision sound), 17 frames, then the delay after and a fall. In the Morph Ball with High Jump, A starts a ballspark: half a block up, a hop on the first frame, one second of charge with the ballsparking sound and the ball turning toward the input, then the same sparks, the collision and the Morph Ball fall. SamusSetPose stops the sounds when a charge or spark is cut short. Checked in tourian_010: run, skid, Down, A: the spark rises to the ceiling, then the delay after.

### Animations

[?] **`mzm.animation.inventory-boundary` — source coverage.** The current classifier assigns 0 discovered routine(s) to this category. Category completeness has not been established.

[+] **`mzm.animation.particles` — Sprite particles and debris.** One-shot particles for the nine small, big and huge water, lava and acid splashes, the bomb explosion, sprite explosions, the screw attack kill and projectile impacts on walls and sprites, and the medium dust effects, with slot reuse, animation, priority and the on-screen window; sprite debris with its eight arcs, bounces, animation and draw range. The super missile impact starts a sixth-of-a-second screen shake both ways. PE_SECOND_MEDIUM_DUST and PE_SECOND_TWO_MEDIUM_DUST reuse the dust tables (their low BG priority is not drawn). ParticleSamusReflection persistently mirrors Samus's live body frame at the Ruins Test owner, follows its position, hides while a shootable symbol is active and dies with the fourth symbol; a chozodia_042 capture shows its mirrored running pose. The small dusts (ParticleSmallDust, never spawned by the game, and its second variant) are ported with sParticleSmallDustOam, and the second dusts now take PARTICLE_STATUS_LOW_BG_PRIORITY as natively. SpriteDebrisSetSplash is ported: a falling or bouncing piece entering the room's liquid leaves a small splash at its position. The remaining SpriteUtilCheckInRoomEffect / CheckOutOfRoomEffect splashes are ported: the Dragon rising and sinking (small), the Geruta diving (big), the Squeept leaving (small) and entering (big) its lava, the Parasites falling (small) and the Acid Worm (huge; only its body plays SOUND_ACID_WORM_SPLASHING, its parts no longer do). Unit tests cover the debris splash, the Dragon's splash and the dusts' priorities.

The shooting flashes and the charging beam are drawn from dedicated runtime state rather than particle slots, so they never take or evict a slot. The splashes are placed at the liquid's surface rather than at the crossing sprite's position (at most one frame's movement apart). Not compared with an emulator.  
Next action: Compare the effects with an emulator.
Evidence: in game: `chozodia_042`.

[+] **`mzm.animation.samus_runtime_library` — Samus animation and palette reconstruction.** The hash-gated private library exports all 832 native body/cannon variants: 1,072 sequences, 4,670 frames and every one of the 67 real SPOSE rows for the Power, Full and Suitless families where they exist. Its semantic map resolves 662 gameplay bindings with no missing binding; the 18 suitless speedboost, skid, screw attack and shinespark combinations are explicitly unavailable because those abilities cannot coexist with the suitless campaign state. All palette rows and their native effects are exported and selected by SamusUpdatePalette. SamusUpdateGraphicsOam selects the per-ACD tables and falls back to the exact pose row; SPOSE_SAVING_LOADING_GAME with lastWallTouchedMidAir selects the three native InShip tables. The native C11 importer writes the library; C unit tests audit the decompilation's pose enum against the generated pointer tables and the body/cannon selection rules, and recorded digests guard every library file, palettes included; gameplay captures cover suited and suitless movement, weapons, palette effects and the Ruins Test reflection.

Native evidence: `mzm:c:src/samus.c:SamusUpdateAnimation`, `mzm:c:src/samus.c:SamusUpdateGraphicsOam`, `mzm:c:src/samus.c:SamusUpdatePalette`.  
Local implementation: [`src/assets/mzm/samus.c`](../src/assets/mzm/samus.c), [`src/runtime/mzm_samus.c`](../src/runtime/mzm_samus.c), [`src/runtime/room_runtime.c`](../src/runtime/room_runtime.c), [`src/assets/mzm/samus_tables.c`](../src/assets/mzm/samus_tables.c).  
Tests: [`tests/test_fusion_mzm_samus_assets.c`](../tests/test_fusion_mzm_samus_assets.c), [`tests/test_native_golden.c`](../tests/test_native_golden.c), [`tests/test_mzm_samus.c`](../tests/test_mzm_samus.c).

[+] **`mzm.collision.clipdata` — Samus and room Clipdata collision.** Every routine of clipdata.c is ported: ClipdataConvertToCollision (the solidity of a subpixel inside a block, slopes included), ClipdataProcessForSamus and ClipdataProcess, ClipdataCheckCurrentAffectingAtPosition (a block's movement and hazard), ClipdataUpdateCurrentAffecting (the hazard blocks, else the room effect's hazard above or below gEffectYPosition), ClipdataCheckCantUseElevator (sElevatorRoomPairs, the second Crateria to Tourian route only after the escape) and ClipdataCheckGroundEffect (sGroundEffectsClipdataValues). Checked in game by every room capture's collisions, by the steep slopes, and by the hazards: norfair_022 (death in the lava) and crateria_007 (water over Samus).

### Weapons and projectiles

[?] **`mzm.weapons.inventory-boundary` — source coverage.** The current classifier assigns 56 discovered routine(s) to this category (56 by source path). Category completeness has not been established.

[+] **`mzm.weapons.beams_and_missiles` — Beam and missile projectiles.** Power Beam, missiles and super missiles move and collide with the room; their hits on ported sprites (damage, stun, invincible hits) and their wall and sprite impact particles are ported. Missiles and super missiles drop their trails (PE_MISSILE_TRAIL every 8 frames, PE_SUPER_MISSILE_TRAIL every 4). ProjectileCheckDespawn removes beams and missiles that are both off screen (the screen plus the projectile's draw distance and two blocks of padding) and 12 blocks above or below or 10 blocks beside Samus. A missile or super missile not weak to the sprite it hits (or hitting an immune sprite) tumbles off it (ProjectileStartTumblingMissile: away from the sprite's x, its thrust sound stopped, no longer hitting sprites, drawn in front of the backgrounds) along sTumblingMissileSpeed with a pixel a frame sideways and through walls, until it leaves the screen (ProjectileMoveTumbling). Checked in brinstar_003: a missile shot diagonally at a brown Ripper bounces back and falls tumbling. (The charge and Long Beams: mzm.weapons.charge_beam; Ice: mzm.weapons.ice_beam; Wave and Plasma: mzm.weapons.wave_plasma_beams; bombs and power bombs: mzm.weapons.bombs, mzm.weapons.power_bombs). The weapon sounds play at their native points: the toggle and arming sounds of SamusSetHighlightedWeapon, the beam shot and the missile and super missile shot and thrust on ProjectileInit, the thrust stopped by the impact particles.

Native evidence: `mzm:c:src/particle.c:ParticleMissileTrail`, `mzm:c:src/particle.c:ParticleSuperMissileTrail`, `mzm:c:src/projectile_util.c:ProjectileCheckDespawn`, `mzm:c:src/projectile_util.c:ProjectileMissileHitSprite`, `mzm:c:src/projectile_util.c:ProjectileMoveTumbling`, `mzm:c:src/projectile_util.c:ProjectileStartTumblingMissile`, `mzm:c:src/projectile_util.c:ProjectileSuperMissileHitSprite`, `mzm:c:src/projectile_util.c:ProjectileUpdate`, `mzm:c:src/projectile_util.c:ProjectileUpdateAnimation`.  
Local implementation: [`src/runtime/mzm_projectiles.c`](../src/runtime/mzm_projectiles.c), [`src/runtime/mzm_sprites.c`](../src/runtime/mzm_sprites.c), [`src/runtime/room_runtime.c`](../src/runtime/room_runtime.c), [`src/assets/mzm/samus.c`](../src/assets/mzm/samus.c), [`src/runtime/mzm_sfx.c`](../src/runtime/mzm_sfx.c).  
Tests: [`tests/test_mzm_samus.c`](../tests/test_mzm_samus.c), [`tests/test_mzm_sprites.c`](../tests/test_mzm_sprites.c), [`tests/test_fusion_mzm_samus_assets.c`](../tests/test_fusion_mzm_samus_assets.c).
Known divergences: The on-screen test of ProjectileCheckDespawn uses the runtime camera's top-left corner with the GBA screen size (the runtime view is wider); the missile trails' low BG priority is not drawn; projectiles without PROJ_STATUS_ABOVE_BG1 are drawn at the sprites' priority instead of one behind BG1.  
Next action: Add the missile and beam item pickups with the item sprites.
Evidence: in game: `brinstar_003`.

[+] **`mzm.weapons.bombs` — Bombs, bomb jumps and bomb damage.** With the Bombs item, B in the Morph Ball places a bomb (four at most, 7 frames apart) at Samus's position: a slow then a fast spin of 16 frames each with their native sprites, then the PE_BOMB explosion and 16 frames during which CAA_BOMB_PISTOL reaches nine points, one a frame (bomb blocks, bomb chains, hatches). On the first explosion frame Samus bounces off a touching bomb (up, left or right from the bomb's middle and gPreviousXPosition; only falling and from below when airborne), and the explosion damages sprites weak to beams and bombs (8). The set and explosion sounds play. Checked in brinstar_000: the bounce, then the three bomb blocks under Samus break. A bomb taken by a morph ball launcher spins again (16 + 16 frames), then its explosion affects no block but launches Samus in morph ball touching it (forcedMovement FORCED_MOVEMENT_LAUNCHED_BY_CANNON, SPOSE_DELAY_BEFORE_BALLSPARKING). The runtime runs ProjectileCheckHittingSprite where ProjectileUpdate does, after the new projectile's spawn and before the projectile loop (MzmWeapons.hit_sprites), so the explosion's PROJ_STATUS_CAN_AFFECT_ENVIRONMENT, set when the fast spin ends, is taken by the next update's pass once.

Native evidence: `mzm:c:src/particle.c:ParticleBomb`, `mzm:c:src/projectile.c:ProjectileCheckSamusBombBounce`, `mzm:c:src/projectile.c:ProjectileMorphballLauncherCheckLaunchSamus`, `mzm:c:src/projectile.c:ProjectileProcessBomb`, `mzm:c:src/projectile_util.c:ProjectileBombHitSprite`, `mzm:c:src/samus.c:SamusBombBounce`.

[+] **`mzm.weapons.charge_beam` — Charge Beam, Long Beam and the pseudo screw.** With the Charge Beam, holding B with no weapon armed counts the charge to 79, then back to 64; releasing it fires a charged beam from 64 or a normal beam above 25; the B press itself still fires a beam, arming a weapon drops the charge and the Morph Ball releases a full charge as it forms or rolls. The charging particle starts at 16 with the begin-to-charge sound (stopped when the charge drops), turns charged at 64 with the full-charge loop every 16 frames, follows the arm cannon and hides in the spin poses; Samus uses the charging palette rows (two consecutive rows by 16-frame step, none on the fourth) and the beam release rows. The Long Beam moves 24 subpixels a frame without the short lifetime, with its hit damage (3) and impact; the charged beams (two at a time, hitboxes 12 and 16, damage 8 and 12) hit sprites weak to the charge beam or beams. Beam shots set the ProjectileSetBeamParticleEffect flash at the cannon. A full charge during a spin is the pseudo screw: 4 * (2 + beams) to weak sprites, PSEUDO_SCREW_DESTROYED on a kill, Samus still hurt by survivors. The beam particles are exported per loaded beam variant (graphics set and sBeamPal row) in the projectile library. Checked in tourian_010: the charge glow and palette, then the charged Long Beam and its flash. The charged Long Beam drops PE_CHARGED_LONG_BEAM_TRAIL every 8 frames.

Native evidence: `mzm:c:src/particle.c:ParticleChargedLongBeamTrail`, `mzm:c:src/particle.c:ParticleChargingBeam`, `mzm:c:src/particle.c:ParticleHittingSomethingWithLongBeam`, `mzm:c:src/particle.c:ParticlePlayBeamFullChargedSound`, `mzm:c:src/particle.c:ParticlePlayBeginToChargeSound`, `mzm:c:src/particle.c:ParticlePseudoScrewDestroyed`, `mzm:c:src/particle.c:ParticleShootingBeamLeft`, `mzm:c:src/particle.c:ParticleShootingBeamRight`, `mzm:c:src/particle.c:ParticleStopBeginToChargeSound`, `mzm:c:src/projectile.c:ProjectileProcessChargedLongBeam`, `mzm:c:src/projectile.c:ProjectileProcessChargedNormalBeam`, `mzm:c:src/projectile.c:ProjectileProcessLongBeam`, `mzm:c:src/projectile_util.c:ProjectileNonIceChargedHitSprite`, `mzm:c:src/projectile_util.c:ProjectileSetBeamParticleEffect`, `mzm:c:src/samus.c:SamusCheckFireBeamMissile`, `mzm:c:src/samus.c:SamusCheckNewProjectile`.

[+] **`mzm.weapons.ice_beam` — Ice Beam and freezing.** The Ice Beam (over the Long Beam) moves 26 subpixels a frame and drops a trailing particle half a block from it when gFrameCounter8Bit & 3 is 0 (drifting one subpixel sideways and one pixel down a frame); its shot, charge and impact sounds, beam graphics, sBeamPal row 2, release row 2 and charging rows 4 and 5 are used. Hits: a sprite weak to beams freezes for 240 when freezable and frame8 & its freeze flag (metadata high byte) is 0, taking 3 (4 with Long Beam) as ice damage; otherwise normal damage; freezable but immune sprites freeze with no damage; others take the solid hit. The charged Ice Beam (12, 16 with Long Beam) needs a charge-beam or beam weakness and always freezes freezable sprites. Freezing sets the frozen row (15 - graphics slot), plays SOUND_FREEZING_SPRITE and the freezing particle (drawn every other frame); every ported AI then only counts the timer down every other frame and flickers in its last 45 ticks. The stun timer now flashes the stun row (14 - slot). The exporter bakes /frozen and /stun variants of every sprite frame. Pseudo screw damage counts the Ice Beam. Checked in brinstar_003 (`--item ice-beam`, L held, B taps): a brown Ripper freezes blue under the ice impact and freezing particles, trails follow the shots and Samus flashes the ice release row. Frozen sprites are solid: a falling Samus lands on them (SpriteUtilSamusStandingOnSprite, STANDING_ENEMY), rising into one stops her under it and walking into one stops her at its side; its kill drops her. Checked in ridley_003 (`--item ice-beam --item charge-beam`): a charged shot freezes a Dessgeega, Samus jumps onto it and stands there until it thaws, then takes its contact damage. The charged Ice Beam also drops PE_CHARGED_ICE_BEAM_TRAIL every 8 frames. A frozen sprite touching Samus on a zipline drops her (SpriteUtilCheckOnZipline: SPOSE_MID_AIR_REQUEST). The paralyzer pistol applies the native suitless Space Pirate freeze countdown and black/purple flicker.

Native evidence: `mzm:c:src/particle.c:ParticleBeamTrailingLeft`, `mzm:c:src/particle.c:ParticleBeamTrailingRight`, `mzm:c:src/particle.c:ParticleChargedIceBeamTrail`, `mzm:c:src/particle.c:ParticleFreezingSpriteWithChargedIce`, `mzm:c:src/particle.c:ParticleFreezingSpriteWithIce`, `mzm:c:src/particle.c:ParticleHittingSomethingWithIceBeam`, `mzm:c:src/projectile.c:ProjectileProcessChargedIceBeam`, `mzm:c:src/projectile.c:ProjectileProcessIceBeam`, `mzm:c:src/projectile_util.c:ProjectileChargedIceBeamHittingSprite`, `mzm:c:src/projectile_util.c:ProjectileFreezeSprite`, `mzm:c:src/projectile_util.c:ProjectileIceBeamDealDamage`, `mzm:c:src/projectile_util.c:ProjectileIceBeamHittingSprite`, `mzm:c:src/projectile_util.c:ProjectileSetTrail`, `mzm:c:src/samus.c:SamusUpdatePalette`, `mzm:c:src/sprite_util.c:SpriteUtilSamusAndSpriteCollision`, `mzm:c:src/sprite_util.c:SpriteUtilSamusStandingOnSprite`, `mzm:c:src/sprite_util.c:SpriteUtilUpdateFreezeTimer`, `mzm:c:src/sprite_util.c:SpriteUtilUpdateStunTimer`.

[+] **`mzm.weapons.paralyzer_pistol` — Suitless paralyzer pistol.** Suitless Samus automatically charges the pistol every frame: B fires the harmless normal shot before 112 frames or the charged shot from 112 through the 127-to-112 wrap, with the native two/six projectile limits, seven-frame cooldown and shot sounds. Both shots use CAA_BOMB_PISTOL, travel 22 subpixels per moving frame without the short-beam lifetime and use their native directional OAM and hitboxes; the charged shot drops PE_CHARGED_PISTOL_TRAIL every eight frames. The normal shot always produces an invincible hit. The charged shot deals 12 to sprites weak to charge shots or beams, but a Space Pirate is instead paralysed for 60 ticks with the suitless black/purple flicker and no damage. The projectile exporter stages the pistol graphics and palette for its muzzle flash and charged trail. Checked in chozodia_000 after the Tourian escape and the suitless status screen: facing left, a tap of B fires the uncharged shot; 130 frames later (the counter charges by itself, without B held) a second tap fires the charged shot, whose PE_CHARGED_PISTOL_TRAIL particles follow it left at 66 pixels per 12 frames while the counter restarts from 0. The capture diagnostics now list the charge counter and the projectiles.

Native evidence: `mzm:c:src/particle.c:ParticleChargedPistolTrail`, `mzm:c:src/projectile.c:ProjectileProcessChargedPistol`, `mzm:c:src/projectile.c:ProjectileProcessPistol`, `mzm:c:src/projectile_util.c:ProjectileCheckHittingSprite`, `mzm:c:src/samus.c:SamusCheckFirePistol`, `mzm:c:src/samus.c:SamusCheckNewProjectile`.

[+] **`mzm.weapons.power_bombs` — Power bombs and their explosion.** In the Morph Ball, R arms power bombs (with ammunition); B (the Bombs item is required, as natively) places one, at most one at a time and none during an explosion: one ammunition (the last disarms them), a slow spin of 54 frames and a fast one of 40, then PowerBombExplosionStart. The semi-minor axis grows by 3 a frame to 159, holds five frames, shrinks back to 4 and ends; while expanding, CAA_POWER_BOMB reaches the hitbox's left and right block columns (even frames) or top and bottom rows (odd frames) from the centre outward, opening power bomb hatches and breaking blocks; every sprite touching the hitbox takes 50 (weak) once per power bomb. The set and explosion sounds play; power bomb drops spawn and refill one. The window rows come from sHaze_PowerBomb_WindowValuesPointers (power_bomb.tsv). Checked in brinstar_000: the explosion and the bomb blocks it breaks. The explosion starts a two-second horizontal screen shake (one way only, intensity 0x80).

Next action: Draw the explosion through the native palette and window effects.
Evidence: in game: `brinstar_000`.

[+] **`mzm.weapons.wave_plasma_beams` — Wave and Plasma Beams.** SamusCheckNewProjectile picks plasma, then wave, ice, long for the beam type and the matching shot sound (all 24 normal and charged combinations); the charge sounds and the release (rows 3 and 4) and charging rows (6 and 8) follow ParticlePlayBeginToChargeSound and SamusUpdatePalette. The Wave Beam moves 28 subpixels a frame through walls, applying CAA_BEAM to three points across the wave (one block away, 45 subpixels diagonally) every frame; the Plasma Beam moves 32 and stops at walls (its impact) unless the Wave Beam is equipped, and goes through the sprites it hits. Both drop the ice trail with the Ice Beam; the charged ones drop their trails (wave, plasma, or the full beam trail with Plasma and Wave) every 8 frames. Their hitboxes are the native per-direction boxes (wave, charged wave and their Plasma variants, with the float factors truncated). Hits deal 3 plus one per other beam (charged 12 plus four) with the Wave or Plasma impact, or are ice hits with the full beam particles when the Ice Beam is equipped. The projectile library exports the Wave and Plasma tables, their ice-coloured versions, the new impact and trail particles for each loaded beam variant (the Plasma trails only with the Plasma graphics). Checked in brinstar_003 (`--item wave-beam --item long-beam`): a wave shot crosses the right wall and stays drawn in front of it until it leaves the screen; with `--item plasma-beam` the green shot goes through a Ripper.

[+] **`mzm.enemies.acid_worm` — Acid Worm encounter.** The native kraid_005 placement waits for the activated zipline and Samus riding into range, then opens its central column with the three native particles and eight upper debris pieces. The head and all nine body actors consume the native sine, swinging and horizontal-wiggle tables around the moving pivot with their individual phase and radius. Health selects the exact hooked-lunge speed, crash debris and hold time; touching Samus aborts the extension, while the distant branch follows the native spitting animation and all three ROM velocity tables. The wall impact copies BG1's live priority, placing the explosion above BG1 but below any priority-0 background; an acid impact retains the normal sprite priority. Below half health on normal or hard, alternate retractions raise and lower the acid through the native delayed cycle. The weak point transfers damage and palette stages to the 600-health main actor. Its lethal hit flashes the curved body, explodes each segment on its room-slot delay, opens the floor and door from the tail, fades the music, descends the acid with dust and screen shakes, then plays the boss-killed track; event 27 persists the opened arena.

[+] **`mzm.enemies.atomic` — Atomics.** Atomics (none on easy) hover around their spawn with sAtomicIdleYMovement / sAtomicIdleXMovement, flee Samus within five blocks (slowing every 16 frames, then back to the spawn by half pixels), charge every 3.33 seconds with the sAtomicDynamicPaletteData flashing (rows 0, 2 and 3, the silent charge sound at the intense part) and spawn their electricity, which follows them until the charge ends, then is shot horizontally, vertically or diagonally at Samus (SSC_HURTS_SAMUS_A_LOT: five times its damage and a stronger knockback) and explodes on the first block; while Samus charges a beam (chargeCounter 64 or more) they chase her arm cannon (AtomicSmoothMovement on last frame's gArmCannonY / gArmCannonX). Contact damage 30, health 40, weak to missiles and the screw attack. Checked by capture in chozodia_058 (the flashing Atomic and its electricity; Samus at 99 energy dies to them, as their damage says).

[+] **`mzm.enemies.baristute` — Baristutes and Kraid's locked Baristute pair.** Baristutes idle, face Samus and, with room in front, jump along the ROM-exported arc or walk six (or twelve) blocks; walls turn them around and ledges make them fall along their falling table, landing with a short screen shake. Their health quarters set damage rows 1 to 3 (baked palette variants) and the faster walking animations and speeds. Death leaves two extra energy or missile drops. Kraid's upper Baristute always walks and the lower one always jumps; together they lock the room until both are dead, then set event 52 and unlock the doors. A capture in kraid_007 shows the pair walking and idling.

[+] **`mzm.enemies.boss_statue` — Kraid and Ridley boss statues.** Both statues load from brinstar_010 with native graphics, placements, offsets and proximity priority. Kraid records the viewed-room event for the Long Beam Dessgeega. After their matching boss events, the eyes activate, doors lock and camera centring starts Kraid's opening plus Ridley's one-second delayed opening. Both sequences reproduce their temporary six-block wall, interior and final-floor Clipdata edits, sounds, shaking and debris. Kraid also moves every live projectile to the native low-OAM pass, keeping ordinary shots behind the other OBJ actors while above-BG1 shots retain their background band. Opened events persist both final collision states; Kraid waits for Ridley before unlocking the shared route, and the camera lock releases after both have opened. A real capture completed both sequences and events while Samus fired through the room.

[+] **`mzm.enemies.chozodia_scripted` — Falling Chozo pillar and the Power Bomb theft.** The falling pillar (behind BG1) appears once the suit animation ends and is on screen, then sinks 256 subpixels with a vertical shake and dust every 16 frames, a final one-second shake and its landing sound. The pirate carrying the Power Bomb shows once in each of its two rooms (events 71 and 72), walks right with its footsteps and vanishes at the next pirate zoneline. The Power Bomb on its pedestal and its invisible trigger disappear once Samus touches the trigger (event 70). A capture in chozodia_032 shows the Power Bomb on its statue. The fully powered suit banner's removal sets RUINS_TEST_FIGHT_STAGE_STARTING_SUIT_ANIM and spawns the pillar (gfx slot 7) four blocks above and twelve right of the Ruins Test's centre; a capture in chozodia_042 (`--ruins-test-end` staging) shows it appearing once the suit upgrade ends and, with Samus walking right, sinking 64 pixels into the shaft behind BG1.

[+] **`mzm.enemies.chozodia_security` — Chozodia searchlights, security lasers, searchlight eyes and security gates.** Searchlights sweep diagonally half a pixel a frame between stop-enemy blocks, flickering, and ring the alarm on contact (SSC_CHECK_COLLIDING), then hold it; security lasers size themselves 2 to 5 blocks to the first block above or right, and when tripped mark the Space Pirates, fade out and hold the alarm; searchlight eyes (immune to projectiles until the Fully Powered Suit, SP_IMMUNE_TO_PROJECTILES with the invincible impact) move along their wall, cast a seven-block beam that rings the alarm unless a wall shields Samus, then shoot projectiles (16 damage) every 1.5 seconds. Checked in chozodia_003 (a tripped laser releases the waiting pirates), chozodia_016 and chozodia_030. Security gates (open by default, or closed by default for the event spritesets): four blocks tall, immune to projectiles, they close with their sound when the alarm rings (gAlarmTimer), become CAA_MAKE_NON_POWER_GRIP solid only once Samus no longer touches them (SpriteCheckCollidingWithSamus), and open again with CAA_REMOVE_SOLID two thirds of a second after the alarm stops; the default closed gates do the opposite. Checked by capture in chozodia_005 (Samus spotted, both gates close; the one around her stays passable).

[+] **`mzm.enemies.crocomire` — Unused Crocomire and Gekitai Machine prototypes.** Crocomire loads its native graphics, all 25 OAM tables and all six composition tables, spawns its five secondary body actors, loops the native screaming composition and accepts the externally requested idle composition. Its body actors retain the original hitboxes, draw order, immunity and dying-state propagation. Gekitai Machine performs its complete native one-line AI and removes itself immediately. Together with the directly tested Red Garuta path, every named retail primary monster id now has a runtime route.

[+] **`mzm.enemies.deorem` — Deorem two-location encounter.** Both native placements reproduce the complete head, body, eye and retreat choreography. The encounter emits its exact fixed and RNG-selected debris groups while entering, waiting, emerging and leaving, with the native dust, sound and screen-shake timing. Deorem waits for every active primary item drop before its retreat countdown resumes. The eye tracks Samus through the native eight angular sectors, alternates RNG-timed idle and pulsing animations, transfers missile hit stun and the low-health palette to the head, then spins with the dying body, recentres, rises, flashes and spawns the Charge Beam on its exact 120-frame timer. A capture in brinstar_012 (with missiles, Samus under its centre) shows Deorem spawning, its head emerging between its spiked segments, then retracting.

[+] **`mzm.enemies.dessgeega` — Dessgeegas and the Long Beam Dessgeega.** Ground and ceiling Dessgeegas share the Sidehopper state machine with their own tables and sizes; every ground Dessgeega death sets the Long Beam event and unlocks the doors (the game's bug, kept). The Long Beam variant checks its events, locks the doors, waits hidden from Samus and projectiles, drops through the floor while clearing both the graphics and collision of three blocks with dust and debris, and lands on the next one. The door locks and unlocks go through the runtime's hatch locks (mzm.transitions.doors_and_hatches). The Long Beam Dessgeega's drop and landing start a vertical screen shake, with its spawning and floor-breaking sounds. Room export retains its first and second event sprite placements and spriteset membership, and RoomLoad selects second, first or default from the save events. Entering brinstar_010 now runs KraidStatueInit and sets EVENT_VIEWED_STATUE_ROOM; the return through brinstar_009 loads DESSGEEGA_AFTER_LONG_BEAM while the untouched default room stays empty. A real brinstar_009 capture confirmed the three-block opening and the landed actor.

[+] **`mzm.enemies.dragon` — Dragons.** Faces Samus, rises from its lava half a pixel a frame up to two blocks while she is above it within 6 blocks (sinking back otherwise), turns toward her, and every 100 frames, when the block two above and one ahead is clear, warns for half a second and spits a fireball (its sound) that arcs with sDragonFireballYMovement at 3 pixels a frame, rotated with sDragonFireballOamRotation, until the effect surface or a block (its exploding table); the big explosion death. Checked in norfair_022 (--spawn 260 300): the Dragon rises and spits an arcing fireball.

[+] **`mzm.enemies.elevator_statue` — Kraid and Ridley elevator statues.** Each statue stands by its elevator as solid, non-grippable blocks (BG priority 1, always active, solid for projectiles). Once its boss is dead and Samus is off the elevator, it waits a quarter second, sets its event (31 or 38), removes its blocks with a horizontal shake and crumbling sound, drops the boss head debris, shrinks its draw height, lands with dust and a vertical shake, and leaves two rubble blocks; on reload it is already rubble. A capture in brinstar_008 shows the Kraid statue standing with Samus on its column. A capture in brinstar_008 with Kraid's event set shows the statue crumbling (poses 0x9, 0xB, 0xF) with its debris and falling into rubble. A capture after patch 0396 shows the Kraid head debris falling and turning to dust; before it the debris fell back to the Skree graphics owner and was not drawn.

[+] **`mzm.enemies.escape_ship` — Chozodia escape ship and escape gates.** Once Mecha Ridley is dead and Samus is up to 12 blocks above and less than 4 blocks left of it, the ship opens (sound) and spawns its pirate; from the 7th opening frame Samus can stand on it, and landing on it sets EVENT_ESCAPED_CHOZODIA, clears her invincibility and sets SPOSE_IN_ESCAPE_SHIP (or the turning pose when she faces left). From then on it holds Samus in the cockpit (43 subpixels up, 80 right), keeps every sprite from touching her, bursts the pirate lasers it touches and draws the pirates behind it. It closes after 20 frames, waits, spawns its flames and nudges right, sways on sEscapeShipHoveringY/XMovement while lifting off, signals the second gate, opens its shutters, and accelerates right for 200 frames, destroying the pirates in its way. Its top part goes in front of Samus once she is in, its tail flashes sEscapeShipFlashingPal rows (baked /f0-/f2, twice as fast once hovering) and its flames hide once it escapes. The first gate (solid grippable blocks) loads sEscapeGateAndTimerGfx and spawns the two black pirates, opens once they are dead and Samus is within 6 blocks to its left (blocks removed on the 5th opening frame), then spawns the pirate that chases the ship; it flashes sEscapeGateFlashingPal (baked from the gate tiles at OBJ VRAM 0x7800 and the last OBJ row). The second gate opens once the ship hovers. A capture in chozodia_092 (event 74) shows the first gate, its two black pirates and the ship. A capture sequence in chozodia_092 (event 74, energy 1299) shows Samus jumping into the open ship, the blue-ship cutscene, then the ship lifting off with the cockpit closed. The capture sequence was repeated with the native fades: the screen drops from an average brightness of 168 to 0 in one frame (1112) and the Chozodia escape starts two frames later. The shared VRAM was checked against the ROM: SpriteLoadAllData's EscapeCheckReloadGraphics copies sEscapeTimerDigitsGfx to OBJ VRAM 0x7800 before the room's sprites, then the first gate copies sEscapeGateAndTimerGfx over it; the eleven digit tiles the timer draws (0x3C0-0x3CA, both rows) are byte-identical in the two graphics, and the timer uses OBJ palette row 3, not the gates' row 15, so drawing them apart gives the same picture.

Native evidence: `mzm:c:src/sprite_util.c:SpriteUtilCountPrimarySprites`, `mzm:c:src/sprites_ai/escape_gate.c:EscapeGate`, `mzm:c:src/sprites_ai/escape_gate.c:EscapeGateChangeCcaa`, `mzm:c:src/sprites_ai/escape_ship.c:EscapeShip`, `mzm:c:src/sprites_ai/escape_ship.c:EscapeShipCheckCollidingWithLaser`, `mzm:c:src/sprites_ai/escape_ship.c:EscapeShipPart`, `mzm:c:src/sprites_ai/escape_ship.c:EscapeShipPartUpdatePalette`, `mzm:c:src/sprites_ai/escape_ship.c:EscapeShipPirateCollision`, `mzm:c:src/sprites_ai/escape_ship.c:EscapeShipSetIgnoreSamus`, `mzm:c:src/sprites_ai/escape_ship.c:EscapeShipSetPirateDrawOrder`.  
Local implementation: [`src/runtime/mzm_escape_ship.c`](../src/runtime/mzm_escape_ship.c), [`src/runtime/mzm_sprites.c`](../src/runtime/mzm_sprites.c), [`src/runtime/room_runtime.c`](../src/runtime/room_runtime.c), [`src/assets/mzm/sprites.c`](../src/assets/mzm/sprites.c), [`src/runtime/mzm_door_fade.c`](../src/runtime/mzm_door_fade.c).  
Tests: [`tests/test_mzm_sprites.c`](../tests/test_mzm_sprites.c).
Dependencies: `mzm.enemies.space_pirate`, `mzm.enemies.mecha_ridley`, `mzm.cutscenes.samus_in_blue_ship`, `mzm.cutscenes.chozodia_escape`.  
Known divergences: EFFECT_CUTSCENE_SAMUS_IN_BLUE_SHIP fades out with COLOR_FADING_SAMUS_IN_BLUE_SHIP before its cutscene (mzm.cutscenes.samus_in_blue_ship); EFFECT_CUTSCENE_ESCAPING_CHOZODIA once the ship is gone cuts the screen to black (COLOR_FADING_CHOZODIA_ESCAPE has no colour fade), runs the ending save and starts the Chozodia escape (mzm.cutscenes.chozodia_escape), then the credits. The escape timer and the gates share OBJ VRAM 0x7800 natively, while the runtime keeps the timer's digits (sEscapeTimerDigitsGfx) and the gates' tiles (sEscapeGateAndTimerGfx) apart; this changes nothing on screen, see the notes. Not compared with an emulator.  
Next action: Compare the escape with an emulator.
Evidence: in game: `chozodia_092`.

[+] **`mzm.enemies.gadora` — Kraid and Ridley Gadora hatch encounters.** Both native variants suppress their matching closed hatch, periodically detect Samus in front, warn and expose an otherwise projectile-immune eye. Destroying the eye runs the flashing death sequence, restores the hatch, persists event 28 or 36 and creates the native primary plus follower drop pair. The Ridley variant keeps its alternate palette, longer opening and delayed horizontal beam that explodes against room collision. A capture in kraid_029 shows Gadora's eye closed on its hatch, then opening once Samus comes within seven blocks in front of it.

[+] **`mzm.enemies.geruta` — Gerutas.** Red and green Gerutas: init 3 pixels up facing Samus, waiting under a ceiling until Samus is low (72 subpixels below) and within 5 blocks on both axes, attacking at once without a ceiling, the warning and launching tables with their sounds, the dive at 3 pixels a frame drifting a pixel toward Samus (the going-down flag is SAMUS_COLLIDING), the floor bounce, the climb, the settle 52 subpixels under the ceiling and the death into drops. [+] **`mzm.enemies.hive` — Brinstar hives, roots, Mellows and Mellow swarms.** Each hive spawns its roots and four Mellows, replaces any lost Mellow, shrinks under half and a quarter of its health (the roots disappear with the first stage), and on death sets event 44 once no hive is left while its idle Mellows flee and despawn off screen. Mellows hover on the ROM-exported idle tables, swap in front of and behind the hive, detect Samus within five blocks and circle her around four waypoints with the native acceleration, pushing overlapping Mellows apart. Mellow swarms keep five (or 3 to 15 by energy) Mellows diving from above the camera. The hives are solid and hurt on contact (SSC_HURTS_SAMUS_SOLID), the Mellows hurt like ordinary enemies. A capture in brinstar_015 shows the hives on their pillars with their Mellows hovering and chasing Samus. (Before patch 0396 its secondary sprites fell back to the Skree graphics owner and were not drawn.)

[+] **`mzm.enemies.imago` — Imago (adult wasp boss).** Imago waits in the ceiling until the last of its five eggs is broken (touched or shot), fades the music and starts the battle music; its body turns vulnerable. It comes down, sweeps the room low (faster as it is hurt, hovering over the floor and its slopes), climbs back at either end and dives at Samus near that end with the ROM-exported sway, turns otherwise. Under two thirds (or when Samus has no missiles) it shoots needles at her, faster under a third, and its body reddens (rows 4 and 5) while its core flashes through sImagoDynamicPaletteData. Killed, it drops its burning stinger, keeps flying while burning, waits for Samus at the Super Missile, charges and breaks the wall column by column, explodes with the boss-killed music, and sets event 35 and unlocks the doors once Samus picks the Super Missiles up. Contact uses the big-knockback hurt (SSC_HURTS_BIG_KNOCKBACK, now handled). Captures in ridley_019 show the glowing eggs (before patch 0396 the eggs, parts, needles and stinger fell back to the Skree graphics owner and were not drawn), then, once Samus breaks the last egg, Imago descending with its wings and stinger and striking her.

[+] **`mzm.enemies.imago_cocoon` — Imago cocoon encounter, passage and remains.** The active norfair_043 cocoon uses its two native multi-sprite tables, locks the doors, changes palette after three of its six 50-health missile-vulnerable vines are destroyed and alternates eight-spore nests on both sides. Its Winged Ripper follows the native ROM sine table, carries a standing Samus across both axes and is crushed by the falling cocoon. Breaking the last vine persists event 33, releases ceiling debris, removes the five central floor blocks in stages, crashes the cocoon into the lower floor, clears the two passage blocks after 90 on-screen frames, unlocks the doors and plays the boss-killed music. The native idle, vine, spore, destruction and battle-music cues are connected. The invisible passage trigger persists event 32. Reloading the killed cocoon restores its in-ground state until the Ridley entrance demo; ridley_028 retains the post-fight remnant and broken actors.

[+] **`mzm.enemies.imago_larva` — Imago larva encounter and passage remnant.** The paired larvae in norfair_042 keep independent multi-sprite state. The left larva locks the room and starts the battle music; the right larva waits at the cocoon, warns when Samus approaches, retracts by one, four or eight subpixels after shell damage, waits longer after each retreat and lunges back to its spawn. Their shell, claws and three dots follow the six native multi-sprite tables; warning hides the dots and temporarily makes the shell immune to another retreat. Body damage triggers the 47-frame counterattack, death synchronizes all parts and their native drops, then event 31 persists the cleared passage, unlocks the doors and starts the boss-killed music. The separate norfair_046 right-side remnant stays non-colliding and projectile-immune while its native 34-frame loop plays the shaking and six heartbeat cues.

[+] **`mzm.enemies.kraid` — Kraid.** Kraid locks the room, rises with its BG2 body (gBg2Movement, the room's BG_PROP_MOVING BG2) for two seconds with ceiling debris and shakes, then steps left and right with the multi-sprite tables (sKraidMultiSpriteData_*, exported from the decompilation with their part offsets), breaking the floor blocks in its way and stomping with dust; the camera follows gLockScreen five blocks left of Samus. Its head opens on its own or for three seconds after a hit on the shut mouth; missiles, super missiles and charged beams flying left into the open mouth hurt it, other shots vanish and missiles bounce off. Under 3/4, 1/3 and 1/4 of its health its sprite palette row and BG palette row 10 (sKraidPal rows 5 to 7) change, it swipes at Samus near on its right, steps faster, and its spikes shoot from the bottom, middle and (at low health) top holes, break the platforms on their way, carry Samus and stick in the wall six seconds; its left arm throws two nails toward Samus when fewer than three drops (one on easy) exist. Its body hurts and stops Samus (SSC_HURTS_KNOCKBACK_IF_INVINCIBLE), its spikes hurt from the right and carry her on top (SSC_HURTS_RIGHT_CAN_STAND_ON_TOP); the right arm and foot are drawn behind BG2 (BGCNT_LOW_PRIORITY). Its death sets EVENT_KRAID_KILLED, sinks it with explosions and dust, keeps Samus out of it, then PE_MAIN_BOSS_DEATH, the boss killed music after a fade (FadeCurrentMusicAndQueueNextMusic), BG2 hidden and every part exploding with its drops. Checked by capture in kraid_030 from the right platforms: missiles fired standing hit the left arm and bounce off, jumping ones reach the mouth (a super missile took it from 300 to 200 health), and the body and the sprites change colour together (the runtime picks the /rowN looks of Kraid's health rows). The blocks its spikes and feet break vanish from BG1 as well (CAA_REMOVE_SOLID through the block engine): the middle spike reaching the wall clears the platform above it, and Samus falls through the gap, as in the game.
