[+] **`mzm.enemies.mecha_ridley` — Mecha Ridley.** The native chozodia_072 placement creates Mecha Ridley's core and ten secondary body actors, with their native room slots, health, hitboxes, collision types, draw order and shared graphics owner. Eighteen ROM-exported multi-sprite tables keep all eleven layers aligned through the entrance, the three stances, their six transitions, the backward crawl and the high fireball mouth. The arena locks, the boss crawls in, starts the battle music, then tracks Samus's height with the native two-second hysteresis and changes stance. Every 64 frames it swipes its claw (lunging a pixel per swing frame, then waiting forward with the native health-scaled delay before raising the arm), or picks the laser, or the fireball choreography once the cover is damaged; damage while curled up makes it retract and crawl back. The arms keep their own animations after the entrance with the native per-frame hitboxes, swipe sounds and ground shake; the neck has its per-animation hitboxes and sounds; the eye blinks; the missile launcher opens only under 150 energy or without missiles and runs its open/fire/close timing. The eye laser (glow, one of four random directions, the actor flying 20 subpixels a frame until a wall), the three launcher missiles (rising and slowing, then homing on Samus with the native acceleration and turning PI / 64 a frame toward her) and the fireballs (high and low trajectories, wall and contact explosions, live counters) are ported. The exporter bakes the seven weapon graphics stages that MechaRidleyLoadMissilesGfx and LoadFireballsGfx copy to OBJ VRAM, and the renderer draws the stage last copied. The parts test the projectiles themselves (BlackSpacePirateProjectileCollision): beams other than the Plasma Beam are absorbed, immune parts make missiles tumble, and the cover, weak only to missiles, takes the damage; below three quarters it enables the fireballs, at zero it explodes and exposes the core with its own health, whose quarters set the threshold and the parts' damage tints. The death runs the boss explosion, the music fade, the timed explosions, the collapse, ten grouped large energy drops, the eye's last looks, then sets event 74, shows the Chozodia escape banner (which starts the escape), unlocks the doors and plays the escape music. Event 74 removes the encounter on reload. Private captures show the high-stance claw swipe hitting Samus at frame 1000 and the arm being raised in the forward position at frame 1250, and the user and a frame-1850 capture saw the three missiles homing on Samus with their animated exhaust. A scripted super-missile fight damaged the cover in game and triggered the high-stance fireball attack (frame 2400); the core, death and escape are only covered by the regression, as the scripted Samus died before winning. Its frames are exported as 8-bit OBJ palette indices with the room's palette (objpal), so the renderer rebuilds its live palette from the DMAs the AI records: the entrance fade through the seven sMechaRidleyFadingPal rows (with SOUND_2AD and BG2/BG3 dimmed from 10/16 back to 16/16 as TransparencySpriteUpdateBldalpha does), the head's green glow cycling sMechaRidleyGreenGlowPal rows 0-3-0 in its current palette row, the destroyed graphics and colors of the death, the eye's dimming during the drops and its green then red blinking. At 100% completion (SpriteUtilGetFinalCompletionPercentage, from the difficulty's tank amounts and the abilities) the core and cover have triple health and the contact doubles its damage. The missiles and fireballs start at Q8.8 scale 1 and, as single-OBJ frames with exported OAM bounds, rotate through the native affine window. A regression drives each palette DMA from its native pose and timing and the 100% rule; a capture in chozodia_072 shows the dark, faded body crawling in and its full colors once the battle starts. Its gDisableAnimatedPalette writes reach the room's alarm palette: off after its cycle at its death, on again when the battle starts and when the escape starts.

[+] **`mzm.enemies.mella` — Mellas.** Hovering under a ceiling with the ROM idle tables from an RNG offset, detection when Samus is low and within 8 blocks vertically and 3 across, a 7-frame delay, the swoop with the ROM going-down, going-up and horizontal speed tables (the going-down flag is SAMUS_COLLIDING, as in the game), the climb back half a block under the ceiling and the small explosion death. A capture in norfair_001 shows the Mellas leaving their ceiling and swarming toward Samus.

[+] **`mzm.enemies.metroid` — Metroids.** Metroids lock their room (rooms 14, 15, 19, 1, 2, 16 of Tourian, one event each) and are removed once its event is set; once on screen they wobble along sMetroidSpawningY/XMovement and, after a random delay, grow from a quarter of their size (palette rows 3, 2, 1, baked as /rowN), then chase Samus's head with a speed that builds up to its cap and a momentum that runs down every other frame, reversing on walls and pushing overlapping Metroids (MetroidCheckBouncingOnMetroid). Samus under a Metroid's middle is grabbed (SSC_METROID): it drains SpriteUtilTakeDamageFromSprite's damage with an invincibility of 1, 4 or 8 frames by suits, rides her head, cycles sMetroidPal_SamusGrabbed in OBJ row 12 (baked as /gN), plays the sucking sounds by suits and slows her (gEquipment.grabbedByMetroid, outside liquids as in the unfixed game) until an exploding bomb ejects it. Frozen, it becomes PSPRITE_FROZEN_METROID (missiles hurt it) with a bigger hitbox and thaws twice as fast except on easy; dead, it and its shell explode (PE_FREEZING_SPRITE_WITH_CHARGED_ICE) and the last one sets the room's event and unlocks the doors. Checked by capture in tourian_014 (the growth, the chase and the grab).

[+] **`mzm.enemies.mother_brain` — Mother Brain.** Mother Brain (gone once EVENT_MOTHER_BRAIN_KILLED or EVENT_ESCAPED_ZEBES is set) waits in its glass, the bottom part's 400 health: missiles break it in three stages drawn on BG0 (BgClipCallMotherBrainUpdateGlass through a new block hook) and shatter it with the glass breaking sprite; the block Samus can't grab appears once she passes its place. The eye then opens with Mother Brain's 700 health: missiles hurt it, and 100 damage (200 on easy, 60 on hard) close it; with the eye closed and Samus above the blocks it charges (sMotherBrainDynamicPaletteData's palette rows, baked as /rowN) and fires its beam (SSC_HURTS_SAMUS_IGNORE_INVINCIBILITY), the eye opening for the shot; Samus below the blocks closes it. The camera locks on the arena and Samus is kept out of it. Its death sets EVENT_MOTHER_BRAIN_KILLED (which also removes the cannons and its Rinkas), flashes it for two seconds, explodes it (PE_MAIN_BOSS_DEATH) and a second later shows the escape banner, starts the Zebes destruction actor and escape countdown, plays the escape music and its beep. Checked by capture in tourian_004 (Mother Brain in its glass, missiles from a platform cracking it: 400 to 260 health, the second stage drawn) and by a test of the whole fight. Its two-second death now requests the native quick palette flash every 64 frames and once more with the final explosion; the shared background-effect processor runs its white, blue and falling-white stages without recoloring OBJ palettes.

[+] **`mzm.enemies.parasite` — Parasites (single and multiple).** Single Parasites crawl (faster for 1.5 seconds once Samus came within range, gParasiteRelated), hop or jump on their frame-counter timer with their three jumping sounds on screen, turn at walls, fall and land, and die crushed under a ceiling. Touching Samus (SSC_PARASITE, a point hitbox) they grab her and crawl over her from her top left corner; with more than three on her and neither the Varia nor the Gravity Suit, the leech takes the forced 1 energy (no knockback, the hurt pose only when it kills), 16 invincible frames, the leeching sound and every fourth time the hurt sound. The screw attack throws them off; any hit (a beam kills them at health 1) throws them away instead, and only an exploding bomb touching them or a power bomb kills them (ParasiteBombCollision). They grab Gerons the same way and are thrown off by their destruction. The multiple Parasites (the bug room) work on ClipdataProcess, the main one spawning five more (SpriteSpawnPrimary), lock the doors when Samus has the Bombs, and the last one killed sets EVENT_BUGS_KILLED and unlocks them. gParasiteRelated runs down at the end of every sprite update, the frozen one included, and is cleared by the room load. Checked by capture in brinstar_016 (four on Samus drain her), brinstar_023 (Gerons destroyed) and brinstar_024 (24 multiple Parasites).

[+] **`mzm.enemies.pipe_bugs` — Zebs, Geegas, Zebbos and Gamets (pipe bugs).** Zebs, blue Zebs, Geegas, white Geegas Geega leaders with their follower, green and yellow Zebbos and Zebbo leaders with their two followers, blue and red Gamets and blue Gamet leaders with their two followers (Gamet hitbox and sounds, followers like the Zebbo leader's): hidden in their pipe, they rise toward Samus when she is above, beside and within five blocks, become harmful after two frames, stop near her height, fly at 12 subpixels a frame through walls, return to their pipe 16 blocks past her and wait a second; deaths leave a drop following the pipe (DEATH_RESPAWNING) that the pipe waits for, and a leader waits for its follower. [+] **`mzm.enemies.polyp` — Polyps.** Polyps knock Samus back on contact without damage (SSC_KNOCKS_BACK_SAMUS: SPOSE_KNOCKBACK_REQUEST, a pixel of x velocity away) and, every two seconds while on screen without a projectile or a drop of their own, warn (with its sound), spit and retract; the projectile goes towards Samus within 5 by 8.6 blocks (else a random side), arcs with sPolypProjectileYVelocity a pixel a frame sideways (SSC_HURTS_SAMUS_STOP_DIES_WHEN_HIT once it moves), dies off screen and explodes on a wall or the floor. Checked by capture in norfair_011 (both Polyps' projectiles arcing towards Samus).

[+] **`mzm.enemies.ridley` — Ridley.** Ridley needs EVENT_GRAVITY_SUIT_OBTAINED (and is gone with its ground blocks once EVENT_RIDLEY_KILLED is set); it locks the room and waits in the ceiling until Samus stands on the ground facing left near the platform's left or the door, then drops, breaks the ground blocks, spits ascending fireballs for five seconds (turning toward Samus), and takes off. Idle, it floats (sRidleyIdleYOffsets), slides along the ground toward Samus and turns at walls; it attacks with small fireballs on the ground, big fireballs in a fan, and its tail (gSubSpriteData2 with the eight tail sprites: diagonal and vertical swipes that shake the ground); 200 damage taken or its death forces the big fireballs then the tail. Its claw grabs Samus from above (SSC_RIDLEY_CLAW: RidleyCheckGrabbing), carries and lifts her in front of its mouth, drains her energy by the difficulty and Varia Suit, spits fireballs at her (SSC_HURTS_SAMUS_STOP_DIES_WHEN_HIT_NO_KNOCKBACK) until a timer or her mashing frees her, a wall dropping her. Its palette rows change at a half and a quarter of its health, the camera locks on Samus near the ground or held, and its death flickers it in explosions and PE_MAIN_BOSS_DEATH, fades the music, then sets EVENT_RIDLEY_KILLED, unlocks the doors, plays the boss killed music and explodes the tail. Checked by capture in ridley_012 (the drop, the fireballs, the grab lifting Samus and draining her, her release) and by a test (the Gravity Suit condition, the parts, the trigger, the landing, the 200 damage threshold and the death's event).

[+] **`mzm.enemies.rinka` — Rinkas.** Orange and green Rinkas: born at their spawn block (the first spawn visible at once), respawns hidden, shown once on screen and vulnerable 11 frames later, a straight flight toward where Samus was along a unit vector computed with the BIOS integer square root, respawn 16 blocks away from her, and respawning deaths that leave a drop only when none of theirs exists. Mother Brain's six Rinka ids (Tourian) use sRinkaZebetiteAndCannonGfx (exported with the cannon's tables), a draw order of 3 as every Rinka, and frozenPaletteRowOffset 1 (their frozen look is the row the others stun with); hidden off screen they respawn, at a second spawn point 20 to 40 blocks left (a block lower or higher for two of them) when Samus is far enough left of the first, flying from the point in use, and they explode and vanish once Mother Brain is dead (EVENT_MOTHER_BRAIN_KILLED). Checked by capture in tourian_004 (the rings flying at Samus). ProjectileCheckHittingSprite visits the sprites by draw order (1 to 16), then slot, from the list of hittable sprites taken before the pass.

[+] **`mzm.enemies.ruins_test` — Ruins Test (Chozo ghost).** The main actor freezes Samus facing the background five blocks below it, waits for the four symbols (battle music, merge) to turn it into her reflection, spawns the reflection and its cover, frees Samus after half a second and starts when she turns. It then shows the shootable symbol for 2, 1.5, 1 or 2/3 seconds after a random delay from sRandomNumberTable (now exported); an uncharged pistol shot on it hurts Samus by 25, 50 or 100 energy by difficulty through the Ruins Test forced movement, and a charged one keeps it out. After the first symbol it spawns the outline and the ghost, then moves in the widening circle and, after two symbols, the six-arc atom pattern, triggering ground and standing lightning; the fourth symbol sends it back to the center, and its despawn removes the ceiling and sets event 67. The ghost fades in and follows the actor, flashing with it, then fades back and out at the center; the outline precedes the ghost and warns of lightning, whose bolts explode on the ground (static) or split into two waves along the floor and up the walls, releasing movement afterwards. The shootable symbol spawns at double speed, despawns with a sound fade or, when hit, becomes a placed symbol flying to its corner of the arena (counted once placed); the fourth sends the ghost away and all four light up again. The closing reflection turns Samus standing on it to face the background, runs the suited reflection timing and fades the music before the cutscene fade, then shows the Fully Powered Suit banner. Captures in chozodia_042 show Samus frozen under the ghost during the merge and the fight started with the ghost under its cover. (Before patch 0396 its secondary sprites fell back to the Skree graphics owner and were not drawn.) Its despawn now requests EFFECT_CUTSCENE_GETTING_FULLY_POWERED (the runtime's request 6), which plays the cutscene and the suit upgrade (mzm.cutscenes.getting_fully_powered); a capture in chozodia_042 with the `--ruins-test-end` staging (the fight skipped) follows the despawn, the closing reflection turning Samus, the cutscene, the banner, the suit upgrade and the fight stage reaching SUIT_ANIM_ENDED. The symbols' dynamic palette is ported: every RUINS_TEST_TRANSFER_DYNAMIC_PAL of the four symbols' lighting, merging and disappearing, the shot symbol's flight (a 4-frame pulse through the base and the six shot palettes) and its placing flash sets MzmSpriteGlobals.ruins_test_palette, and the renderer draws the tables that read OBJ row 14's first eight colours with the baked /s1 to /s6 variants (reset with the spriteset). A capture sequence in chozodia_042 shows the four symbols turning from purple to light blue as they light up, then merging. (The earlier note that the symbols were invisible predated patch 0396's graphics owners; they were drawn with the base palette since.) The reflections now reproduce every native OBJ update: the opening actor fades through sRuinsTestPal_SamusReflection and switches to the direction-specific turning tiles; the closing actor starts with the suitless tiles and palette pulse, is hidden while the full-suit tiles are copied to OBJ VRAM 0x4280/0x4680, then fades through sRuinsTestPal_SamusReflectionFullSuit. The exporter bakes the /tr, /tl, /u and /f variants from the ROM and the runtime selects them through MzmSpriteGlobals.ruins_test_reflection_gfx and ruins_test_reflection_palette. A chozodia_042 `--ruins-test-end` capture sequence shows the suitless silhouette, the hidden copy, the pale full suit and its final colours. The ghost now reproduces each native `BLDALPHA` phase: 16/0 to 16/8 then 9/8 on spawn, 10/16 to 16/16 while fading back, and 15/16 to 0/16 while despawning; its damage flash disables alpha temporarily. The renderer evaluates `OBJ * EVA / 16 + target * EVB / 16` with independent coefficients. A `--ruins-test-ghost` capture sequence in chozodia_042 verifies the visible progression at 16/0, 16/4, 14/8 and 9/8. The lightning warning now starts BG_FLASH_QUICK_YELLOW at its native OAM frame: the shared palette processor snapshots rows 0 to 14, applies white 16/16, blue 16/16 and the falling white stages 14, 12, 8, 6, 4 and 2, then restores the snapshot; a `--background-flash quick` sequence verifies the full visual transition. The opening reflection now hands off after two frames to ParticleSamusReflection: it mirrors Samus's current animated body around the moving ghost, stays alive off screen, hides while a symbol is shootable and is removed when the last symbol is hit. A chozodia_042 capture shows Samus running right while the mirror runs left at the arena centre. The despawn records gInGameTimerAtBosses[2] (MzmSpriteGlobals.igt_at_bosses, saved with the file); unit-tested.

[+] **`mzm.enemies.skree` — Skrees and their explosion.** Green and blue Skrees: detection range, spin, dive with sSkreeFallingSpeed and the growing drift toward Samus, landing, the four impact debris, the crash timing, the huge explosion and the four spinning explosion parts as secondary sprites with their own damage, removed off screen. Each explosion frame is now extracted as separate OBJ art; the renderer rotates every OBJ center with the ROM sine table and native signed Q8.8 rounding, keeps OAM priority, ignores affine Y flip and clips each object to its double-size hardware window. Checked by capture in brinstar_004: a green Skree dives, crashes, releases all four rotating secondary parts and removes them when their animation ends.

Native evidence: `mzm:c:src/sprite.c:SpriteCheckOnScreen`, `mzm:c:src/sprite.c:SpriteSpawnSecondary`, `mzm:c:src/sprites_ai/skree.c:Skree`, `mzm:c:src/sprites_ai/skree.c:SkreeExplosion`.

[+] **`mzm.enemies.sova` — Sovas.** Purple and orange Sovas (orange: palette row 1, one subpixel faster): placement onto any surface, a speed that follows the animation frame, crawling on floors (with their two slope tables), walls and ceilings (work2, with its own table instead of a Y flip), the eight native turns with their offsets, falls with the big hazard-specific liquid splash, landings in a random direction and death into drops. During a screen shake (SpriteUtilShouldFall), a Sova on the screen stops, and one on a wall or ceiling falls.

[+] **`mzm.enemies.space_pirate` — Space Pirates and their lasers.** The five SpacePirate sprites (normal, three waiting, the faster-shooting variant) and SSPRITE_SPACE_PIRATE_LASER: line-of-sight detection on the clipdata grid (forward rows, diagonals up and down, crawling and climbing cases) through the gSpriteDrawOrder scratch flags, walking with acceleration and slope slowdown, the obstacle probes (jumps from the four ROM tables, crawling under low ceilings, turns), zonelines and wall jump points from the exported clipdata movement (M lines), climbing, wall pauses, wall jumps, crawling, alerted walking, charging and two-laser volleys on the ground and on walls, lasers hurting Samus and other pirates, the Chozodia alarm timer (set on sight, 480 frames, stopped by the last pirate's death, kept after Mecha Ridley) and the laser's CAA_BOMB_PISTOL on hatches. With the alarm on (or Mecha Ridley dead), every door transition spawns a waiting pirate at the arrival point (half a block back toward the door; graphics slot 5 in spritesets 88 and 103; crawling out of hatchless doors), and a room whose spriteset has no pirate stops the alarm. Rooms export their default, first-event and second-event spritesets; RoomLoad and waiting-pirate arrivals use the active variant, with second-event priority as in SpriteLoadRoomSprites. The charged paralyzer pistol drops a pirate's standing state, freezes it for 60 suitless ticks and flickers its black/purple rows near release. Smoke-run in the 47 Chozodia rooms with pirates. The black pirates (after the Fully Powered Suit) share the pirate routines in palette row 2 (baked), wait until on screen, take damage only from the Plasma Beam and missiles (other beams are absorbed), burst the other pirates' lasers, charge and shoot twice as fast, hop over the pirate ahead or jump after shooting with their own jump table and faster alerted jumps, and die without the alarm bookkeeping; a capture in chozodia_013 (event 67) shows two of them on the walkway. The escape-ship pirate (spawned by the opening escape ship) runs the same routines behind its own first poses: hidden mid-jump on the seventh jumping frame, shown and hurting after a frame (draw order 12), then a jump out after 6 frames with jump table 3, its momentum dropped when Samus is behind. DecrementChozodiaAlarm now enables the room's alarm palette and plays the alarm music (priority 0x40) every frame it rings, and DisableChozodiaAlarm lets the palette stop after its cycle and, before the Fully Powered Suit, returns to the stealth or detected music (UpdateMusicAfterAlarmDisable); a new game (not a loaded file) stops the alarm as SpriteLoadAllData does. The lasers have draw order 3 and their burst BG1's priority; the hatch shot no longer plays the firing sound. A capture in chozodia_013 (event 67) shows the pirates firing with the alarm ringing and the room's alarm palette (animated palette 10) cycling.

[+] **`mzm.enemies.tangle_vine` — Tangle Vines and the vine Geruta.** The four plain vines stand on the ground or hang a block higher from the ceiling with their native hitboxes, are solid and hurt on contact, and die with their native explosion heights. The red Geruta and larva vines keep their offsets and hitboxes. The vine Geruta animates its grip and Geruta through the ROM-exported multi-sprite table; killing the Geruta leaves the bare vine, killing the vine frees the Geruta as a red Geruta. A capture in norfair_024 shows the vines, red vines and a Geruta perched on a vine. (Before patch 0396 its secondary sprites fell back to the Skree graphics owner and were not drawn.)

[+] **`mzm.enemies.tourian_defences` — Zebetites and Tourian cannons.** Zebetites (both ids, sharing the first's graphics) are solid sideways (SSC_SOLID_SIDEWAYS), change their look every 40 health lost after the first 20 (with the hurt sounds), heal 20 health every half second (a second on easy) while not hurt, and when destroyed explode, set their first event and come back 20 blocks left, the second destruction setting the next event; a pair already destroyed twice is removed. Cannons (Cannon, Cannon2, Cannon3) look away from an adjacent wall (else everywhere, random start), sweep through their five aims with a 1.1 s delay, shoot on arrival by chance (.625 sideways, .25 down, .125 diagonally) on screen, move to their other place 20 or 24 blocks away when Samus is past their range (Cannon3 a block higher there) and explode once Mother Brain is dead; their bullets leave from the mouth along the aim until a solid block. Checked by capture in tourian_004 (both Zebetites, cannons sweeping and firing).

Native evidence: `mzm:c:src/sprites_ai/zebetite_and_cannon.c:Cannon`, `mzm:c:src/sprites_ai/zebetite_and_cannon.c:CannonBullet`, `mzm:c:src/sprites_ai/zebetite_and_cannon.c:CannonCheckSurroundings`, `mzm:c:src/sprites_ai/zebetite_and_cannon.c:CannonFireBullet`, `mzm:c:src/sprites_ai/zebetite_and_cannon.c:Zebetite`.

[+] **`mzm.enemies.worker_robot` — Worker Robots.** Worker Robots are solid (SSC_SOLID: Samus stands on them and is carried) and immune to projectiles; they sleep facing away from Samus until a hit's stun flash wakes them, stand half a second, then walk a subpixel a frame until Samus is in front of them, a ledge or a wall on a slope stops them, when they go back to sleep lying the other way. A missile (half a second) or a super missile (a second) touching them from the side, in their own pass before ProjectileCheckHittingSprite, is removed with its impact particle and pushes them away at up to two pixels a frame with five times the animation speed and the footstep sound. Without a floor they fall (sSpritesFallingSpeed awake, sWorkerRobotSleepingFallingSpeed asleep) and land with their sound. The runtime now runs ProjectileCheckHittingSprite where ProjectileUpdate does, after the spawn and before the projectile loop, which their missile pass depends on. A capture in chozodia_051 shows the robot asleep, woken by two beam shots (WAKING_UP), then walking away along the corridor.

[+] **`mzm.enemies.zeela` — Zeelas and the red Zeela eyes.** Zeelas reuse the Zoomer crawler with their speed, hitbox, ground X flip, single slope table, faster falling and landing animations and the big hazard-specific liquid splash; the red Zeela throws two spinning eyes that stop on solid collision or Samus and explode. Their full OAM boxes are exported beside the cropped art, so each single-part eye rotates around its hardware center and is clipped to the native 8x8 OBJ window. Checked by capture in kraid_017: a red Zeela dies under Screw Attack and releases both spinning eye sprites.

[+] **`mzm.enemies.zoomer` — Sprite engine core, the Zoomer and sprite combat.** Room sprite placements, sprite graphics, the sprite RNG, animation and collision core, sprite families (a family whose OAM tables are missing is reported and disabled alone), secondary sprites (SpriteSpawnSecondary), whole and per-part rotation, the Zoomer crawl, turns and falls, Samus contact damage with knockback and screw attack kills (one hurting contact per frame), beam and missile hits (missiles tumbling off the sprites they cannot hurt), stuns with the stun palette flash (SpriteUtilUpdateStunTimer), freezing (SpriteUtilUpdateFreezeTimer in every ported AI, the frozen and stun rows baked as /frozen and /stun library variants), frozen and SSC_SOLID / SSC_SOLID_CHECK_COLLIDING sprites as solid platforms (SpriteUtilSamusStandingOnSprite: Samus stands on them, is pushed below them or stopped at their sides), SSC_ZIPLINE grabs (SPOSE_ON_ZIPLINE under the zipline from mid-air or spinning, SPOSE_MORPH_BALL_ON_ZIPLINE inside it from morph ball mid-air or ballsparking, gPreviousXPosition moved with her), frozen sprites dropping Samus from a zipline (SPOSE_MID_AIR_REQUEST), SP_SOLID_FOR_PROJECTILES sprites (every projectile only sets their solid hit, with its own impact particle; the Plasma Beam goes through), and death with its explosion particle into enemy drops are ported. Sprites draw by drawOrder (1 in front), orders 9 and above behind Samus (SpriteDrawAll_LowPriority before SamusDraw) and the others in front of her; SSC_CAN_STAND_ON_TOP sprites only let Samus stand on them; SP_ALWAYS_ACTIVE sprites keep running while gPreventMovementTimer stops the others; secondary sprites draw with their spawning sprite's graphics. During any screen shake, Zoomers and Zeelas on the screen stop crawling and those on walls or ceilings fall (SpriteUtilShouldFall). SSC_HURTS_SAMUS_A_LOT hurts with five times the stats damage and a knockback a subpixel stronger; the contact damage follows the difficulty (halved on easy, doubled on hard, after the suits) and the sprites see gSamusWeaponInfo.chargeCounter, last frame's gArmCannonY / gArmCannonX and gDifficulty. A capture in brinstar_000 and brinstar_001 shows the yellow and red Zoomers crawling along floors, walls and ceilings (one climbs a wall from y 406 to 451 in 90 frames).

Native evidence: `mzm:c:src/clipdata.c:ClipdataConvertToCollision`, `mzm:c:src/projectile_util.c:ProjectileCheckHittingSprite`, `mzm:c:src/projectile_util.c:ProjectileDealDamage`, `mzm:c:src/projectile_util.c:ProjectileHitSolidSprite`, `mzm:c:src/projectile_util.c:ProjectileHitSprite`, `mzm:c:src/projectile_util.c:ProjectileMissileHitSprite`, `mzm:c:src/projectile_util.c:ProjectileSuperMissileHitSprite`, `mzm:c:src/sprite.c:SpriteCheckOnScreen`, `mzm:c:src/sprite.c:SpriteDrawAll_LowPriority`, `mzm:c:src/sprite.c:SpriteDrawAll_MediumPriority`, `mzm:c:src/sprite.c:SpriteInitPrimary`, `mzm:c:src/sprite.c:SpriteLoadRoomSprites`, `mzm:c:src/sprite.c:SpriteUpdate`, `mzm:c:src/sprite.c:SpriteUpdateAnimation`, `mzm:c:src/sprite_util.c:SpriteUtilAlignYPositionOnSlopeAtOrigin`, `mzm:c:src/sprite_util.c:SpriteUtilCheckOnZipline`, `mzm:c:src/sprite_util.c:SpriteUtilCheckPullingSelfUp`, `mzm:c:src/sprite_util.c:SpriteUtilCheckStopSamusAgainstSolidSpriteLeft`, `mzm:c:src/sprite_util.c:SpriteUtilCheckStopSamusAgainstSolidSpriteRight`, `mzm:c:src/sprite_util.c:SpriteUtilCheckVerticalCollisionAtPosition`, `mzm:c:src/sprite_util.c:SpriteUtilGetCollisionAtPosition`, `mzm:c:src/sprite_util.c:SpriteUtilIsSpriteStunned`, `mzm:c:src/sprite_util.c:SpriteUtilSamusAndSpriteCollision`, `mzm:c:src/sprite_util.c:SpriteUtilSamusStandingOnSprite`, `mzm:c:src/sprite_util.c:SpriteUtilShouldFall`, `mzm:c:src/sprite_util.c:SpriteUtilSpriteDeath`, `mzm:c:src/sprite_util.c:SpriteUtilSpriteTakeDamageFromSamusContact`, `mzm:c:src/sprite_util.c:SpriteUtilTakeDamageFromSprite`, `mzm:c:src/sprite_util.c:SpriteUtilUpdateFreezeTimer`, `mzm:c:src/sprite_util.c:SpriteUtilUpdateStunTimer`, `mzm:c:src/sprites_ai/zoomer.c:Zoomer`, `mzm:c:src/sprites_ai/zoomer.c:ZoomerDeath`.

### Items and drops

[+] **`mzm.items.chozo_statues` — Item, hint and unknown item Chozo statues, refills and hints.** "The eight item statues (Long, Ice and Wave Beams, Bombs, Speed Booster, High Jump, Screw Attack, Varia Suit) and their hint statues run ChozoStatue with gSubSpriteData1: the multi-sprite tables (Standing, Sitting, Seated) are read from the decompilation, the head and leg take their OAM and position from it, the eye, arm and glow parts their position; the statue faces its native direction, makes its seated or standing body solid (CAA_MAKE_NON_POWER_GRIP, the hint statue's hand CAA_MAKE_SOLID_GRIPPABLE) and spawns its parts and, while Samus lacks the item, the ball. The ball (SSC_SOLID, one health) reveals the item when shot, then Samus touching it starts gPreventMovementTimer, registers the item and its events (ChozoStatueRegisterItem) and opens the item's one-line message banner, the ball flickering until the banner closes; the statue then waits 40 frames and idles, its arm glowing. Statues draw with their own graphics; the sprite engine gained drawOrder, SP_ALWAYS_ACTIVE and graphics owners for secondary sprites. Checked in brinstar_005: shooting the ball reveals the Long Beam orb, touching it shows the ""Long Beam"" banner, and after A the ball is gone and the hand glows. The statue's hand grabs Samus (SPOSE_GRABBED_BY_CHOZO_STATUE, at its exact height and within a quarter block): a hint statue in the Morph Ball or rolling registers the hint, opens its eye, fades the music for a second (FadeMusic), flashes its palette rows (sChozoStatueFlashingPaletteRows every 12 frames, the hint sound), plays MUSIC_CHOZO_STATUE_HINT, sits down along the Sitting table with flickering echoes (SpawnChozoStatueMovement), seats and refills; a refill statue in the Morph Ball refills energy, missiles, super missiles and power bombs (SpriteUtilRefill*, each followed by 13 frames of its HUD counter) with the refill glow and palette flash, opens ""Energy Tank recharge complete"" or ""Weapons and energy fully restored"", sleeps, closes its eye (a hint statue then replays gMusicTrackInfo.currentRoomTrack, the last room track the runtime entered) and lets Samus go when she may move. The exporter adds the statues' palette rows 1 to 3. Checked in brinstar_005 (`--spawn 126 112 --item long-beam`, Down twice): grabbed, golden flash, refill banner, released in the hand; in brinstar_002 (`--spawn 808 112`, Down twice) the Long Beam hint statue grabs Samus, flashes, sits down with her and refills. The unknown item statues (Plasma Beam, Space Jump, Gravity Suit) run the same AI with their own OAM tables (UnknownItemChozoStatue*, the unknown balls), multi-sprite tables (read from the positional initializers of the decompilation), part and refill sprites; their item is obtained (gEquipment.beamBombs / suitMisc) but stays inactive, as UpdateSuitType keeps it for the normal suit, and its banner reads ""Unknown Item"". Checked in kraid_033 (`--spawn 186 128`, B, Right): the Space Jump statue's ball reveals the unknown orb and touching it shows ""Unknown Item"". A hint statue's flashing ends by opening the hint map screen (gPauseScreenFlag PAUSE_SCREEN_CHOZO_HINT) with the hint music, then the statue sits down. Its common hint flash now runs the exact slight-yellow behavior table against BG palette rows 0 to 14, leaving the animated row and every OBJ untouched; the Long Beam hint still lacks its separate long transparency." Taking the Varia Suit now runs the item acquisition status screen and, back in the room, IGC_GETTING_VARIA (the suit upgrade flash of mzm.cutscenes.getting_fully_powered's module): Samus facing the foreground at (10 blocks - 8, 8 blocks), the Varia Suit active at its stage 11; checked in brinstar_027.

[+] **`mzm.items.enemy_drops` — Enemy drops.** Drop rolls from the ROM probabilities (as the sprite or, for respawning sprites, as a drop following its slot) with the full energy and ammunition substitutions, the hidden spawn, pickup refills, blinking and despawn timers, native BG1 priority, and the common sprite graphics are ported. Power bomb drops spawn and refill one power bomb with their sound. Mecha Ridley's grouped pickup selects one of three native OAM tables from its follower slot, refills 60 energy and clamps to maximum. A capture in brinstar_000 shows a Zoomer shot dead leaving a large energy drop, which Samus collects by walking over it (energy 50 to 70).

Native evidence: `mzm:c:src/hud_generic.c:HudGenericLoadCommonSpriteGfx`, `mzm:c:src/sprite.c:SpriteSpawnDropFollowers`, `mzm:c:src/sprite_util.c:SpriteUtilCheckHasDrops`, `mzm:c:src/sprite_util.c:SpriteUtilDetermineEnemyDrop`, `mzm:c:src/sprites_ai/enemy_drop.c:EnemyDrop`.

[+] **`mzm.items.item_sprites` — Morph Ball, Power Grip and Charge Beam pickups.** The Morph Ball (outline sprite with the ball's status), the Power Grip (glow removed once it is taken, EVENT_POWER_GRIP_OBTAINED) and the Charge Beam (spawning table, glow following its height, the float of sChargeBeamIdleYMovement, EVENT_CHARGE_BEAM_OBTAINED): removed when owned, touched they give their item, hold Samus with SAMUS_ITEM_PMT and the item banner with its jingle, and flicker until the banner releases her. Checked in brinstar_000 (--spawn 212 496: the jump onto the pillar takes the Morph Ball and shows its banner) and crateria_012 ( the Power Grip from the statue's hands).

[+] **`mzm.items.tanks` — Energy, missile, super missile and power bomb tanks.** On odd frames, the four probes of BgClipCheckTouchingTransitionOrTank (Samus's right and left hitbox edges on her center row, the bottom and top rows on her center column) read the behaviors of the current Clipdata, last probe first; a revealed tank (hidden ones are not tanks until their block breaks) on an explored minimap tile (MinimapCheckIsTileExplored) starts gCollectingTank, sets gPreventMovementTimer, raises the equipment within the game total (sStartingHealthAmmo, the total of sNumberOfTanksPerArea and sTankIncreaseAmount, exported from the decompilation: 100 energy refilled, 5 missiles, 2 super missiles, 2 power bombs) and spawns the message banner (the first missile, super missile or power bomb tank with its own message). BgClipFinishCollectingTank (on the banner's removal) empties the BG1 block, clears the Clipdata to air or water (underwater tanks) and registers the item in gItemsCollected by area and room and on the minimap (MinimapUpdateForCollectedItem); BgClipRemoveCollectedTanks removes them again on every room load. Checked in brinstar_019 (`--spawn 615 96`, Right): Samus walks onto the energy tank column, energy 99 to 199, the banner shows its two lines and Samus stays frozen until A; the tank block is then empty. The tank increases follow the game's difficulty (sTankIncreaseAmount[gDifficulty]: 100/5/2/2 on easy and normal, 50/2/1/1 on hard), exported per difficulty.

Native evidence: `mzm:c:src/bg_clip.c:BgClipCheckTouchingSpecialClipdata`, `mzm:c:src/bg_clip.c:BgClipCheckTouchingTransitionOrTank`, `mzm:c:src/bg_clip.c:BgClipFinishCollectingTank`, `mzm:c:src/bg_clip.c:BgClipRemoveCollectedTanks`, `mzm:c:src/bg_clip.c:BgClipSetItemAsCollected`, `mzm:c:src/minimap.c:MinimapCheckIsTileExplored`, `mzm:c:src/minimap.c:MinimapUpdateForCollectedItem`.  
Local implementation: [`src/runtime/mzm_items.c`](../src/runtime/mzm_items.c), [`src/runtime/room_runtime.c`](../src/runtime/room_runtime.c), [`src/assets/mzm/runtime_room.c`](../src/assets/mzm/runtime_room.c).  
Tests: [`tests/test_mzm_items.c`](../tests/test_mzm_items.c), [`tests/test_runtime_steep_slopes.c`](../tests/test_runtime_steep_slopes.c).
Dependencies: `mzm.environment.breakable_blocks`, `mzm.interface.message_banner`, `mzm.interface.hud_and_minimap`.  
Known divergences: the transition probes run every frame for doors, the tank probes on odd frames only.  
Next action: Save the collected items and the minimap progress with a save file.
Evidence: in game: `brinstar_019`.

### Environment interactions

[?] **`mzm.environment.inventory-boundary` — source coverage.** The current classifier assigns 0 discovered routine(s) to this category. Category completeness has not been established.

[+] **`mzm.environment.breakable_blocks` — Breakable, reforming and crumble blocks, hidden tanks and bomb chains.** Every beam, missile, super missile, bomb explosion point, power bomb explosion edge and pirate laser impact runs BlockApplyCcaa on the block it reaches (hatches first): shot, missile and super missile blocks break by weakness, 2x2 blocks break together, never-reform blocks are remembered and removed when the room reloads, reforming blocks play the native stages (255-frame wait, held while Samus stands in them) with the BG1 breaking frames, hidden tanks are revealed, bomb chains run both ways with their sub-chains; the screw attack and the speed booster break blocks on contact and crumble blocks break under Samus's feet. The tables come from src/data/block_data.c through fusion_mzm_serialize_block_tables (src/assets/mzm/export.c). Checked in brinstar_022: crumble blocks give way under Samus and a shot block breaks through its frames. CAA_MAKE_SOLID_GRIPPABLE, CAA_MAKE_STOP_ENEMY and CAA_MAKE_NON_POWER_GRIP make an air block solid through gMakeSolidBlocks (whose loops visit every other entry, as natively), and CAA_REMOVE_SOLID keeps the BG1 block of a block made solid that way.

Native evidence: `mzm:c:src/bg_clip.c:BgClipCheckWalkingOnCrumbleBlock`, `mzm:c:src/block.c:BlockApplyCcaa`, `mzm:c:src/block.c:BlockCheckCcaa`, `mzm:c:src/block.c:BlockCheckRevealBombChainBlock`, `mzm:c:src/block.c:BlockCheckRevealOrDestroyBombBlock`, `mzm:c:src/block.c:BlockCheckRevealOrDestroyNonBombBlock`, `mzm:c:src/block.c:BlockCheckSamusInReformingBlock`, `mzm:c:src/block.c:BlockCheckStartNewSubBombChain`, `mzm:c:src/block.c:BlockDestroyNonReformBlock`, `mzm:c:src/block.c:BlockDestroySquareBlock`, `mzm:c:src/block.c:BlockProcessBombChains`, `mzm:c:src/block.c:BlockRemoveNeverReformBlocks`, `mzm:c:src/block.c:BlockSamusApplyScrewSpeedboosterDamageToEnvironment`, `mzm:c:src/block.c:BlockStartBombChain`, `mzm:c:src/block.c:BlockStoreBrokenNonReformBlock`, `mzm:c:src/block.c:BlockStoreBrokenReformBlock`, `mzm:c:src/block.c:BlockStoreSingleNeverReformBlock`, `mzm:c:src/block.c:BlockUpdateBrokenBlockAnimation`, `mzm:c:src/block.c:BlockUpdateBrokenBlocks`, `mzm:c:src/block.c:BlockUpdateMakeSolidBlocks`, `mzm:c:src/samus.c:SamusCheckScrewSpeedboosterAffectingEnvironment`.

[+] **`mzm.environment.gunship` — Gunship.** "A new game from the file select starts in Crateria's first room with the gunship six blocks up landing in 3.2 seconds (SOUND_GUNSHIP_LANDING, the vertical then horizontal flames, the HUD hidden, the pause disabled), Samus inside it (SPOSE_SAVING_LOADING_GAME with lastWallTouchedMidAir, drawn with the native in-ship animation behind the ship); half a second before touching down it starts the intro text, which leads to the game's start in Brinstar. On the ground the gunship's entrance opens and its platform rises while Samus is near; Samus standing on top (SSC_CAN_STAND_ON_TOP) enters it (turning from the foreground, sinking with the platform behind the closing entrance), is refilled (energy, missiles, super missiles, power bombs with the HUD's refill animations and SOUND_GUNSHIP_REFILL), gets ""Weapons and energy fully restored"", then the save prompt: yes saves the file (the gunship's saving sound and ""Save complete""); then she rises out of the hatch and faces the screen. A file saved there resumes with Samus rising out of the ship. Checked by capture: the landing and its fade into the intro text, entering, the refill, the save and leaving in crateria_000. The tests run the landing, entering, the refill, the save prompt and leaving."

The escape (Mother Brain killed), EFFECT_CUTSCENE_EXITING_ZEBES and the complete Tourian escape handoff are connected, but the take-off is untested in a full playthrough. Next action: Play through and verify the escape take-off and Tourian sequence against an emulator.
Evidence: in game: `crateria_000`.

[+] **`mzm.environment.map_stations` — Map stations.** The station AI follows the decompilation: two blocks lower, retracted on its last frame when the area's map is already downloaded (gEquipment.downloadedMapStatus) or during the alarm, else idle with its back and front screens (MapStationPart). Samus standing (not crouching or morphed) on its platform at its exact height and within a block of its centre is moved to the centre, loses her invincibility and is set to SPOSE_TURNING_AROUND_TO_DOWNLOAD_MAP_DATA (facing right) or SPOSE_DOWNLOADING_MAP_DATA, with the station sound. After the grab animation the download lasts 70 frames: its timer reaching 10 sets gPauseScreenFlag to PAUSE_SCREEN_MAP_DOWNLOAD, which opens the pause screen's map download; then the retracting animation, the area's map message (the enemy location message elsewhere) with Samus frozen on her first frame and the sound faded, the wait for the banner's removal, a 10-frame delay, the retraction with its sound and Samus back to standing. The screens switch to their downloading and downloaded animations and vanish. Checked by capture in brinstar_032 (grab, download, message, retraction, the minimap showing the downloaded map).

Native evidence: `mzm:c:src/samus.c:SamusSetPose`, `mzm:c:src/sprites_ai/map_station.c:MapStation`, `mzm:c:src/sprites_ai/map_station.c:MapStationCheckSamusGrabbedAnimEnded`, `mzm:c:src/sprites_ai/map_station.c:MapStationDelayBeforeRetracting`, `mzm:c:src/sprites_ai/map_station.c:MapStationDownloading`, `mzm:c:src/sprites_ai/map_station.c:MapStationIdle`, `mzm:c:src/sprites_ai/map_station.c:MapStationInit`, `mzm:c:src/sprites_ai/map_station.c:MapStationPart`, `mzm:c:src/sprites_ai/map_station.c:MapStationRetracting`, `mzm:c:src/sprites_ai/map_station.c:MapStationSpawnMessage`, `mzm:c:src/sprites_ai/map_station.c:MapStationWaitForMessage`.  
Local implementation: [`src/runtime/mzm_gadget_sprites.c`](../src/runtime/mzm_gadget_sprites.c), [`src/runtime/mzm_sprites.c`](../src/runtime/mzm_sprites.c), [`src/runtime/mzm_samus.c`](../src/runtime/mzm_samus.c), [`src/assets/mzm/sprites.c`](../src/assets/mzm/sprites.c), [`src/runtime/room_runtime.c`](../src/runtime/room_runtime.c).

Dependencies: `mzm.environment.room_gadgets`, `mzm.interface.message_banner`, `mzm.menus.map_download`.  
Known divergences: Not compared with an emulator. The station and its screens keep the sprites' drawing priority instead of BG1's (bgPriority 1) and SP_SOLID_FOR_PROJECTILES is not modeled; the message banner is the runtime's single banner rather than a sprite slot.  
Next action: Port the save stations, then compare a station with an emulator.
Evidence: in game: `brinstar_032`.

[+] **`mzm.environment.room_gadgets` — Elevator pads, water drops, steam, Gerons, unknown item blocks, morph ball launchers, ziplines, pistons, the glass tube and the rising Chozo pillar.** The AIs follow the decompilation: the elevator pad (moved 2 pixels up, always active, synced to Samus and on its Moving animation while she uses an elevator); water drops (hidden for gSpriteRng * 8 frames, spawning animation, falling by sSpritesFallingSpeed, splashing on a block with SpriteUtilCheckVerticalCollisionAtPositionSlopes or on the water surface held at gEffectYPosition, hidden again for 100 + rng * 8 frames at a random x offset); steam (ground, ceiling with Y flip one block up, left or right wall turned horizontal, killed in the air; large/small draw distances; a random first frame; the large or small steam sound on screen at frame 0); diagonal steam (X flip without a wall on its left, Y flip for the downward ones); Gerons (their event checks, three CAA_MAKE_NON_POWER_GRIP blocks, shaking on ISFT 2 with its sound, the destroyed stump after their event; the destruction and its events when four Parasites hold one) and the Norfair Geron (gone after EVENT_POWER_GRIP_OBTAINED); unknown item blocks (2x2 CAA_MAKE_SOLID_GRIPPABLE blocks, ignoring projectiles until their item event, then destroyed by a hit: CAA_REMOVE_SOLID, the breaking sound, the light secondary at frame 7). Checked by capture in crateria_001 (steam), brinstar_021 (Geron), kraid_012 (Space Jump block) and crateria_011 (Plasma Beam block, water drops). Morph ball launchers: their back part and the five blocks around made non power grip; a bomb in its first spin set on the launcher is taken (BOMB_STAGE_PLACED_ON_LAUNCHER), the launcher waits half a second and plays its launching animation, and when the bomb's explosion launches Samus (SPOSE_DELAY_BEFORE_BALLSPARKING) the energy appears around her for a second. Checked by capture in brinstar_000 (the launch is stopped by the power bomb blocks above, as natively before the Power Bombs). Ziplines: the initial direction away from the stop-enemy block beside them, Samus grabbed from below (SSC_ZIPLINE), the grabbing, grabbed and releasing animations (unlit, then lit once EVENT_ZIPLINES_ACTIVATED is set), the run to the other stop-enemy block once lit (a quarter block a frame, a pixel less carrying Samus, the moving sound every 16 frames and its fade), the snap to the block's middle that drops her (SPOSE_MID_AIR_REQUEST) and the turn; zipline buttons (two blocks up, bound to the room's zipline or removed, lit with the event, a shot calls the zipline with its sound, active until it stops); the zipline generator (its conductor behind Samus, four CAA_MAKE_SOLID_GRIPPABLE blocks, Samus in morph ball at its top set to SPOSE_ACTIVATING_ZIPLINES for two seconds with the activating sound, the morph symbol over her, the electricity at 1.5 seconds, the animated palette back on at 16 frames from the end, then the event and SPOSE_MORPH_BALL; already activated after the event). Checked by capture in kraid_004 (activation, the button shot from the generator, the zipline's run from 440 to 232 and its turn, Samus hanging under it). Pistons: four body blocks made non power grip, the bottom one once Samus is away, a damaging beam or bomb hit on the head (ISFT_DAMAGED) opens them with their sound, freeing the bottom block on the opening's third frame, then the top block once Samus is out of it; checked by capture in brinstar_028 (a standing shot from the right raises the piston). The glass tube: intact (with the rain sound before the Fully Powered Suit), a power bomb touching it after that suit sets EVENT_GLASS_TUBE_BROKEN, two seconds later it cracks with its sound, then opens its two rows of eleven blocks with dust at its four ends and a half-second shake both ways, and stays broken (its rows removed at once on later visits); the intact tube was checked by capture in chozodia_023. Before this patch the gadget OAM tables past the 72nd (the ziplines' and gates') overflowed MzmSpriteKind and read zero durations; the limit is now 128 with static asserts. The rising Chozo pillar (crateria_016 and crateria_017): never drawn, it waits for EVENT_POWER_GRIP_OBTAINED, then for 704 frames raises BG2 (gBg2Movement.yOffset, half a pixel a frame) with its sound, a vertical shake every 32 frames and the ROM's debris and dust patterns, spawns its three shadowed, two and one plain platforms over three frames (two CAA_MAKE_SOLID_GRIPPABLE blocks each, the spawning sound and animation, the shadow behind Samus on the 22nd frame), and sets EVENT_CHOZO_PILLAR_FULLY_EXTENDED; later visits place the platforms at once. Checked by capture in crateria_016 with --event 16 (the pillar rising, then the platforms appearing). Gerons in their idle state catch every Parasite whose point is inside them (the Parasite's collision cleared so it counts once); four destroy them; checked by capture in brinstar_023 (VARIA3 destroyed, VARIA1 getting destroyed).

Native evidence: `mzm:c:src/sprite_util.c:SpriteUtilFindPrimary`, `mzm:c:src/sprites_ai/elevator_pad.c:ElevatorPad`, `mzm:c:src/sprites_ai/geron.c:Geron`, `mzm:c:src/sprites_ai/geron_norfair.c:GeronNorfair`, `mzm:c:src/sprites_ai/glass_tube.c:GlassTube`, `mzm:c:src/sprites_ai/glass_tube.c:GlassTubeChangeCcaa`, [+] **`mzm.environment.save_platforms` — Save platforms (and the Chozodia save platform).** The AIs follow the decompilation: the platform (always active, its top part eight blocks above) opens after Samus stands on it for 10 frames (not crouching or morphed), and once opened grabs her (centred, invincibility cleared, SPOSE_TURNING_FROM_FACING_THE_FOREGROUND with her animation frozen, gDisablePause), spawns the save prompt after 4 frames, and on YES flashes with palette row 2 (a baked /row2 variant) while the top extends and the tube, its shadow and the rising ray play; the ray turns it off, the save complete message follows a second later, then a 10-frame delay and the release (SPOSE_FACING_THE_FOREGROUND, the pause allowed again); Samus walking off closes it. In a loaded file's room (gIsLoadingFile) it starts with the tube around Samus and releases her after 340 frames. Checked by capture in brinstar_033 (prompt, saving, message, release, reload). A new game is the native one whatever the runtime's test options: Sram_InitSaveFile and SamusInit without a file (99 energy, no item, no ammunition, no event, no collected item, no opened hatch, a zero timer, the chosen difficulty), the gunship landing in Crateria, the intro text, then RoomLoad at Brinstar's door 0 (x = xStart blocks + xExit + 8 pixels, y = yEnd + 1 blocks + yExit pixels - 1, its own exit exported as X lines) with SamusInit's pose facing the foreground, facing left; checked by a capture of the whole flow from the title screen (Samus at x 632, falling down the first shaft, 99 energy and no ammunition). The equipment is no longer rebuilt from the debug suit preset: the collected items stay through the debug menu, and Samus's graphics follow the equipment. The Chozodia save platform (PSPRITE_SAVE_PLATFORM_CHOZODIA, its own tables, part and sounds, the off tables swapped, off during the alarm) refills Samus before the prompt: energy, then missiles, super missiles and power bombs (energy only suitless) with each counter's refill animation, the refill light and sound, then MESSAGE_WEAPONS_AND_ENERGY_RESTORED (MESSAGE_ENERGY_TANK_RECHARGE_COMPLETE suitless); checked by capture in chozodia_004 (30 energy refilled, the message, the save written, the file loaded back into the tube and Samus released). Both variants initialize off while EscapeDetermineTimer reports the Tourian or Chozodia escape.

### Rooms, areas and maps

[?] **`mzm.rooms.inventory-boundary` — source coverage.** The current classifier assigns 85 discovered routine(s) to this category (85 by source path). Category completeness has not been established.

[+] **`mzm.rooms.backgrounds` — Room backgrounds: layers, priorities, animations, liquids, BG0 blend and BG3 scrolling.** With the ROM bank, mzm_room_gfx rebuilds the room's VRAM and BG palettes as RoomLoadTileset leaves them (animated tiles at 0x4000, common tiles at 0x4800, the tileset at 0x5800, the BG3 graphics below 0xFDE0, palette rows 0-2 common and 3-15 from the tileset), decompresses the BG3 and LZ77 BG0 maps, sets the BG control registers and priorities of TransparencySetRoomEffectsTransparency, and writes the block layers' metatiles (gTilemap, then gCommonTilemap past block 1024) into the 512x256 maps around the camera. Every frame it runs AnimatedGraphicsUpdate (16 slots with their five types, mirrored at the end of VRAM for slots 12-15), the tank animation, AnimatedPaletteUpdate on palette row 15 (disabled without the alarm or the ziplines event, paused during a power bomb), the hatch flashing, and positions a liquid LZ77 BG0 from gEffectYPosition with the water wave (sWaterLoopCounterArray), which also feeds the hazard and sprite liquid surface. Lava, acid and water now animate and blend: checked by capture in norfair_022 (lava), crateria_007 (water over Samus and the Skulteras) and brinstar_000. Screen shakes (mzm_screen_shake, one shared state as gScreenShakeX/Y): a longer shake replaces the current one; once a frame a 2-pixel offset every third frame (up or left, then down or right with intensity bit 0, else nothing), halved in the last quarter second, moves BG0 to BG2 and BG3 by half, never the sprites; the room load clears them. Checked by capture during a power bomb in chozodia_005 (BG1 2 pixels left, then back). A BG_PROP_MOVING BG2 (crateria_016, kraid_030) follows ScrollBg2: BG1's position plus gBg2Movement, kept within BG2 for the GBA screen (the runtime's wider view may show past it). The shared background-effect processor now implements the quick, slight-yellow and heavy-yellow native tables: it snapshots BG rows 0 to 14, pauses during transitions and power bombs, advances one command per frame and restores the exact palette; `--background-flash` captures verify the quick sequence while ROM-free tests cover all three.

Native evidence: `mzm:c:src/animated_graphics.c:AnimatedGraphicsLoad`, `mzm:c:src/animated_graphics.c:AnimatedGraphicsTanksAnimationReset`, `mzm:c:src/animated_graphics.c:AnimatedGraphicsTanksAnimationUpdate`, `mzm:c:src/animated_graphics.c:AnimatedGraphicsTransfer`, `mzm:c:src/animated_graphics.c:AnimatedGraphicsUpdate`, `mzm:c:src/animated_graphics.c:AnimatedPaletteCheckDisableOnTransition`, `mzm:c:src/animated_graphics.c:AnimatedPaletteUpdate`, `mzm:c:src/animated_graphics.c:BackgroundEffectProcess`, `mzm:c:src/animated_graphics.c:BackgroundEffectStart`, `mzm:c:src/animated_graphics.c:BackgroundEffectUpdate`, `mzm:c:src/in_game_cutscene.c:MakeBackgroundFlash`, `mzm:c:src/room.c:RoomLoadBackgrounds`, `mzm:c:src/room.c:RoomLoadTileset`, `mzm:c:src/room.c:RoomSetInitialTilemap`, `mzm:c:src/room.c:RoomUpdateAnimatedGraphicsAndPalettes`, `mzm:c:src/room.c:RoomUpdateBackgroundsPosition`, `mzm:c:src/room.c:RoomUpdateHatchFlashingAnimation`, `mzm:c:src/room.c:RoomUpdateHorizontalTilemap`, `mzm:c:src/room.c:RoomUpdateVerticalTilemap`, `mzm:c:src/screen_shake.c:ScreenShakeStartHorizontal`, `mzm:c:src/screen_shake.c:ScreenShakeStartVertical`, `mzm:c:src/screen_shake.c:ScreenShakeUpdateHorizontal`, `mzm:c:src/screen_shake.c:ScreenShakeUpdateVertical`, `mzm:c:src/scroll.c:ScrollBg2`, `mzm:c:src/scroll.c:ScrollBg3`, `mzm:c:src/scroll.c:ScrollGetBg3Scroll`, `mzm:c:src/scroll.c:ScrollUpdateEffectAndHazePosition`, `mzm:c:src/transparency.c:TransparencyGetBgSizeFlag`, `mzm:c:src/transparency.c:TransparencySetRoomEffectsTransparency`.

Known divergences: Each background is rendered alone by the PPU and the runtime composes them with its sprites by priority, so the BG0 blend is approximated by SDL blending (additive at EVA/16, alpha at 16 - EVB) instead of the GBA's per-pixel targets; the block layers are redrawn around the view every frame instead of the native row and column streaming (the same tiles); the mother ship common tiles (gUseMotherShipDoors, set by door types) are not tracked; the lightning background effect, BG0 and BG3 movement (clouds, snow), the 0x43/0x44/dark-room BG0 positions, haze, gradients, windows and screen fades are not reproduced; without the ROM bank the exported metatile sheets are drawn instead (static). The camera is the runtime's 320x224 follow view.  
Next action: Port the lightning background effect, BG movement and haze, then compose the layers with the sprites per pixel.
Evidence: in game: `chozodia_005`.

### Transitions

[?] **`mzm.transitions.inventory-boundary` — source coverage.** The current classifier assigns 0 discovered routine(s) to this category. Category completeness has not been established.

[+] **`mzm.transitions.doors_and_hatches` — Doors, hatches and room transitions.** Hatches are BG1 and Clipdata blocks driven by the native state machine: BgClipCheckOpeningHatch on beam, missile and laser hits (weakness and health from sHatchBehaviors, super missiles open missile hatches), the opening and closing frames of sHatchesTilemapValues with sHatchesAnimationDurationCounter, the hatch Samus comes through closing behind her, opened hatches remembered per area and overridden to normal hatches. Checked in brinstar_000: a beam opens the blue hatch through its grey frames. Hatch locks (src/runtime/mzm_hatches.c): gray hatches wait for gDoorUnlockTimer (ConnectionLoadDoors); the rooms export their sHatchLockEvents (Q lines: the hatch's door, the event, before or after, unlockable) and RoomLoad locks them as navigation hatches (ConnectionCheckHatchLockEvents); a sprite's LOCK_DOORS locks every hatch when the door fade starts (the arrival hatch closing with the locking sound), and its negative countdown unlocks them (SOUND_DOORS_UNLOCKING, the flashing hatches, then the shots open them); the Long Beam Dessgeega uses both. Door fades (src/runtime/mzm_door_fade.c): a hatch fades the room to black over 16 frames (sColorFading_345f60) except the hatches' palette row, then BG3 shows sDoorTransitionTilemap with sDoorTransitionPal, blended in over BG1 in 8 frames and covering everything (priority 0, Samus included); once the room is loaded the tube slides to the arrival door's height (3 pixels a frame), waits, slides across (6 pixels a frame), then the room comes back from black over 16 frames and the room's track starts (CheckPlayTransitionMusicTrack); the other doors fade to black over 8 frames and back. Samus waits during both (no SamusUpdate, the pause and the in-game timer wait); the medium sprites are hidden after a hatch's fade out. The backgrounds' palette fade is ApplySpecialBackgroundFadingColor on the native room palette. Checked by capture: brinstar_002's hatch to brinstar_003 and chozodia_007's passage to chozodia_037. Area connection doors (sAreaConnections: elevators and the passages between areas) now lead to the connected area's door: the exporter resolves the destination door of each, the runtime takes them like the other doors (an elevator ride without vertical offset) and RoomLoad's elevator placement follows.

Native evidence: `mzm:c:src/bg_clip.c:BgClipCheckOpeningHatch`, `mzm:c:src/color_effects.c:CallApplySpecialBackgroundFadingColor`, `mzm:c:src/color_fading.c:ColorFadingFinishDoorFade`, `mzm:c:src/color_fading.c:ColorFadingFinishDoorTransition`, `mzm:c:src/color_fading.c:ColorFadingProcess`, `mzm:c:src/color_fading.c:ColorFadingProcess_Default`, `mzm:c:src/color_fading.c:ColorFadingProcess_DoorTransition`, `mzm:c:src/color_fading.c:ColorFadingStart`, `mzm:c:src/color_fading.c:ColorFadingStartDefault`, `mzm:c:src/color_fading.c:ColorFadingTransferPaletteOnTransition`, `mzm:c:src/color_fading.c:ColorFadingUpdate`, `mzm:c:src/color_fading.c:ColorFadingUpdate_Default`, `mzm:c:src/color_fading.c:ColorFadingUpdate_DoorTransition`, `mzm:c:src/color_fading.c:unk_5bd58`, `mzm:c:src/connection.c:ConnectionCheckAreaConnection`, `mzm:c:src/connection.c:ConnectionCheckEnterDoor`, `mzm:c:src/connection.c:ConnectionCheckHatchLockEvents`, `mzm:c:src/connection.c:ConnectionCheckUnlockDoors`, `mzm:c:src/connection.c:ConnectionHatchFlashingAnimation`, `mzm:c:src/connection.c:ConnectionHatchStartLockAnimation`, `mzm:c:src/connection.c:ConnectionLoadDoors`, `mzm:c:src/connection.c:ConnectionLockHatches`, `mzm:c:src/connection.c:ConnectionLockHatchesWithTimer`, `mzm:c:src/connection.c:ConnectionOverrideOpenedHatch`, `mzm:c:src/connection.c:ConnectionProcessDoorType`, `mzm:c:src/connection.c:ConnectionSetHatchAsOpened`, `mzm:c:src/connection.c:ConnectionUpdateHatchAnimation`, `mzm:c:src/connection.c:ConnectionUpdateHatches`.

Known divergences: The mother ship hatch graphics are not reproduced; gLastDoorUsed is the arrival door of a door transition only (a loaded file or a new game locks no hatch as the one Samus came through); opened destinations are remembered by position, not by door index. Event-based area destinations (ConnectionFindEventBasedDoor) are not resolved: the normal destination is used. Door fades: the runtime's view is 320x224 with its own camera, so the door transition tilemap's tube starts at the view's edge on the door's side (its last 24 pixels shown on the right, its first 24 on the left, as on the GBA screen) and slides 336 pixels instead of 256 (56 frames instead of 43); the sprites' faded palette rows are drawn as a colour modulation of their baked frames, the BG3 blending as a black veil plus an additive layer; ColorFadingGradients' BG2/BG3 alpha steps, the haze and the window are not reproduced.  
Next action: Compare the door fades and the hatch locks with an emulator; port the mother ship hatches.
Evidence: in game: `brinstar_000`, `brinstar_002`, `brinstar_003`, `chozodia_007`, `chozodia_037`.

### Events and scripts

[?] **`mzm.events.inventory-boundary` — source coverage.** The current classifier assigns 45 discovered routine(s) to this category (45 by source path). Category completeness has not been established.

[+] **`mzm.events.escape_sequence` — Escape countdown and Zebes destruction.** Mother Brain's death starts the invisible Zebes explosion actor at its native offset. It follows the native 16-frame particle and debris cadence, camera-relative positions, Samus-height correction, four explosion sounds and ten-frame two-axis shakes. When the Zebes escape banner finishes spawning, EscapeStart and EscapeSetTimer select the event-driven escape and difficulty duration (easy 3:00, normal 2:00, hard 1:00); the native hundredths and seconds cadence freezes outside gameplay or while movement is prevented. ParticleEscape copies both rows of sEscapeTimerDigitsGfx to their native OBJ VRAM addresses and draws the eight 8x16 OAM objects at their absolute HUD position. At zero, the runtime freezes Samus and the countdown, fades all sounds and music over 60 frames, applies ColorFadingProcess_EscapeFailed's eight white levels, holds white for 61 frames, then enters the native Game Over screen. A successful gunship take-off applies the native exit fades and enters GM_TOURIAN_ESCAPE. TourianEscapeInit loads the native room, OBJ graphics and palettes. TourianEscapeZebesExploding switches from Tourian room 4 to Tourian room 5 and Crateria room 1 at frames 104 and 176, animates four native explosion objects and five debris objects with the room shake, starts the cutscene music, then shows Zebes with the animated departing ship and scrolling explosion through frame 512. TourianEscapeSamusInHerShip runs through frame 372: it selects the Power or Varia palette, expands the native brightness window around suited Samus, reveals the suitless image with the three removal sounds, alpha-fades the flash, and switches to her opened eyes. TourianEscapeSamusLookingAround runs for 57 frames: its two alpha-blended OAM halves converge vertically on the centre of the cockpit while BG0 switches from the left to the right native tilemap. TourianEscapeSamusSurrounded runs for 113 frames: a fixed BG0 cockpit surrounds a centred affine BG2 view that zooms out from Samus's ship to reveal the pirate formation between frames 32 and 47. TourianEscapeSamusFlyingIn reuses the intro starfield, ship graphics and complex affine OAM for 33 frames: the two six/nine-part ship poses alternate as its vertical speed eases and its scale rises from 32 to 480. TourianEscapeSamusChasedByPirates runs for 257 frames: six alternating particles recycle across the scrolling starfield while Samus and three pirate ships cross in both directions with their native OAM poses, coordinates and four sound cues. TourianEscapeSamusChasedByPiratesFiring runs for 385 frames: inherited projectiles rise and recycle while Samus follows eight native accelerated paths, both formations use complex affine OAM, the pirate group shrinks away and three sound cues cover the firing, dodging and departure. TourianEscapeSamusGettingShot runs for 401 frames: a brightness fade reveals Samus and six inherited projectiles over the scrolling starfield, the ship decelerates then oscillates after the hit, and the decisive shot switches to the dedicated BG1 view and sound. TourianEscapeSamusGoingToCrash runs for 81 frames: the damaged ship and its two looping smoke trails are alpha-blended over Zebes, while their native OAM offsets and alternating fire palette accompany a six-frame background drift and the free-falling sound. TourianEscapeSamusCrashing runs for 289 frames: the falling ship crosses a foreground view with four debris poses, the collision reveals the explosion layer and raises every layer to full white before the long native hold. TourianEscapeSamusLookingAtSky runs for 433 frames: the crash white reveals three native layers, two pirate ships cross diagonally, the layers pan at their independent thresholds and the scene ends on black. TourianEscapeSamusLookingAtMotherShip runs its two drifting native backgrounds under diagonal rain, renders ""The timing..."" into OBJ VRAM, alpha-fades its twelve-part text composition over the image, darkens the view and holds frame 448 until A or B. The following frame starts CUTSCENE_COULD_I_SURVIVE; dismissing it activates suitless Samus and loads Chozodia room 0. The --tourian-escape runtime option exposes the sequence for direct captures. The same timer core includes Mecha Ridley's 5:00 or hard 3:00 route. Save platforms initialize in their native off state while either escape is active. The first stage's room views now have their block layers: LoadRoomCutscene's RoomSetInitialTilemap(0 to 2) is ported as mzm_room_gfx_initial_tilemaps, which RLE-decompresses (RoomRleDecompress, the BG form) each room's own BG0-BG2 block data from the ROM and writes the 21 x 16 blocks around the view. Captures of --tourian-escape at frames 40, 130 and 200 show Tourian rooms 4 and 5 and Crateria room 1 with their walls, pipes and rocks under the explosions (the earlier capture showed garbage tiles). A bank-backed test checks that Tourian room 4's empty RLE BG1 is filled by mzm_room_gfx_initial_tilemaps.

Known divergences: The explosion actor is stored outside gSpriteData, so its RNG omits the native free sprite slot. The gameplay fades are a white overlay rather than per-palette-row brightness: during BACKGROUND_EFFECT_EXIT_ZEBES_FADE it is drawn just before Samus (the native mask 0xFFFC spares her OBJ rows), so the objects drawn after her are spared too; the colour fade that follows covers everything, as natively. The post-Tourian monologue is followed by the suitless status screen sequence (mzm.menus.status_screen), then Chozodia room 0; the paralyzer pistol is covered by mzm.weapons.paralyzer_pistol. Not compared with an emulator.

### Cutscenes

[?] **`mzm.cutscenes.inventory-boundary` — source coverage.** The current classifier assigns 170 discovered routine(s) to this category (170 by source path). Category completeness has not been established.

[+] **`mzm.cutscenes.chozodia_escape` — Chozodia escape sequence (GM_CHOZODIA_ESCAPE).** The escape ship's EFFECT_CUTSCENE_ESCAPING_CHOZODIA freezes the room, fades it out and enters the sequence on the non-gameplay screen from the ROM bank; --chozodia-escape starts it directly. After a 16-step fade in it plays the five native stages with their lengths (312, 224, 304, 280 frames, then the letters up to 472): the mother ship door opening and the blue ship growing out of it and diving (double-sized affine objects, matrix 0), then climbing away; the mother ship heating up (sChozodiaEscapeShipHeatingUpPal / GroundHeatingUpPal rows, ever faster shaking), its white explosion growing as an ellipse (sHaze_PowerBomb_WindowValuesPointers stretched by 1.2, window 0 with brightness up inside); the crater with the decaying mother ship, its explosions and the flash fading by alpha then to black; Zebes with the ship far away, then flying past the camera with the escape music; Samus in her ship turning grey (ApplyMonochromeToPalette of sChozodiaEscapeMissionAccomplishedPal) with the English MISSION ACCOMPLISHED letters revealed one every four frames, and the fade out. Its five sounds, the music fade and the escape music play. Before the sequence, the current slot receives the English completion bit for its difficulty and a separate versioned, checksummed options file receives the ending gallery bit and difficulty clear; first-clear and first-hard transient flags feed the later pop-ups. A bank-backed test checks the fades, stage lengths, sounds, explosion window, grey palette and letters; captures of every stage and one from the escape ship in chozodia_092 show the sequence. It is entered through ColorFadingProcess_ChozodiaEscape: the screen cut to black at once, then the ending save (unit-tested, captured in chozodia_092). The Time Attack is ported (mzm_time_attack.c): TimeAttackCheckSaveFileValidity (the first four bosses in order, each past sTimeAttackAntiCheatTimes), CheckUnlockTimeAttack's unlock value (the sums of the boss timers' fields shuffled with the region, sanity checks on the tanks and abilities), and for a Time Attack run TimeAttackCheckSetNewRecord: the Any% and 100% records with their 20-character password from TimeAttackGenerateSeed (88 seed bits; the native 62-byte array is written past its end and read back), its eight shuffles and TimeAttackRandomizeSeed, ENDING_FLAG_FIRST_TIME_ATTACK_CLEAR (the empty 0xFF record) and ENDING_FLAG_NEW_TIME_ATTACK_RECORD. The port was compiled against the decompilation's own time_attack.c with stub globals: identical results on 200 000 random cases (24 185 unlocks, 150 201 records); three of those vectors are a unit test.

Known divergences: The H-blank WIN0H writes are a per-line table read by the PPU (line y takes the value computed for line y - 1, line 0 its own), and the register keeps the last line's value once the H-blank stops. The runtime has no soft reset, so gDisableSoftReset's lock has nothing to lock. The ending text is English only. The Time Attack record is kept in the runtime's unlock file (version 2) rather than its own SRAM block. GM_CREDITS follows (mzm.cutscenes.credits). Not compared with an emulator.  
Next action: Compare the sequence with an emulator.
Evidence: in game: `chozodia_092`.

[+] **`mzm.cutscenes.credits` — Credits and ending screens (GM_CREDITS).** After the Chozodia escape, ChozodiaEscapeGetItemCountAndEndingNumber computes the ending from the maximums (sTankIncreaseAmount of the difficulty), the abilities, the difficulty and the in-game hours, and GM_CREDITS plays from the ROM bank with the credits music: the US sCredits lines scroll at 7/16 pixel a frame over the dark Chozo wall, each built from sCreditsCharactersGfx (white, blue and red rows, the two-row white names, the copyright lines) and written by the V-blank into BG0, or BG1 half a tile aside for an odd width; at the end line the wall fades in and the text out, the wall scrolls down to its bottom (513 frames), zooms into the Chozo drawing and fades to black (961 frames). The end screen then slides and blends Samus's poses (sEndingSamusPosingGfx_1 to _5), the last by ending (full suit for image 0, helmet off for 1, suitless otherwise, through the white palettes), with the drifting space background, and fades out. EndingImageInit then selects one of the eight native ending illustrations, loads the English text and the exact clear-time and collection-rate digits into OBJ VRAM, slides both background halves into place and reveals CLEAR TIME, the timer, YOUR RATE FOR COLLECTING ITEMS IS, the percentage and NEXT MISSION letter by letter on their native schedule. At 22.93 seconds it waits for A, B or START, fades the music over 256 frames and ends at frame 1664. A bank-backed test checks the ending numbers, the first credit line, stage lengths, final pose, image number, 03:04:05 and 100% graphics, letter promotion, permanent lines, input gate, music fade and exact ending frame. UnlockedOptionsInit then opens the native window over its blue panel; the English Gallery, Sound Test or Time Attack Records message is drawn eight characters per frame, A closes the panel at frame 128, and a first clear shows only Gallery. The new-record OAM badge is also supported. The first-clear, difficulty-clear and gallery-image state persists in a separate versioned, checksummed global options file, while the current slot persists its difficulty completion bit. The tests check the first-clear panel, its window geometry and rendered pixels, the other message priorities and the unlock file's checksum. A Time Attack run now produces ENDING_FLAG_FIRST_TIME_ATTACK_CLEAR and ENDING_FLAG_NEW_TIME_ATTACK_RECORD (mzm.cutscenes.chozodia_escape).

[+] **`mzm.cutscenes.getting_fully_powered` — Getting the fully powered suit (cutscene and suit upgrade).** The Ruins Test's despawn requests the cutscene: the room freezes and fades out; at the fade's end (ColorFadingProcess_GettingFullyPowered) the fight stage becomes STARTING_CUTSCENE, EVENT_STATUE_VARIA_SUIT_GRABBED is set (with EVENT_VARIA_SUIT_OBTAINED and EVENT_SKIPPED_VARIA_SUIT when the Varia Suit was skipped) and the Varia Suit is obtained. The cutscene runs from the ROM bank on the non-gameplay screen: Samus on BG2 and the backdrop on BG3 (pages 0x1E and 0x1C, 512-pixel-high maps), sGettingFullyPoweredSuitPal in both palettes, the ring and sparkles in OBJ VRAM; Samus brightened (BLDY to 12 by the sprite effect) outside window 1, which ends half a block below the ring; the ring rises at a pixel a frame with its palette cycled through OBJ row 10 every third frame, six sparkles spawn around it after random delays (sRandomNumberTable) and five rise from below the screen (delays read from the background graphics' bytes, as natively, including the first timer landing on the ring); BG2 and BG3 scroll down to Samus, window 1 is switched off, the whole screen brightens to white while the ring flies away faster, then the end after a second (MUSIC_GETTING_FULLY_POWERED_SUIT_CUTSCENE). The status screen then shows the items being enabled (mzm.menus.status_screen); back in the room (RoomLoad's PAUSE_SCREEN_FULLY_POWERED_SUIT_ITEMS return) the Gravity Suit is held back, Samus facing the foreground at (24.5, 31) blocks with pause disabled and the charge reset; the closing reflection shows the banner, whose removal starts the suit upgrade (InGameCutsceneUpgradingSuit, waiting for no power bomb): BG0 faded out and hidden, window 1 opened two pixels wide on Samus, grown vertically by 4 then horizontally by 7 pixels a frame with BG0's tile 0xC0 (palette 9) blended at 13/16 over the screen inside it and SOUND_ACQUIRING_GRAVITY, the Gravity Suit activated at stage 11 (Samus's graphics follow), the window shrunk back (InGameCutsceneCalculateSuitFlashOffset), BG0 restored, then RUINS_TEST_FIGHT_STAGE_SUIT_ANIM_ENDED, lastWallTouchedMidAir and gDisablePause cleared. Checked in chozodia_042 with the `--ruins-test-end` staging (the actor sent straight to RUINS_TEST_POSE_CHECK_GHOST_DISAPPEARING): the cutscene at frame 700 shows the ring, its sparkles and the brightened Samus; the return shows the fully powered suit and the banner at 392,496; after B the window grows from x 120-122, the purple flash (tileset 56's tile is solid colour 0x7C19) covers the screen at stage 13, the Gravity Suit is active (items 0x60) and the fight stage reaches 8, after which the pillar falls. The same in-game cutscene module plays IGC_GETTING_VARIA after the Varia Suit's status screen (brinstar_027: the window at x 118-120 growing, then shrinking at stage 14, the suit active).
