Known divergences: US version (the cutscene opens with a fade to black; the upgrade's stage 19 returns IGC_RESULT_NEXT_STAGE and stage 20 keeps running). COLOR_FADING_GETTING_FULLY_POWERED's white fade is stood in by the COLOR_FADING_CANCEL fade to black. At the cutscene's end the status screen's fully powered items sequence runs (mzm.menus.status_screen); without the ROM bank every obtained item is activated at once instead. The upgrade's BG0 alpha fade (stages 2 and 18) is kept as state but the room's BG0 keeps its own blend when drawn; the flash is an additive rectangle, stretched to the view's edges when window 1 reaches the GBA screen's, and the HUD is drawn above it. The upgrade is cleared on a room change. Not compared with an emulator.  
Next action: Port the white colour fade; compare with an emulator.
Evidence: in game: `chozodia_042`.

[+] **`mzm.cutscenes.samus_in_blue_ship` — Samus in the blue ship cutscene.** The escape ship's request freezes the room (no sprite update, as SUB_GAME_MODE_LOADING_ROOM) while the game fades out, then the cutscene runs on the non-gameplay screen from the ROM bank: sSamusInBlueShipPal in both palettes, the Samus graphics and tile table on BG3 (page 0x1F, priority 3), the control panel graphics in OBJ VRAM drawn by ProcessCutsceneOam through sSamusInBlueShipOam bound to BG3 with OBJ alpha blending. It fades in from black (32 steps after 4 frames), lights the panel after half a second (SOUND_BLUE_SHIP_TURNING_ON, then sSamusInBlueShipPanelTransparency every 3 frames), shakes BG3 a pixel either way every other frame with SOUND_BLUE_SHIP_POWERING_UP, and ends after 1.5 seconds; the game then fades back in as it was (gPauseScreenFlag PAUSE_SCREEN_PAUSE_OR_CUTSCENE). A bank-backed test follows the stages, sounds, fade, flicker and shake; captures in chozodia_092 show the cockpit with the panel lighting up. The game now fades out with COLOR_FADING_SAMUS_IN_BLUE_SHIP itself: ColorFadingProcess_BeforeBlueShip, the CANCEL fade's colours (colour set 0, COLOR_FADING_FUNCTION_2, to black) with one more stage stopping the room's animated palette (one frame longer than CANCEL, unit-tested). As natively (playRoomMusic FALSE) the room music is left playing. Checked again in chozodia_092 (event 74, energy 1299, --spawn 362 420, input 0:70,17:25,1:15): the fade, the cutscene, then the lift-off.

Native evidence: `mzm:c:src/color_fading.c:ColorFadingProcess_BeforeBlueShip`, `mzm:c:src/cutscenes/cutscene_utils.c:CutsceneFadeScreenToBlack`, `mzm:c:src/cutscenes/cutscene_utils.c:CutsceneReset`, `mzm:c:src/cutscenes/cutscene_utils.c:CutsceneSetBgcntPageData`, `mzm:c:src/cutscenes/cutscene_utils.c:CutsceneStartBackgroundFading`, `mzm:c:src/cutscenes/cutscene_utils.c:CutsceneTransferAndUpdateFade`, `mzm:c:src/cutscenes/cutscene_utils.c:CutsceneTransferFade`, `mzm:c:src/cutscenes/cutscene_utils.c:CutsceneUpdateFading`, `mzm:c:src/cutscenes/samus_in_blue_ship.c:SamusInBlueShipHandler`, `mzm:c:src/cutscenes/samus_in_blue_ship.c:SamusInBlueShipInit`, `mzm:c:src/cutscenes/samus_in_blue_ship.c:SamusInBlueShipPoweringUp`, `mzm:c:src/cutscenes/samus_in_blue_ship.c:SamusInBlueShipProcessOam`, `mzm:c:src/cutscenes/samus_in_blue_ship.c:SamusInBlueShipShakeScreen`, `mzm:c:src/cutscenes/samus_in_blue_ship.c:SamusInBlueShipUpdateControlPanel`, `mzm:c:src/menus/pause_screen.c:ProcessCutsceneOam`, `mzm:c:src/oam_id.c:UpdateCutsceneOamDataId`.

[+] **`mzm.cutscenes.story_text` — Story text cutscenes.** "A new game's gunship landing plays CUTSCENE_INTRO_TEXT on the native screen's PPU before the game starts (`--cutscene N` plays one before the game for diagnostics). CutsceneHandler skips the pre-cutscene fade that cannot start, runs CutsceneInit (blending, cleared graphics, backgrounds at NON_GAMEPLAY_START_BG_POS) and StoryTextCutsceneHandler: the message from sEnglishTextPointers_Story drawn with the native font into the BG0 graphics while hidden (eight characters per frame, line by line), the vertical offset centering the lines, the alpha blending fade in over the backdrop (CutsceneStartBackgroundEffect, interval 4) with SOUND_INTRO_TEXT_LANDING, the wait of two seconds then A or B, the fade out, and the ending's cleared palettes, graphics and OAM. Checked by capture: the ""Planet Zebes..."" monologue after a new game from the title, then the game, and the mothership monologue. ### Music and sound effects

[?] **`mzm.audio.inventory-boundary` — source coverage.** The current classifier assigns 134 discovered routine(s) to this category (134 by source path). Category completeness has not been established.

[+] **`mzm.audio.sound_engine` — Sound engine, music and room music.** The custom C engine is ported routine by routine with the assembly mixer: the sequencer commands, DirectSound channels with the native envelope, allocation and group-of-four mixing paths, the four PSG channels through their registers, reverb, fades and the sound queue. ROM data (songs, voice groups, samples) is read through its GBA addresses from a private sound bank; out-of-table ROM reads of GetNoteDelay and unk_1ccc are exported. Rooms export their musicTrack and the runtime plays it through CheckSetNewMusicTrack and CheckPlayTransitionMusicTrack; the gameplay modules request their sound effects through mzm_sfx (identifiers generated from the decompilation's Sound enum by fusion_sound_ids, src/tools/sound_ids.c), which the runtime forwards to the engine. InsertMusicAndQueueCurrent saves the channels of players 1, 3, 6 and 8 (unk_34ac), stops the others and plays the inserted music on player 0; its 0xB6 end runs ReplayQueuedMusic, which restores them (unk_35d0) and restarts the current music. BackupTrackData2SoundChannels / RetrieveTrackData2SoundChannels release and restore the channels of player 2; mzm_sfx carries PlayMusic, UpdateMusicPriority, FadeMusic, the insertion and the backup requests. With the extracted bank the item jingle plays 355 frames before Brinstar resumes (an engine-only check: captures have no sound). The pause lowers the music (DecreaseMusicVolume: the pause sound, the other players' channels saved, unk_3058 with gUnk_Audio0x50 on players 0 and 1, kept by ReplayQueuedMusic) and ResetMusicVolume restores it when the pause screen closes. DecreaseMusicVolume (the pause) plays SOUND_63 (0x63); the port once queued sound 63 (MUSIC_IMAGO_COCOON_BATTLE), which replaced the room's music after the pause. Rendering the extracted Brinstar music offline confirmed the fix: the per-frame spike of the output's second difference fell from about ten times its mean to the level of the FIFO's own steps; what remains (the envelopes applied once a frame) is already in the mixer's output. Under the stealth and alarm priorities, CheckSetNewMusicTrack (unk_378c) keeps the stealth or detected music for the stealth area tracks and only remembers them under the alarm, CheckPlayTransitionMusicTrack (unk_38d8) starts nothing under the alarm, and UpdateMusicAfterAlarmDisable fades back to the remembered stealth track or to the detected music; the audio test checks that the alarm priority holds the current track through a room change. Every entry of sSoundDataEntries plays through the engine with the extracted bank (a bank-backed test): 612 of the 708 are audible within four seconds, and the 95 silent ones are silent in the ROM itself (the empty song shared by the unused entries, songs whose tracks set VOL 0 such as SOUND_ATOMIC_CHARGE_SILENT, and MUSIC_33, 38 and 47 at VOL 6 or 7 with velocity 12, below the 8-bit mixer's resolution). Promoted to functional on the user's review of the in-game playback (captures carry no sound).

Native evidence: `mzm:asm:asm/audio_internal.s:InitTrack`, `mzm:asm:asm/audio_internal.s:ResetTrack`, `mzm:asm:asm/audio_internal.s:StopMusicOrSound`, `mzm:asm:asm/audio_internal.s:UpdateAudio`, Tests: [`tests/test_mzm_audio.c`](../tests/test_mzm_audio.c), [`tests/test_mzm_items.c`](../tests/test_mzm_items.c), [`tests/test_runtime_steep_slopes.c`](../tests/test_runtime_steep_slopes.c).
Dependencies: `mzm.transitions.doors_and_hatches`.  
Known divergences: Not compared with hardware or an emulator. MidiKey2Freq (BIOS) uses the high-level formula; the PSG and the output mixer are an emulation of the GBA sound hardware, resampled to 48 kHz with a zero-order hold of the FIFO that keeps the held sample across GBA frames (the port once reset it to zero at each frame's start, a 60 Hz buzz) and PSG steps that carry their fractional cycles; the sound device pulls the output (SDL's stream callback): each pull runs UpdateAudio and renders one GBA frame (280896 cycles) at a time at the 65.536 kHz PWM rate that MZM selects in SOUNDBIAS, and SDL's band-limited resampler converts it for the device; the game only changes the engine's state under the stream's lock, so a slow game frame can no longer starve the device (the earlier push from the game steps ran the queue dry and crackled), and the music keeps the hardware's 59.73 Hz frame clock independently of the game's speed; REG_VCOUNT reads as 160 during UpdateAudio. The room's track is chosen when the destination room is loaded (natively when the door is touched) and starts when the door fade ends; the Norfair and cutscene music cases (the gMusicTrackInfo.unk checks) are not ported. The 7-entry channel backups drop what would overflow them instead of writing past the array. A zero divisor in unk_1ccc is taken as a zero quotient.  
Next action: Trigger the sounds of the systems still to port as they arrive; compare a rendered track with an emulator capture.
Evidence: in game: `user playback review`.

[?] **`mzm.death.inventory-boundary` — source coverage.** The current classifier assigns 0 discovered routine(s) to this category. Category completeness has not been established.

[+] **`mzm.death.game_over` — Game over screen.** "When the hit kills Samus (SUB_GAME_MODE_DYING) the sprites, projectiles and HUD stop and vanish, the room fades to black in 8 frames (gMonochromeBgFading, COLOR_FADING_TO_BLACK) and its backgrounds switch off; at the dying animation's first flash the backdrop fades to white (COLOR_FADING_TO_WHITE), with Samus alone in front. When SamusDying's timer reaches 120 the runtime opens the game over screen on the native screen PPU: GameOverInit loads its palette, the file select icons, the background and text tile tables, the graphics and the English ""try again"" prompt from the ROM bank, stops the sounds and fades from white (BLDY 16 down to 2, then the window and darkening up to 6) before the game over music. The GAME OVER letters breathe (sGameOverTextGradientPaletteOffset every 20 frames). UP and DOWN choose between ""yes (return to game)"" and ""no (to title screen)"" with Samus's head, the text palettes and the WIN1 window following, with the menu sounds; A or START plays the start sound and the head's animation, then the screen darkens to black with the music fading. Yes loads the most recent save file (Samus in the save platform's tube) or restarts the game's start; no leads to the intro (GM_INTRO), then the title. Checked by capture in norfair_022 (death in the lava with --energy 2, the menu, both answers)."

The head is processed with ProcessMenuOam instead of ProcessComplexMenuOam (it is never rotated or scaled). The continue leaves one frame earlier (unk_75c04 runs in the same frame). Without a saved file the continue restarts the runtime's starting state (the room and equipment the runtime started with), not the native new-file start. The debug variant and the demo ending are not ported. Not compared with an emulator.  
Next action: Compare the death and the game over with an emulator.

[+] **`mzm.technical.non_gameplay_screens` — Non-gameplay screen helpers.** MzmScreen holds what the menus and cutscenes share: the GbaPpu they draw on, gOamData and gNextOamSlot, the *_NonGameplay background offsets and blending values, the EWRAM work area and gChangedInput / gButtonInput in GBA key bits. ProcessMenuOam runs the menu object animations from the ROM's struct OamArray and FrameData tables with all their pre-actions and writes the OAM parts at the object's position minus its bound background's offset, with its priority and object mode; ResetFreeOam, UpdateMenuOamDataId, ClearGfxRam and ApplySpecialBackgroundFadingColor (in, flash, out and the unnamed fade) are ported; MenuOamData records are read from their 16-byte ROM layout. ProcessComplexMenuOam is ported (mzm_complex_menu_oam_process): ProcessMenuOam with an object's rotationScaling sent through ProcessComplexOam (double-sized, matrix 0) with gCurrentOamRotation and gCurrentOamScaling; the game over screen draws its head with it (never rotated, so placed as ProcessMenuOam places it). A bank-backed test checks both cases. The helpers run on every captured non-gameplay screen (the title screen, the file select, the game over, the cutscenes).

Native evidence: `mzm:c:src/color_effects.c:ApplySpecialBackgroundFadingColor`, `mzm:c:src/init_helpers.c:ClearGfxRam`, `mzm:c:src/init_helpers.c:ResetFreeOam`, `mzm:c:src/menus/pause_screen.c:ProcessComplexMenuOam`, `mzm:c:src/menus/pause_screen.c:ProcessMenuOam`, `mzm:c:src/oam_id.c:UpdateMenuOamDataId`.  
Local implementation: [`src/runtime/mzm_screen.c`](../src/runtime/mzm_screen.c), [`src/runtime/mzm_game_over.c`](../src/runtime/mzm_game_over.c).

[+] **`mzm.technical.ppu_and_rom_bank` — GBA picture processing unit and ROM data bank.** The register bank keeps BG2/BG3 affine registers independent from the lower display registers. The renderer covers every display path selected by MZM: modes 0 to 2 text/affine backgrounds, all regular and affine object shapes, 1D/2D mapping, flips, double size, windows and object windows, alpha and brightness effects, mosaic, priority and forced blank. The intro, title, file select, pause/status, Game Over, credits, Tourian escape and Chozodia escape run through it and have tests and recorded captures. The native hash-gated private bank indexes 14,462 sized ELF objects and is generated by the C11 importer in the rebuild graph; mzm_rom resolves names and GBA addresses and implements the BIOS LZ77 and run-length streams. ProcessComplexOam and CalculateOamPart4 are shared by the affine escape scenes. The technical audit rejects any future use of unsupported bitmap modes.

Native evidence: `mzm:c:src/syscall_wrappers.c:CallLZ77UncompVram`, `mzm:c:src/syscall_wrappers.c:CallLZ77UncompWram`.  
Local implementation: [`include/fusion/assets/mzm.h`](../include/fusion/assets/mzm.h), [`src/assets/mzm/rom_bank.c`](../src/assets/mzm/rom_bank.c), [`src/runtime/gba_ppu.c`](../src/runtime/gba_ppu.c), [`src/runtime/mzm_rom.c`](../src/runtime/mzm_rom.c), [`src/tools/fusion_rebuild.c`](../src/tools/fusion_rebuild.c), [`src/runtime/mzm_screen.c`](../src/runtime/mzm_screen.c).  
Tests: [`tests/test_fusion_assets_mzm.c`](../tests/test_fusion_assets_mzm.c), [`tests/test_native_golden.c`](../tests/test_native_golden.c), [`tests/test_mzm_hud.c`](../tests/test_mzm_hud.c), [`tests/test_mzm_screens.c`](../tests/test_mzm_screens.c), [`tests/test_source_contracts.c`](../tests/test_source_contracts.c).
Known divergences: The bitmap modes 3 to 5 are not rendered, but a source audit proves the pinned MZM program never selects them. Registers are sampled by the host renderer rather than hardware dots; the Chozodia escape's only required H-blank rewrite is represented by its per-line WIN0H table. Affine reference latching, the object cycle limit and affine-object mosaic are not hardware-cycle emulated. The ROM bank contains the whole verified ROM privately and is never committed.  
Next action: Compare representative affine and blended scenes with an emulator.
Evidence: in game: `brinstar_000`, `tourian_000`, `chozodia_092`.

[+] **`mzm.technical.save_file` — Save file.** Three files, file_a.sav to file_c.sav in --save-dir (else SDL's preference folder). YES on a save platform's prompt writes the current file through a temporary file renamed over it. The file select screen reads their info (SramWrite_FileInfo: existence, area, energy, missiles, suit, timer, completed flags, difficulty) and continues one: its room is loaded and its state restored with Samus in SPOSE_SAVING_LOADING_GAME and gIsLoadingFile set for the room's first sprite update, so the save platform releases her; --load does the same at start with the most recent file. The in-game timer runs each game frame as InGameTimerUpdate does (frames, seconds, minutes, hours, capped at 99:59:59 with gMaxInGameTimerFlag). Truncated, corrupted, foreign or newer files are rejected. The file select screen's copy and erase menus copy a save file over another or delete it. The game over's continue loads the most recent file the same way, or restarts the game's start without one. Version 3 adds gInGameTimerAtBosses (Kraid, Mother Brain, the Ruins Test and Mecha Ridley record the in-game timer at their death, as natively). Checked in game through the save platform of brinstar_033 (the prompt, the save, then the reloaded file).

Native evidence: `mzm:c:src/in_game_timer.c:InGameTimerUpdate`, `mzm:c:src/save_file.c:SramLoadFile`, `mzm:c:src/save_file.c:SramSaveFile`, `mzm:c:src/save_file.c:SramWrite_FileInfo`, `mzm:c:src/save_file.c:SramWrite_ToEwram`.  
Local implementation: [`src/runtime/mzm_save.c`](../src/runtime/mzm_save.c), [`src/runtime/room_runtime.c`](../src/runtime/room_runtime.c).

Known divergences: The runtime writes its own versioned little-endian files (header, payload, CRC-32; version 3 with gInGameTimerAtBosses, versions 1 and 2 still read) instead of the GBA SRAM layout with its copies and checksums; a file keeps the room alias, Samus's position and facing, the equipment, the events, the minimap progress, the collected items, the opened hatches, the never-reform blocks, the area, the difficulty, the in-game timer, the timers at the four bosses and the completed game flags, but not the music or the rest of gSamusData. gMostRecentSaveFile is the newest of the three files. The save completes in one frame instead of the multi-frame SramSaveFile stages. The difficulty is kept and applied: the contact damage (halved on easy, doubled on hard), the tank increases, the stun threshold (CONVERT_SECONDS(.24f) on hard) and the bosses' difficulty branches.  
Next action: Compare the files' timer and contents with an emulator.
Evidence: in game: `brinstar_033`.

### Startup and initialization

[?] **`aos.startup.inventory-boundary` — source coverage.** The current classifier assigns 5 discovered routine(s) to this category (2 by native name, 3 by exclusive referrers). Category completeness has not been established.

[~] **`aos.saves.portable_slots` — Portable frontend save slots.** Checksummed atomic host files are connected to the native menu's typed create, load, copy, delete and name requests. A new file records sub_08008ED0's native area 0 room 0 Soma position (120, 141); the selected file launches the room runtime there after the introduction. Save-room interaction restores HP and updates that file. Source-derived and unit-tested, not emulator-compared. Schema v3 adds the experience and gold, bounded by the native 99999999 experience cap; the runtime rebuilds the level from the saved experience and stores both at save points. Schema v4 adds the equipped weapon, armor and accessory and v5 the item, weapon and armor inventory counts (0-9); older files take sub_08014548's new-game set (weapon 0 and armor 0, one of each). Schema v6 adds the soul inventories as their native bytes, the enabled abilities and totalNbrSoulsCollected. Schema v7 adds the equipped red, blue and yellow souls and currentMP (older files: none equipped, full MP).

Native evidence: `aos:asm:asm/code/code_08050A3C.s:Object1CCreate`, `aos:asm:asm/code/code_08050A3C.s:Object1CUpdate`, `aos:asm:asm/code/code_08060B98.s:sub_08068AD4`, `aos:c:src/code_08012744.c:SaveData_ClearSlotInSram`, `aos:c:src/code_08012744.c:SaveData_LoadSlotFromSram`, `aos:c:src/code_08012744.c:SaveData_SaveSlotToSram`, `aos:c:src/code_08012744.c:sub_080127F0`.

Known divergences: The ROM-free v7 text schema is intentionally not the native six-slot 0x47C-byte SRAM layout, although v1 to v6 host files remain readable. Only three regular files, encoded name, character, challenge flags, room, spawn, HP, movement mask, play time, experience, gold, equipment, inventory counts, soul inventories, enabled abilities, the soul total, the equipped souls and current MP are represented (the level and its stats are rebuilt from the experience); quicksaves, completed-file metadata, inventory, equipment, souls, event/map state, native healing animation, save question and audio remain absent.  
Next action: Add the remaining progression and completed-file fields, then reconstruct quicksave and native SRAM serialization behavior.

[~] **`aos.startup.mode_dispatch` — Aria game mode dispatcher.** The host-neutral handler table connects Reset, both startup logos, all currently reconstructed title stages, MAIN_MENU, INTRO_CUTSCENE and IN_GAME. The SDL title host persists regular-file menu operations, runs the introduction for new files and launches gameplay only after its native IN_GAME handoff. Source-derived and unit-tested, not emulator-compared.

### Title screen

[?] **`aos.title.inventory-boundary` — source coverage.** The current classifier assigns 28 discovered routine(s) to this category (7 by native name, 21 by exclusive referrers). Category completeness has not been established.

[~] **`aos.title.input_stage8` — Aria title and main-menu controllers.** The lifecycle connects title stages 0-9 to all three native main-menu operation controllers. PLAY retains its file/quicksave and special-mode topology; COPY and DELETE retain their cursor and confirmation paths. Native name entry retains wrapped editing and its special codes. The verified-ROM renderer reconstructs the base, PLAY, COPY, DELETE and name-entry background planes in all three languages. aos_main_menu_prompt_id selects the localized prompt (0x354-0x356 base, 0x35B/0x35C PLAY, 0x358/0x359/0x379 COPY, 0x35A/0x377 DELETE) as sub_08004928, sub_08005908 and the copy/delete controllers do; the frontend reads it from the ROM text table (control 6 new line, 10 end) and draws it with the native proportional font. All ten prompts parse in the three languages of the verified ROM (optional local test). The SDL launcher consumes typed regular-file requests, runs the opening controller for new files and waits for its IN_GAME handoff. Source-derived, unit-tested and ROM-smoke-tested; not emulator-compared.

### Menus and options

[?] **`aos.menus.inventory-boundary` — source coverage.** The current classifier assigns 33 discovered routine(s) to this category (10 by native name, 23 by exclusive referrers). Category completeness has not been established.

[~] **`aos.menus.menu_bg0` — Aria main-menu BG0 layer and map writers.** BG0 of GameModeMainMenuUpdate's setup: character base 2 with the text engine's tiles 0x000-0x0FF and the English, French or German bank (0x0827B200, 0x08277984, 0x0826C848) from tile 0x100 through sub_0803FD9C, palette 12 from 0x081183F4 and palette 13 from row 3 of 0x080E5C20 through sub_0803C918, and the setup loop mapping text tiles 0x20-0x9F from map cell (2, 15). sub_08046DD4 writes consecutive entries and sub_08046E5C right-aligned digits with blank leading zeros (base + 0xD), padding, overflow and the negative case. The frontend now shows the menu prompt and YES/NO through this BG0 with the native palette. With the verified ROM the three banks hold opaque digit tiles 0x100-0x109 and an offscreen render of the copy confirmation shows the native prompt, Yes and No at their cells (local checks). Source-derived and unit-tested, not emulator-compared.

Native evidence: `aos:asm:asm/code/code_08040A38.s:sub_08046DD4`, `aos:asm:asm/code/code_08040A38.s:sub_08046E5C`, `aos:c:src/code/code_08039340.c:sub_0803C8B0`, `aos:c:src/code/code_08039340.c:sub_0803C918`, `aos:c:src/code/code_08039340.c:sub_0803FD9C`.

Dependencies: `aos.menus.text_window`, `aos.title.input_stage8`.  
Known divergences: The palette loaders write the destination rows directly; the EWRAM palette buffer and its transfer flags (unk_123DC, unk_123E1) are not modelled, and an out-of-range source row copies nothing instead of a negative DMA. Only the main menu's BG0 is modelled (unscrolled, map writes outside the 32x32 map dropped). The file panel (sub_08006D9C) that uses the number writer is not ported, because the portable saves lack its level, item, gold and area records.  
Next action: Extend the portable save with the file panel's records, then port sub_08006D9C and sub_08007204 onto this BG0.

[~] **`aos.menus.text_window` — Aria text cursors and windows.** Both text cursors of gEwramData + 0x115A0 (context A, 12-pixel rows up to 20) and + 0x115B2 (context B, 8-pixel rows up to 31) are ported from the assembly: cell and pixel cursors (with the native 12-pixel row of sub_0804090C in context B), windows (right edge x + w - 2, bottom y + h - 1, clamped after the store), newline and glyph advance with the single 6-pixel carry, right-edge and bottom wrapping, positioned puts, window clears (left column to one past the right) and the print loops that stop at the first control. The main menu prints its prompt at (0, 1) of window (0, 1, 0x25, 3) and the copy/delete YES/NO entries at (9, 2) and (0x14, 2) for COPY or (0xA, 2) and (0x15, 2) for DELETE; the frontend shows text tiles 0x20-0x9F through the native BG0 map from screen tile (2, 15). With the verified ROM all ten menu texts fit their row in the three languages (optional local test). Source-derived and unit-tested, not emulator-compared.

Native evidence: `aos:c:src/code/code_08039340.c:sub_0804066C`, `aos:c:src/code/code_08039340.c:sub_080406E4`, `aos:c:src/code/code_08039340.c:sub_08040748`, `aos:c:src/code/code_08039340.c:sub_08040814`, `aos:c:src/code/code_08039340.c:sub_08040898`, `aos:c:src/code/code_08039340.c:sub_0804090C`, `aos:c:src/code/code_08039340.c:sub_08040970`, `aos:c:src/code/code_08039340.c:sub_08040A38`, `aos:c:src/code/code_08039340.c:sub_08040AB8`, `aos:c:src/code/code_08039340.c:sub_08040BDC`, `aos:c:src/code/code_08039340.c:sub_08040C60`, `aos:c:src/code/code_08039340.c:sub_08040E10`, `aos:c:src/code/code_08039340.c:sub_08040FE0`, `aos:c:src/code/code_08039340.c:sub_08041204`, `aos:c:src/code/code_08039340.c:sub_08041318`, `aos:c:src/code/code_08039340.c:sub_08041338`.

Context B's font (metrics 0x080E2EC0) is not loaded, so only context A is drawn. The native print loop is unbounded; the port stops at the entry's end.

[~] **`aos.ui.hud` — Aria in-game HUD bars.** sub_0800DA50 sets BG0 (map base 28, scrolled 6 pixels up), the tiles of 0x0827B208 at tile 0x200 and palette rows 13-14 from 0x0820C428 (exported together as metadata/hud.bin), lays the frame of sub_0800DB78 (sUnk_080E0F74's two blocks) and draws both bars with sub_0800F138: eight tiles from cell (4, 1) for HP (tiles 0x200-0x208) and (4, 2) for MP (0x210-0x218), filled value * 100 / max * 64 / 100 pixels and at least one when positive. sub_0800DC70 moves the shown HP, MP and maxima two steps a frame toward the real values and redraws a bar that moved. ### Player state machine

[?] **`aos.player.inventory-boundary` — source coverage.** The current classifier assigns 0 discovered routine(s) to this category. Category completeness has not been established.

[~] **`aos.items.money_pickups` — Aria money, item and MP heart pickups.** sub_08043F70 creates a money pickup from the seven-record table 0x085250E4 (1, 10, 50, 100, 500, 1000 and 2000 gold, exported as metadata/money.tsv), lifted 8 pixels for the four small variants. sub_08043DD0 makes it fall at 0x2000 per frame up to 0x40000, land on the floor probe of sub_08001D94 (8 pixels lower for money), rest 300 frames and blink 64 frames before it is removed. The money case of sub_080441F8 adds the value to the gold, capped at 999999. The runtime spawns one where an enemy's drop roll gives sub_08043F70, at the enemy's (x, y - 8), collects it when Soma's hurtbox meets its 16 x 16 box (0x1010F8F8), and saves the gold at save points. Source-derived and unit-tested, not emulator-compared. sub_08044054 decodes the global item numbers (1-32 items, 33-91 weapons, 92-136 armor and accessories) into pickups that fall like money; cases 2-4 of sub_080441F8 count them in the item, weapon or armor inventory up to 9 and leave them on the ground above 8, with the refusal sound behind a 0x20-frame cooldown. The runtime spawns an item pickup where an enemy's drop roll gives an item and saves the inventories (schema v5). sub_08043C10 creates the MP hearts of enemy drops (popping 2 pixels per frame upward): a big heart (15 %) falls like money through sub_08043DD0, a small one (3 %) rises, then drifts down 0.25 per frame swaying along half a sine until the floor probe 4 pixels below meets ground (its depth subtracted from the raw 16.16 Y, as natively), rests 200 frames and blinks 64; both use the 8 x 8 box 0x0808FCFC, and case 0 of sub_080441F8 (sub_08043BB0) restores that share of the maximum MP, at least 1, capped. The sprites are exported from the shared sheet 0x081C15F4 and frames 0x0820ED60: sub_0803B924's attribute bits select the OBJ palette row, filled by sub_0800C5D8 from bank sUnk_084F0DD8[row] of 0x082099FC, and + 0x65 selects a still frame. Small coins loop sequence 21 (sub_0803F2C8) on rows 6, 7, 9, 8, bags show frame 33 on rows 6-8, hearts frames 40 and 39 on row 7 and souls loop sequence 6 on the rows of 0x080E1CD0; the runtime draws them from creation, the lifted coins 8 pixels up. A local capture showed coins, a bag and both hearts. Item pickups show the icon of their record (+ 0x2, exported as metadata/item_icons.tsv): icon n is block (n - 1) % 64 of sheet sUnk_084F1060[(n - 1) / 64] (0x081C5E00, 0x081C7E04, 0x081C9E08), drawn by the frames of 0x080E10F0 at (-8, -16) on OBJ row 6 (every record's + 0x3), which sub_0800C5D8 fills with bank 2 of 0x082099FC; the 132 icons the records use are exported as Icon/i<n>. A local capture showed a potion, a sword, an armor and a ring on the floor.

Native evidence: `aos:asm:asm/code/code_08040A38.s:sub_08043BB0`, `aos:asm:asm/code/code_08040A38.s:sub_08043C10`, `aos:asm:asm/code/code_08040A38.s:sub_08043CE4`, `aos:asm:asm/code/code_08040A38.s:sub_08043DD0`, `aos:asm:asm/code/code_08040A38.s:sub_08043F70`, `aos:asm:asm/code/code_08040A38.s:sub_08044054`, `aos:asm:asm/code/code_08040A38.s:sub_080441F8`, `aos:c:src/code_08011DD0.c:sub_0801232C`, `aos:c:src/code_08011DD0.c:sub_080124F4`.  
Local implementation: [`src/runtime/aos_pickup.c`](../src/runtime/aos_pickup.c), [`src/runtime/aria_runtime.c`](../src/runtime/aria_runtime.c), [`src/assets/aos/objects.c`](../src/assets/aos/objects.c).  
Tests: [`tests/test_aos_pickup.c`](../tests/test_aos_pickup.c).
Dependencies: `aos.progression.experience`.  
Known divergences: The icon VRAM slots of sub_0801232C (eight shared slots at 0x13800) are not modelled, so any number of items show their icons; the blinking of + 0x0F is shown as every other frame, an approximation. sub_08043DD0 is ported for pickup types up to 4 only (the floating soul path and the palette cycles of states 0x63-0x65 are not), and sub_080441F8 for its money and item cases (the placed-item flags of + 0x30, the 0x42C flags, the item-name messages of 0x08506734 and the recent-item lists of sub_0804AE3C are not); its message (sub_0800EF98) is reported and its sound 0xB6 not played. The player's hurtbox stands for the player side of the type-0xB collision. Pickups live in the room and vanish on a room change. sub_08044054 is ported for its 0xFF numbering and categories 2-4 (its money redirect and the icon setup of sub_0801232C / sub_080124F4 are not); item sounds 0xB5 / 0xB7 and the refusal sound 0x13B are reported, not played.  
Next action: Trace the + 0x0F blink and the icon slot limit, then the item-name and soul messages.

[~] **`aos.player.soma_controller` — Soma movement and state controller.** Movement and the currently traced ability states are ported; the full native player state machine is not complete.

Native evidence: `aos:asm:asm/code/code_08014548.s:sub_0801B0D8`.  
Local implementation: [`src/runtime/aos_soma.c`](../src/runtime/aos_soma.c).  
Tests: [`tests/test_aos_soma.c`](../tests/test_aos_soma.c).
Next action: Port the remaining native Soma states.

[~] **`aos.progression.equipment_stats` — Aria equipment and derived ATK and DEF.** sub_0804ACD4 sums the weapon record (0x08505D3C, 59 x 28 bytes) and the armor and accessory records (0x085063B0, 45 x 20 bytes): ATK, DEF, the signed STR, CON, INT, LCK and, for armor and accessory only, the ORed resistances; sub_0804AD9C adds the base stats and sub_08021FA4 / sub_08021C44 give currentATK = STR + equipment ATK and currentDEF = CON / 2 + equipment DEF, at least 0. The object export writes both tables as metadata/equipment.tsv. Portable saves (schema v4) keep the weapon, armor and accessory, older files taking sub_08014548's new-game set (weapon 0, armor 0, no accessory); the runtime equips them, derives ATK and DEF at load and after every level up, and a new file starts at ATK 15 and DEF 11 (local runtime check; level 5 gives 19 and 13). --atk and --def remain overrides. Source-derived and unit-tested, not emulator-compared.

Native evidence: `aos:asm:asm/code/code_08040A38.s:sub_0804ACD4`, `aos:asm:asm/code/code_08040A38.s:sub_0804AD9C`, `aos:asm:asm/code/code_08040A38.s:sub_0804B314`, `aos:c:src/code/code_080211F0.c:sub_08021C44`, `aos:c:src/code/code_080211F0.c:sub_08021F64`, `aos:c:src/code/code_080211F0.c:sub_08021F84`, `aos:c:src/code/code_080211F0.c:sub_08021FA4`.  
Local implementation: [`src/runtime/aos_stats.c`](../src/runtime/aos_stats.c), [`src/runtime/aria_runtime.c`](../src/runtime/aria_runtime.c), [`src/runtime/aos_save.c`](../src/runtime/aos_save.c), [`src/assets/aos/objects.c`](../src/assets/aos/objects.c).  
Tests: [`tests/test_aos_stats.c`](../tests/test_aos_stats.c), [`tests/test_aos_save.c`](../tests/test_aos_save.c).
Dependencies: `aos.progression.experience`, `aos.saves.portable_slots`.  
Known divergences: Only the three equipped records are summed (sub_0804ACD4's preview override, used by the equipment menu, is not modelled) and an index past its table counts as nothing. There is no inventory or equipment menu yet, so equipment changes only through the file or --weapon; armor and accessories have no other effect than their records (no accessory-specific behaviour such as MP regeneration). The resistance word is computed but not yet applied to damage, and the effective INT and LCK are not used.  
Next action: Port the inventories and the equipment menu, apply the resistances and the accessory effects, then the souls.

[~] **`aos.progression.experience` — Aria levels, experience and enemy drop rolls.** sub_08033DA4 starting stats for Soma and Julius, sub_08033DFC's x(x + 1)(3x + 8) thresholds, sub_08033CAC's level loop to 99 with its four gain tables (every fourth level gains a single INT point), sub_08033E10, sub_08033E38 and sub_08033E6C. The experience part of sub_080683BC grants an enemy record's u16 at 0x10 once per entity (its + 0x3E guard), marks the 0x133A0 bestiary bit, applies the 0x133 / 256 bonus flag and the 99999999 cap, and levels up; Julius and the mode bits skip it. Its drop part rolls, with the same random draws in the same order, the soul (rarity * 8 - (LCK / 16 - 32), at least 16; thresholds 3, 6 unowned, 7 with the 0xA1 flag, +8 with accessory 0x28), then unless the ground type blocks it the rare (below 0x40, 0x60 with 0x27) or common item (rate * 4 - (LCK / 16 - 16); 4, 8 with 0x27) with its item bitset, then for records with an item the sub_08043F70 pickup (bits 3-12 <= 7, 0x3F and +3 with 0x2B) or the sub_08043C10 pickup (bits 4-13 <= 7, 0x3F with 0x1D). The enemy-stats export (schema v3) now carries the experience and drop fields and the runtime rewards each enemy it reports killed, raising maximum HP on a level up and drawing its drop in the native order; a v3 save with 700 experience starts at level 5 with 368 maximum HP (local runtime check). Source-derived and unit-tested, not emulator-compared.

Native evidence: `aos:asm:asm/code/code_08060B98.s:sub_080683BC`, `aos:c:src/code_08033CAC.c:sub_08033CAC`, `aos:c:src/code_08033CAC.c:sub_08033DA4`, `aos:c:src/code_08033CAC.c:sub_08033DFC`, `aos:c:src/code_08033CAC.c:sub_08033E10`, `aos:c:src/code_08033CAC.c:sub_08033E38`, `aos:c:src/code_08033CAC.c:sub_08033E6C`.  
Local implementation: [`src/runtime/aos_stats.c`](../src/runtime/aos_stats.c), [`src/runtime/aria_runtime.c`](../src/runtime/aria_runtime.c), [`src/runtime/aos_save.c`](../src/runtime/aos_save.c), [`src/assets/aos/objects.c`](../src/assets/aos/objects.c).  
Tests: [`tests/test_aos_stats.c`](../tests/test_aos_stats.c), [`tests/test_aos_save.c`](../tests/test_aos_save.c).
Dependencies: `aos.saves.portable_slots`, `aos.player.soma_controller`.  
Known divergences: sub_080683BC is ported for its experience reward and its drop decision, but the pickups it creates (sub_0804459C souls, sub_08044054 items, sub_08043F70 and sub_08043C10, entities 0x49-0xCF) are not, so a drop is only reported; the two pickups are named by routine because their contents are not traced. The runtime has no equipment or soul inventory yet: it passes the equipped accessory, the effective LCK and whether the soul is held, but the 0xA1 flag as clear, and gEwramData + 0x4CC as 0. The item bitsets (0x133B0, 0x133C0) and the bestiary are not saved. The level-up display sub_080455BC, the equipment refresh sub_0804AD9C and the walking-experience accessory path of sub_0801B0D8 are not ported. Levels raise Soma's maximum HP but MP, STR, CON, INT and LCK are not yet used by combat. sub_08045F9C (a candle's drop) creates the sub_08043C10 pickup unless MP is full, which identifies it as the MP heart (ported with aos.items.money_pickups).  
Next action: Port the pickup entities (souls, items and the two pickups) with their collection, then the soul and item inventories, equipment and the derived stats of sub_0804AD9C.

[~] **`aos.souls.inventory_pickups` — Aria soul inventory and soul pickups.** The soul inventories keep native nibble counts in two groups per colour (red 2 x 28, blue 2 x 13, yellow 2 x 18 bytes) and a single ability array that both groups read, so an ability soul counts double as natively; SoulInventory_AddAmountToGroupTotal clamps a group to 0-9 and AddAmountToSoulTotal first takes the amount back from group 1 when it holds some and the total would pass 9. sub_08032ADC enables an ability bit and sub_08032DBC counts collected souls up to 999. sub_0804459C creates a dropped soul heading away from a random point above Soma (one draw) at speed 0x30000; sub_080446D4 turns it toward Soma's y - 16 (sub_0802D408 over ArcTan2, step + 0x2A per frame up to 0x4000), speeds it up by 0x1800 up to 0x80000, makes the trail's two random draws on even timer values and lets it live 200 frames plus 64 blinking. Cases 5-8 of sub_080441F8 add it, enabling an ability soul's ability when new. The runtime creates souls from enemy drop rolls (now with the equipped accessory, effective LCK and held-soul test), runs move, collision and trail in that order, and saves the souls (schema v6). Source-derived and unit-tested, not emulator-compared.

Native evidence: `aos:asm:asm/code/code_080211F0.s:sub_0802D344`, `aos:asm:asm/code/code_080211F0.s:sub_0802D408`, `aos:asm:asm/code/code_08040A38.s:sub_0804459C`, `aos:asm:asm/code/code_08040A38.s:sub_080446D4`, `aos:c:src/code_08032444.c:SoulInventory_AddAmountToGroupTotal`, `aos:c:src/code_08032444.c:SoulInventory_AddAmountToSoulTotal`, `aos:c:src/code_08032444.c:SoulInventory_GetSoulTotal`, `aos:c:src/code_08032444.c:sub_08032ADC`, `aos:c:src/code_08032444.c:sub_08032DBC`.  
Local implementation: [`src/runtime/aos_stats.c`](../src/runtime/aos_stats.c), [`src/runtime/aos_pickup.c`](../src/runtime/aos_pickup.c), [`src/runtime/aria_runtime.c`](../src/runtime/aria_runtime.c), [`src/runtime/aos_save.c`](../src/runtime/aos_save.c).  
Tests: [`tests/test_aos_pickup.c`](../tests/test_aos_pickup.c), [`tests/test_aos_save.c`](../tests/test_aos_save.c).
Dependencies: `aos.items.money_pickups`, `aos.progression.experience`, `aos.saves.portable_slots`.  
Known divergences: Souls are collected and counted but not yet usable: equipping souls, their attacks, MP costs and the ability effects are not ported. The soul pickup is not drawn (sprite sequence of sub_08032B14 and palette cycle 6 of the shared resources), its trail spark sub_08045B44 is not created (only its two random draws are made; any draws the spark itself makes are not), and the new-soul and held-soul messages (sub_08045C34, sub_08045BC8, sub_0800E708), sub_08049E64 and sound 0xBC are reported, not played. Soul pickups created with a non-zero last argument (state 0x63, the palette-cycle states) are not ported.  
Next action: Port the soul equipment (equippedRedSoul, Blue, Yellow) and the souls' use: red soul attacks with MP, blue and yellow effects, then the ability souls' movement upgrades.

[~] **`aos.souls.red_soul_use` — Aria red soul casting and MP.** Soma's red soul use of sub_0801B0D8 (_0801C298, Up held and a new attack press with no 0x540 flag) runs sub_08019478: with no skill entity alive the last used soul follows the equipped one; the cast needs that soul's record (0x080E1598, exported as metadata/red_souls.tsv: 56 records, one per red soul count of the 0x1C-byte inventory groups) to have a skill routine, at least its MP cost (Soma may spend his last points, unlike Julius), fewer active entities than the last used record's maximum (sub_0802D2BC) and a free slot of eight (sub_0802D208). It spends the MP, plays the cast animation of sub_0802D1D8 (0x080E1918: ground 22/78/79/80, air 49/78/79/80) with the pose's hop (vy -3.5, gravity -0x1000), lunge (3 px per frame) or recoil byte, sets flag 0x100 and enters state 2, which steers in the air, brakes on the ground and ends with the animation. The weapon attack that follows is refused by the 0x100 flag. sub_08021994 regenerates one MP every 64 ticks of Soma's + 0x22 counter (16 with accessory 0x29, full with 0x2C). SkillBatUse places its entity at Soma's facing and (0, -26) (sub_08031EA4), counts itself on its first update, plays sequence 0 of its resource (0x081CD8D0 / 0x0820B0B0 / 0x082311F8, exported as Enemy/skill_bat with its per-frame attack boxes) and ends with it, freeing its slot. The ring's frame boxes are type-5 attacks: after the weapon, each enemy tests them with the recent-hit slot and cooldown of type 5 (0x080E33F0), and sub_0806E218 deals sub_08021530(5) = (STR * power / 10 + INT / 4 + power * 6) / 16 of the last used soul's record (sub_0802D244; the bat's power is 60, 26 at level 1) through the existing damage formula with the record's elements. Portable saves keep the equipped souls and MP (schema v7). SkillSkullArcherUse places its bow at Soma's facing and (-12, -24), counts itself on its first update and plays sequence 0 of its resource (0x081D510C / 0x0820B2EC / 0x08233BCC, exported as Enemy/skill_archer); on animation frame 14 it takes a free slot (sub_08023530) for the arrow sub_08028BC4 at its own position, and it ends with its animation or outside the screen margins 4 (sub_0806D128). The arrow (still frame 1) flies 6 pixels a frame with the type-5 box (8, -1, 2, 2) of 0x0202FF08; once an enemy took it (sub_0802DB84, reported back through the soul attack) it stops, blinks 16 frames and is deleted, as it is outside the margins 2. The Skeleton soul's routine (shared by six souls) reads the record of 0x084F12C8 its parameter selects (exported as metadata/skill_throws.tsv); for parameter 3 it places the bone (resource 7 of 0x080E13BC, exported as Enemy/skill_bone) at Soma's facing and (8, -26), throws it at (0.5, -4) pixels a frame (mirrored facing left) with gravity 0x2000 capped at 8 pixels a frame (sub_0806D430, sub_0806D460), turns it 0x0B00 a frame (negated facing left; sub_0803E058's matrix turns it clockwise), keeps the type-5 box (-8, -8, 16, 16) of 0x1010F8F8 and deletes it the frame after its moved position leaves the screen margins 4 (+ 0x59 bit 2 is set by sub_08019478). Its hit callback sub_0802DACC only marks the hit, so the bone passes through. SkillMermanUse (resource 0 of 0x080E13BC, exported as Enemy/skill_shared) plays sequence 0 at Soma's facing and (0, -26), counts itself and creates five sub_08026DB8 drops from (6, -26) through sub_08023530: drop k flies k + 1 pixels a frame slowed by its 128th, looping sequence 6, with the type-5 box (-8, -8, 16, 16) for 16 frames; then its attack is disabled and it falls with gravity 0x2000 (sub_0806D3D8, X moved twice on that frame) until it leaves the screen margins 3. SkillWingedSkeletonUse shows still frame 0 of resource 0 at Soma's facing and (8, -26) (sub_08031EA4 copies whole pixels only, now for every skill), throws it 4 pixels a frame ahead and 1.125 up with gravity 0x2000, turning 0x100 a frame from -0x800 while its angle is at most 0x3FFF (mirrored facing left), and keeps the point 16 pixels along it (turned by 0x8000 facing left); when the cell at its whole position plus that point is solid and not special (sub_08001A00, sub_08001F3C) it sticks and blinks 32 frames, and outside the screen margins 4 it goes. Its child sub_08025F70 carries the type-5 box (-8, -8, 16, 16) at that point until the spear's state 3. A local runtime run with soul 1 equipped drew the spear leaving Soma's hand and turning down. SkillDurgaUse shows still frame 2 of resource 24 (exported as Enemy/skill_blades) at Soma's facing and (8, -26), flies 4 pixels a frame ahead (sub_0806D3D8), attacks with its still frame's box (frame record + 8 through sub_08042848, exported as a static_attack row: (-16, -4, 32, 8)) and is deleted in the update it leaves the screen margins 3. A local runtime run with soul 13 equipped drew the flying sword. SkillHarpyUse, placed at Soma's facing and (20, -26), counts itself and creates two sub_0802CAC0 daggers (sub_08023530 marks each child's + 0x59 bit 2 and counts it), then is deleted on its next update: dagger 0 faces right and dagger 1 left whatever Soma's facing, 5 pixels a frame, still frame 1 of resource 24 with the type-5 box (-1, -1, 2, 2) of 0x0202FFFF; once its hit callback sub_0802D9B4 ran it goes, against a wall 6 pixels ahead (sub_08002058 or sub_0800207C) it blinks 16 frames and goes, and outside the screen margins 4 it goes. Its trail (sub_0802C9E8, sub_0802C944, sub_0802DC60) keeps a 10-entry history of its whole position (entry 0 of the fresh entity is left at (0, 0), as natively) and draws frames 3, 4, 5 at entries 1, 2, 3. A local runtime run with soul 24 equipped drew both daggers and their trails. SkillAxeArmorUse shows still frame 6 (its parameter) of the same resource at Soma's whole X and 26 pixels above his whole Y, unmirrored; it flies 3.25 pixels a frame toward his facing and is pulled back by 0x1000 a frame while within 8 pixels a frame, turns 0x800 a frame (sub_0803E058 with + 0x2A << 11), keeps the type-5 box (-10, -10, 20, 20) of 0x1414F6F6 and is deleted the frame after it leaves the screen margins 4. Its two sub_080240A0 afterimages show its whole X 3 and 6 frames back (a 10-entry history), its current Y and an angle lagging by that many steps, with no attack, and go when it reaches state 2. A local runtime run with soul 5 equipped drew the axe turning away with its afterimages. A local runtime run with soul 4 equipped drew the drops as a stream from Soma's hand. A local runtime run with soul 3 equipped drew the bone rising and turning. A local runtime run with soul 6 equipped drew the bow behind Soma and an arrow leaving it to the right. A local runtime run with soul 2 equipped cast it (MP 80 to 75, state 2, animation 22) and drew the expanding ring. Source-derived and unit-tested, not emulator-compared. SkillTinyDevilUse (sound 0x1A3) creates three SkillTinyDevilUpdate fires at angles -0x1800, 0 and 0x1800 (mirrored facing left); each plays sequence 0 of resource 21 (exported as Enemy/skill_devil with its 16 direction frames), then picks the first filled homing-list slot from random & 3 (sub_08021924 registers, sub_08021DC4 ages), plays 0x134 and homes, turning 0x100 a frame and speeding up by 0x4000 to 0x50000, until its 64-frame timer, its hit or its target's death sends it straight; its type-5 box is (-8, -8, 16, 16) and it goes outside the screen margins 2. A local run in room 0/6 registered three enemies and sent the fires at them. sub_SkillBlueCrowUse (sound 0xEE) shows looping sequence 2 of resource 31 (exported as Enemy/skill_crow with its frame boxes), facing away from Soma's facing, and circles him for 50 frames: angle 0x1000 stepping -0x147 (0x7000 and +0x147 facing left), radius sin(timer * 0x28F) * 60 pixels around (0, -18) from Soma, attacking with its frame's box. A local run drew the crow arcing around Soma. SkillFleamanUse (sound 0x19C) places a flea of resource 20 (exported as Enemy/skill_flea with its still frames, boxes and the walker probes 0x084F13BE) at Soma's facing and (8, -26), turned to walk ahead of him with gravity 0x2000; for 160 frames it steps the probe walker sub_0806CAF8 at 1.5 (mode 8, shared with the enemies through aos_enemy_probe_walk), shows still frame 0, 1 or 2 by its vertical speed, turns at walls and on landing hops at -1.0 three times in four, else at -5.0 plus a random fraction; then it blinks through sequence 1 for 16 frames and goes; it goes too outside the screen margins 2 and attacks with its frame's box. A local run drew it hopping ahead of Soma. Flags 4 and 0x40 of the throw routine are ported for parameter 5 (the Zombie Soldier's grenade, still frame 3 of resource 7): it rolls with the probe walker sub_0806CAF8 (mode 0, the speed negated, probes 0x084F1388) and its gravity, turns at walls, bounces at -vy / 2 with sound 0x11B when it lands faster than 1.0 and otherwise stops rolling; its attack stays off until its 0x50-frame fuse runs out, when sub_08045A98(x, y, 0) creates burst list 0 (0x08525498, exported as metadata/bursts.tsv: 18 pieces whose sub_08045E80 waits its delay, moves and animates) with sound 0xD7, the throw is hidden and its 0x2838E2E4 box (-28, -30, 56, 40) attacks for the 2 + 16 frames of states 2 and 4. Every throw now plays its record's song (0xE6 for these two). A local run drew grenades rolling and bursting. SkillGhostUse requests 0xD6 and creates two sequence-1 spirits from resource 21; each selects the nearest homing-list target, otherwise turns toward native-range random points, accelerates and steers for 64 frames with its type-5 box, then leaves through margin entry 2. A local room 0/3 capture drew the cast. Source-derived and unit-tested, not emulator-compared. SkillSirenUse parameter 0 chooses still frame 2 or 3 of resource 0 and sound 0xDD or 0xDE from its first random draw, starts at Soma's facing and (8, -26), flies four pixels per frame unmirrored with sine Y motion seeded by its second draw, attacks with (-4, -4, 8, 8), and goes one frame after a hit or margin-1 exit. A local room 0/3 capture drew the note. Source-derived and unit-tested, not emulator-compared.

Tests: [`tests/test_aos_soul.c`](../tests/test_aos_soul.c), [`tests/test_aos_skill_throw.c`](../tests/test_aos_skill_throw.c), [`tests/test_aos_skill_merman.c`](../tests/test_aos_skill_merman.c), [`tests/test_aos_skill_axe.c`](../tests/test_aos_skill_axe.c), [`tests/test_aos_skill_spear.c`](../tests/test_aos_skill_spear.c), [`tests/test_aos_skill_shot.c`](../tests/test_aos_skill_shot.c), [`tests/test_aos_skill_dagger.c`](../tests/test_aos_skill_dagger.c), [`tests/test_aos_skill_devil.c`](../tests/test_aos_skill_devil.c), [`tests/test_aos_skill_crow.c`](../tests/test_aos_skill_crow.c), [`tests/test_aos_skill_flea.c`](../tests/test_aos_skill_flea.c), [`tests/test_aos_soma.c`](../tests/test_aos_soma.c), [`tests/test_aos_save.c`](../tests/test_aos_save.c), [`tests/test_aos_enemy.c`](../tests/test_aos_enemy.c), [`tests/test_aos_skill_ghost.c`](../tests/test_aos_skill_ghost.c), [`tests/test_aos_skill_siren.c`](../tests/test_aos_skill_siren.c).
Dependencies: `aos.souls.inventory_pickups`, `aos.player.soma_controller`, `aos.progression.equipment_stats`.  
Known divergences: Only SkillBatUse, SkillWingedSkeletonUse, SkillSkullArcherUse, SkillMermanUse, SkillAxeArmorUse, SkillDurgaUse, SkillHarpyUse, SkillTinyDevilUse, sub_SkillBlueCrowUse, SkillFleamanUse, SkillGhostUse, SkillSirenUse parameter 0 and the shared throw routine for its records whose flags are 0x80, 4 or 0x40 (parameters 3 and 5) have skill entities; the runtime refuses the other red souls instead of spending MP, including the other users of the throw routine (parameters 0 and 1), whose terrain probe 2, flash 0x20, landing 0x10 and timer 0x400 flags are not ported. sub_08042A54(1, 0x1E) is not run, and its turn is drawn about the frame's centre rather than the native affine object's. The Merman drops' splashes (sub_0802DAD4) and the spout's sound 0xED are not created or played. Durga's sound 0xC7 and the Harpy's sound 0xE6 are not played; the dagger's trail is kept inside the dagger's skill entity and drawn behind it (natively a separate entity in slots 0x49-0xCF that deletes itself after the dagger's slot empties). The spear's sound 0xE4 and its tip's sub_08042A54(1, 0x14) are not played or run. The axe's afterimages are drawn at half opacity (their semi-transparent mode's blend weights are not traced), and sound 0xE3 is not played. Child skill entities update in the frame they are created when their slot follows the parent's, which is not verified against the native entity order. The arrow's stuck helper sub_080239E4 and its sound 0xC8 are not ported, the arrow's first update both sets its speed and moves (the split between its states 0 and 1 is not verified frame by frame), and the arrow's hit has not been observed against an enemy in a runtime run. sub_08021530 is ported for type 5 in normal play only (the gEwramData + 0x4CC bit 6 alternative using 0x133EE is not, and the + 0xA1 bit 4 reduction is never set by the runtime); the soul hit takes the elements of the record's + 0xC on the assumption that a type-5 attacker reaches sub_0806B7D8's flag-8 path, and the per-enemy reactions documented as weapon-only (Valkyrie, harpy and siren recoils) stay weapon-only. The ring's damage was not observed in a runtime run (no enemy near the start). Sound 0xE2, sub_08042A54(1, 0xF), the cast's per-animation palette and the 0x131B8 & 0x800 gate. sub_08021994 is ported for its MP regeneration only (the curse states of unk_13270 and the earlier input-driven effect of unk_13276 are not), the cast state's pose-3 recoil (+ 0x0B with + 0x6E == 3, sound 0xEB) is not modelled, and the + 0x1E byte the poses write has no traced reader. There is no soul menu: a red soul is equipped through the file or --red-soul. Skill entities are cleared on a room change. The Julius variant (sub_08034624, table 0x080E1ED8) is not ported. The homing list is kept by the runtime as room entity indices: every active, unrewarded enemy inside the screen box (-16, 0, 0x110, 0xB0) registers each frame, which approximates the native sub_0806CC20 call sites, the forced target 0x13220 is never set, and the Tiny Devil fires' afterimage trails (sub_0802383C) are not ported. The flea's end sparks (sub_08045CEC(x, y, 0, 7)) are not created, only their two random draws are made. SkillGhostUse does not create sub_0802A3CC's native visual trail and the boss-only forced target is not connected. SkillSirenUse's non-zero parameter path and its trail helpers are not ported; the Siren soul record uses parameter 0.  
Next action: Port the remaining red soul skills in order of availability, then the soul equipment menu and the blue and yellow souls.

[+] **`aos.weapons.weapon_entity` — Soma weapon entity and attack boxes.** Weapon classes 0, 2 and 3 (sub_080221CC), class 1 (sub_080224BC: the swing, its affine angle and lifetime), class 4 (sub_08022A54: the gun, its accelerating shot with the box (-8, -4, 16, 8) packed in the pointer 0x0810FCF8, the wall impact and the bouncing casing of sub_080228F8) and class 5 (sub_08022DEC: class 0 without the deletion on Soma's 0x20) are ported; the native C11 asset path exports all 59 records, frame boxes and sprite sequences byte-identically.

Next action: Port sub_08033F58's player copy if its data source is traced; play the sounds once the Aria sound path exists.

[+] **`aos.enemies.axe_armor` — Axe armor, its axe and the probe walker.** Walking with the native probe walker, patrols, high and low throws, the returning axe with its strike flame (sub_0806D5C0), the death with its fire column and sparks (sub_080B11DC / sub_08045CEC: animation 1 row 8, animation 16 row 6) and state 3 under gEwramData + 0x8E bit 0x40 are ported. PlaySong requests occur for a struck axe (0x86), death start (0x6F), each eighth-frame spark (0x6E) and the red-soul axe (0xE3); the runtime resolves them through the exported 495-entry gSongTable. Source-derived and unit-tested, not emulator-compared.

Known divergences: The axe turns clockwise for a positive angle after its horizontal flip (sub_0803E058 traced); the affine double-size placement is not modelled. Sound requests are timed and routed through the real song player and priority metadata, but the MP2K sequencer, sample banks, mixer and SDL renderer are absent, so they are not audible. The palette bits and the global pause 0x4BE are not ported. The walker's one-way platform bit is not used by the armor (mode 0x14) and is not ported.  
Next action: Port the MP2K sequencer, voice and sample banks, mixer and SDL sink, then compare timing and output with an emulator.
Evidence: in game: `aria area_00_room_015: knife kill at frame 63 (--spawn 300 430 --atk 80 --repeat 2), fire and sparks captured at frames 72, 82 and 94 (5, 11 and 18 effects)`.

[+] **`aos.enemies.bat` — Bat enemy.** Hanging, swoop, combat, the falling death with its flames (sub_0806BE74 over 8 x 6 pixels every 15 frames, sub_0806E524 with animation 0 of the shared effect frames 0x0820ED60, palette row 7 = bank 3 of 0x082099FC) and the inert state 4 under gEwramData + 0x8E bit 0x40 are ported. Its death plays sound 0x72 through PlaySong (unit-tested; the mixer rendered it in a local run).

[+] **`aos.enemies.beam_skeleton` — Beam skeleton.** The patrol at 0.375 around its home column (turning 40 pixels away, at walls and the room's edges), the closing in at a random braked pace toward or away from the player with its wait (+ 0x1C) and patience (+ 0x19), the pause, the attack (animation 2, 33 frames charging, animation 4 firing at sprite frame 0x13, 43 frames of full beam, 64 frames shrinking), the beam (12.0 bolts from the eye looping beyond 92 pixels, 3/2 of its contact attack, harmless once shrinking) with the translucent muzzle flash, and the death (hidden, seven bones of sub_0806C5AC from 0x08528B17 flung away from the player) are ported, with state 4 under gEwramData + 0x8E bit 0x40. Its shattering plays sound 0x6B (sub_080CB4F0), unit-tested and rendered by the mixer in a local run.

[+] **`aos.enemies.curly` — Curly.** Pacing at 0.5 slowed uphill (sub_0806AC5C), turning at walls, drops and the room's edges, chasing the player once hurt, the lunging attack with three chances in four, the frame boxes, the generic death with flames and state 3 under gEwramData + 0x8E bit 0x40 are ported. [+] **`aos.enemies.ghost` — Ghost.** The native sheet, palette and frame boxes are connected. Animation 0 appears with collision disabled, then animation 1 attacks while the Ghost follows its random-height acceleration, damping, speed cap and close-target impulse. State 2 follows the event animation path. The eight-frame generic flame/blink death requests 0x6E. A parameter-1 record runs the hidden producer with its delay, 1-in-32 gate, active-child cap, coordinate rejection and child accounting. Source-derived and unit-tested, not emulator-compared.

Native evidence: `aos:asm:asm/code/code_080809C0.s:EnemyGhostCreate`, `aos:asm:asm/code/code_080809C0.s:EnemyGhostUpdate`, `aos:asm:asm/code/code_080809C0.s:sub_08089E64`, `aos:asm:asm/code/code_080809C0.s:sub_0808A030`, `aos:asm:asm/code/code_080809C0.s:sub_0808A0C8`, `aos:asm:asm/code/code_080809C0.s:sub_0808A1AC`, `aos:asm:asm/code/code_080809C0.s:sub_0808A1F8`.

Tests: [`tests/test_aos_enemy.c`](../tests/test_aos_enemy.c), [`tests/test_native_golden.c`](../tests/test_native_golden.c).

Producer coordinates and motion are source-derived but not emulator-compared.  
Next action: Compare the producer timing, target motion and palette callback frame by frame in an emulator.
Evidence: in game: `aria area_00_room_018 (--spawn 180 150 --hp 9999): the hidden producer creates a Ghost; it appears, homes into Soma and deals 17 contact damage`.

Harpy and Siren request 0x8B at the shared idle boundary, 0x87 on a dive, 0x89 after each volley and 0x1C3 at death; all are unit-tested. Source-derived, not emulator-compared.

[+] **`aos.enemies.merman` — Merman.** The active body now runs its submerged wait, rise, ballistic water arcs, animation-3 attack and animation-6 horizontal shot. Parameter 1 creates the hidden producer, capped at three linked bodies whose removal releases its count. The specialized 40-frame falling death, combat, global inert state, graphics, palettes, animations and frame boxes are connected. Its shot plays sound 0x7A (sub_080714A4) and its death 0x6E (sub_080715B4), unit-tested and rendered by the mixer in a local run.

Known divergences: The four direct OAM records and collision boxes are ported; the linked helper follows its parent with static frames and native offsets, and the three sub_080C7B54 visual slots follow the owner as translucent non-combat children. Their state-0 to state-2 activation, per-child timer and 24-record integer-position/frame/mirror history now match sub_08045748; palette callbacks and native sounds remain outside the body slot.  
Next action: Compare the three delayed effect trails and palette behavior against an emulator.
Evidence: in game: `aria area_03_room_000 (--spawn 200 335 --hp 9999): Nemesis body and technical children render and Soma takes its native 52-damage contact hit by frame 180`.

[~] **`aos.enemies.peeping_eye` — Peeping Eye.** The parent tracks Soma with the native angle convention; its 180 by 200 entry window runs the traced ten-stage, 40-frame extension sequence, then selects and steers toward the traced random upper-side target through two eye overlays and six linked polar frames.

Native evidence: `aos:asm:asm/code/code_080809C0.s:EnemyPeepingEyeCreate`, `aos:asm:asm/code/code_080809C0.s:EnemyPeepingEyeCreateEyeball`, `aos:asm:asm/code/code_080809C0.s:EnemyPeepingEyeUpdate`, `aos:asm:asm/code/code_080809C0.s:sub_08088D28`, `aos:asm:asm/code/code_080809C0.s:sub_08088E08`.

[+] **`aos.enemies.red_crow` — Red crow.** The perch until the player is within 120 pixels, the 128-frame hover turning to him or away, the 64-frame V-shaped swoop, the return to its perch after an odd round with the player away, the hit stun, and the blue crow's death (feathers, blood, flames; sub_080CA308 and sub_080CA3CC / sub_080CA474 copy it) with palette bank 1 are ported, with state 3 under gEwramData + 0x8E bit 0x40. Its swoop plays 0x8B (sub_080CA0D4) and its death 0x72 (sub_080CA308), unit-tested.

[+] **`aos.enemies.siren` — Siren (Harpy's routines).** The native constructor and updater alias Harpy with palette bank 1. It uses the shared flight, recoil and death states plus its distinct three-note frame 14/13/14 fan and repeating 16-frame angular bend. Its 0x8B, 0x87, 0x89 and 0x1C3 sounds are requested and unit-tested. Source-derived, not emulator-compared.

[~] **`aos.title.intro_cutscene` — Aria opening introduction controller.** The seven-stage controller retains all native durations, scrolls, blends, skip destinations and the sub_08008ED0 handoff. Its six-actor group preserves fixed-point motion, thresholds, lifetimes, alpha ownership and cleanup. sub_080093E0 retains the complete row-oriented script protocol. The verified-ROM resource view resolves the three language banks, proportional font, complete background palette, two compressed character banks and three block maps without exporting proprietary bytes. The SDL host renders BG3 through BG1 at their native fixed-point scroll positions and maps text through palette row 15. Source-derived, unit-tested, ROM-smoke-tested and locally reviewed in SDL; not emulator-compared.

Native evidence: `aos:c:src/code/code_08039340.c:sub_0803FEA4`, `aos:c:src/code/code_08039340.c:sub_0803FF64`, `aos:c:src/code/code_08039340.c:sub_08040048`, `aos:c:src/code_08008750.c:GameModeIntroCutsceneUpdate`, `aos:c:src/code_08008750.c:GameModeIntroCutsceneUpdate_inline`, `aos:c:src/code_08008750.c:sub_08008ED0`, `aos:c:src/code_08008750.c:sub_08008F40`, `aos:c:src/code_08008750.c:sub_08008FEC`, `aos:c:src/code_08008750.c:sub_08009054`, `aos:c:src/code_08008750.c:sub_08009178`, `aos:c:src/code_08008750.c:sub_080092A0`, `aos:c:src/code_08008750.c:sub_080093E0`, `aos:c:src/code_08008750.c:sub_080094AC`, `aos:c:src/code_08008750.c:sub_08009610`, `aos:c:src/code_08008750.c:sub_08009648`.

### Music and sound effects

[?] **`aos.audio.inventory-boundary` — source coverage.** The current classifier assigns 101 discovered routine(s) to this category (99 by source path, 2 by native name). Category completeness has not been established.

[~] **`aos.audio.play_song` — PlaySong and audio control.** The native C11 importer reads all 495 gSongTable entries plus each fixed MP2K header, and aria_sound exports their header pointer, player, channel, track count, block count, priority and reverb into a private manifest guarded by a golden digest. The Aria runtime loads the player and priority fields before PlaySong runs and advances MPlayVolumeUpdate once per lifecycle frame. A bounded host-neutral MP2K track decoder now reproduces the native wait clock table, running status, FINE, GOTO, three-level PATT and PEND, REPT, EOT, note parsing and the PRIO, TEMPO, KEYSH, VOICE, VOL, PAN, BEND, BENDR, LFOS, LFODL, MOD, MODT and TUNE state changes. Invalid pointers, recursive command loops and unsupported PORT or XCMD streams fail closed. Source-derived and unit-tested, not emulator-compared. The private MP2K bank (0x0827B2C0-0x084F08F8: the voice groups the song headers use, from the decompilation's audio segment, through gMPlayTable and the song data) is exported as sound/runtime/sound_bank.bin; aos_mp2k_player_start reads a song header (track count, block count, priority, reverb, voice group) and starts each track at its pointer, failing closed outside the bank. StartSong calls it only after its player-priority replacement accepts the song. A local --check run initialized all 495 song headers of the exported table. aos_mp2k_player_frame ports MP2KPlayerMain's tempo clock: each frame the counter (+ 0x22) gains the interval (+ 0x20); while it is at least 150 every existing track advances one clock, MP2K_event_tempo sets the raw tempo to twice its value and the interval to raw * scale >> 8, the clock (+ 0x0C) counts, and a clock that finds no track ends the player (+ 0x04 = 0x80000000); then the counter loses 150. The runtime runs it for every player each frame after the volume update. A local --check ran all 495 songs for 600 frames each: 340 ended and none hit an unsupported or invalid stream. The channel side of MP2K_event_nxx is ported: each note (logged with the voice and track priority current when it was read) resolves its tone from the voice group (key split 0x40 through its key table, rhythm 0x80 with the entry's key and pan, a nested split or rhythm dropped), takes priority min(255, player + track) and a channel: its CGB channel when free, stopping, lower or equal from a track not before it; otherwise the first free of the 8 DirectSound channels of DEFAULT_SOUND_MODE, else the best stopping one, else the lowest active one not above it. Gates count down at each track clock and stop their channel at 0; MP2K_event_endtie stops the newest unstopped channel of the track with its key; MPlayStart's TrackStop stops and unlinks the player's channels. A local --check gave 5640 channel notes and 54 dropped ones over 600 frames of every song. TrkVolPitSet (volume split by pan, key shift and pitch from bend, range, tune and the public controls), ChnVolSetAsm (each channel's right and left volume from its velocity and rhythm pan) and MidiKeyToFreq (gScaleTable as its closed form, gFreqTable, the 178 clamp) are ported; they run at each note and after every player frame for the linked channels, which gives the values the native VOLSET and PITSET flags guard. A local --check read every DirectSound sample's rate from the exported bank (none outside it). SoundMainRAM without reverb is ported as aos_mp2k_mix: per frame each DirectSound channel's envelope steps (a start begins the attack with the wave's loop flag, attack to 0xFF, decay to sustain, release on a stop down to the echo volume, the echo countdown), its mix volume is (right + left) * envelope >> 9 with master volume 15, and 176 signed 8-bit mono samples at 10512 Hz (DEFAULT_SOUND_MODE, divFreq 798) are mixed with the native wrapping byte sums: fixed-frequency tones one sample per output, the others linearly interpolated in 23-bit steps of frequency * divFreq, looping waves restarting inside their loop. The runtime plays it through an SDL audio stream (--mute disables it) and can record it (--audio-wav); --play-song requests a song at the start. A local offscreen run recorded 600 frames of song 1 as 105600 samples, 95 percent non-zero, RMS 10-15 per second. sub_08013960 is ported as aos_room_music_enter and runs at the start and at each room change: save and warp rooms quiet the music, transition rooms mute player 0 once (sub_08013F6C(0x1040)) and forget the song, other rooms set it loud and play the area's song (sUnk_084F106C, sUnk_084F1086 for Julius) or a room's special song (0x2A, 0x27, 0x29, 0x25 by their room and event bits), stopping the previous one. The sound export writes metadata room_music.tsv (save and warp bits of the map table 0x08116650, transition rooms from sUnk_0850E968: 17 save, 8 warp and 16 transition rooms). A local run of room 0/3 recorded the area's song with no --play-song.

Native evidence: `aos:asm:asm/m4a0.s:ChnVolSetAsm`, `aos:asm:asm/m4a0.s:MP2KPlayerMain`, `aos:asm:asm/m4a0.s:MP2K_event_bend`, `aos:asm:asm/m4a0.s:MP2K_event_bendr`, `aos:asm:asm/m4a0.s:MP2K_event_endtie`, `aos:asm:asm/m4a0.s:MP2K_event_fine`, `aos:asm:asm/m4a0.s:MP2K_event_goto`, `aos:asm:asm/m4a0.s:MP2K_event_keysh`, `aos:asm:asm/m4a0.s:MP2K_event_lfodl`, `aos:asm:asm/m4a0.s:MP2K_event_lfos`, `aos:asm:asm/m4a0.s:MP2K_event_mod`, `aos:asm:asm/m4a0.s:MP2K_event_modt`, `aos:asm:asm/m4a0.s:MP2K_event_nxx`, `aos:asm:asm/m4a0.s:MP2K_event_pan`, `aos:asm:asm/m4a0.s:MP2K_event_patt`, `aos:asm:asm/m4a0.s:MP2K_event_pend`, `aos:asm:asm/m4a0.s:MP2K_event_prio`, `aos:asm:asm/m4a0.s:MP2K_event_rept`, `aos:asm:asm/m4a0.s:MP2K_event_tempo`, `aos:asm:asm/m4a0.s:MP2K_event_tune`, `aos:asm:asm/m4a0.s:MP2K_event_voice`, `aos:asm:asm/m4a0.s:MP2K_event_vol`, `aos:asm:asm/m4a0.s:SoundMain`, `aos:asm:asm/m4a0.s:SoundMainRAM`, `aos:asm:asm/m4a0.s:SoundMainRAM_NoReverb`, `aos:asm:asm/m4a0.s:TrackStop`, `aos:asm:asm/m4a0.s:umul3232H32`, `aos:c:src/code_08001194.c:CheckRoomTransition`, `aos:c:src/code_08001194.c:GetSaveRoomFlagFromMapPosition`, `aos:c:src/code_08001194.c:GetWarpRoomFlagFromMapPosition`, `aos:c:src/code_08013960.c:sub_08013960`, `aos:c:src/code_08013960.c:sub_08013F6C`, `aos:c:src/code_080D73B8.c:AudioCommand`, `aos:c:src/code_080D73B8.c:MPlayVolumeSet`, `aos:c:src/code_080D73B8.c:MPlayVolumeUpdate`, `aos:c:src/code_080D73B8.c:PlaySong`, `aos:c:src/code_080D73B8.c:StartSong`, `aos:c:src/m4a.c:MPlayStart`, `aos:c:src/m4a.c:MidiKeyToFreq`, `aos:c:src/m4a.c:TrkVolPitSet`.  
Local implementation: [`src/assets/aos/sound.c`](../src/assets/aos/sound.c), [`src/runtime/aos_audio.c`](../src/runtime/aos_audio.c), [`src/runtime/aos_room_music.c`](../src/runtime/aos_room_music.c), [`src/runtime/aos_mp2k.c`](../src/runtime/aos_mp2k.c), [`src/runtime/aria_runtime.c`](../src/runtime/aria_runtime.c), [`src/pipeline/tasks.c`](../src/pipeline/tasks.c).  
Tests: [`tests/test_fusion_assets_aos.c`](../tests/test_fusion_assets_aos.c), [`tests/test_aos_audio.c`](../tests/test_aos_audio.c), [`tests/test_aos_room_music.c`](../tests/test_aos_room_music.c), [`tests/test_aos_mp2k.c`](../tests/test_aos_mp2k.c), [`tests/test_native_golden.c`](../tests/test_native_golden.c).
Known divergences: MP2KPlayerMain is ported for its sequencer clock and channel gates only: fades (FadeOutBody), track LFOs and TrkVolPitSet are not run. The CGB channels are allocated but neither computed (MidiKeyToCgbFreq) nor synthesized, the reverb path of SoundMainRAM (songs whose header sets reverb, such as 0x99) and the pseudo echo set by XCMD are not ported, the mixer output is pushed to SDL once per runtime step (the runtime's fixed step, not the GBA's 59.73 Hz VBlank), and the result has not been compared by ear or against an emulator; TrkVolPitSet runs without the LFO (modCalculated stays 0) and the public controls (volPublic and the others) keep their start values because MPlayVolumeControl is not wired to the tracks; nothing frees a stopped channel, so stopped channels stay the preferred victims. The track-address comparisons of the channel choice order players by index, an assumption about the players' memory layout. The decoder's PORT and XCMD events, tempo-to-frame scheduler, voice allocation and gate countdown are absent. m4aSoundMode hardware changes, the voice and sample banks, mixer and SDL output remain absent, so the controller still produces no sound. Room music takes a room's save, warp and transition flags from any of its map cells (natively the cell under the camera at load); the event bits of unk_37E, Julius, the boss mode and unk_4BE stay clear in the runtime, and the fade flag (unk_42C bit 0x40000) is never cleared because its clearing is not traced.  
Next action: Listen to and compare the output (emulator capture), then port the CGB channels (MidiKeyToCgbFreq, CgbSound), the reverb path, the LFO and the public volume controls, and wire the native PlaySong requests of rooms, monsters and menus.

[~] **`aos.death.soma` — Soma death, death fade and game-over screen.** Soma's death (sub_0801AF20) enters the in-game death sub-state: sub_0800C8C8 fades BG to black over 0x40 frames and OBJ over 0x80 and enters GAME_OVER once the OBJ fade has ended. GameModeGameOverUpdate shows BG1 in 256 colours (tiles 0x080E5C08 and 0x080E5C10, palette 0x080E6A3C, map 0x080E9584; the background renderer now supports 8-bpp tiles), fades it in from black over 0x20 frames, waits 0x80 frames, creates the language's GAME OVER sprite at (0x80, 0xE) and blends it in over 0x20 frames (BLDALPHA from EVA 0 / EVB 16 to 16 / 0), waits for any new press, fades to white over 0x20 frames and returns to the Konami logo. The runtime runs the death fade (Soma died at frame 284 in room 0/3 and the game over followed at frame 412) and relaunches the frontend with --game-over, which shows the native painting and sprite from the ROM and continues through the boot modes. An offscreen render of the screen showed the native painting with 'Game Over'. Source-derived and unit-tested, not emulator-compared.

Native evidence: `aos:asm:asm/code/code_08014548.s:sub_0801AF20`, `aos:c:src/code/code_08039340.c:sub_0803CDF0`, `aos:c:src/code/code_08039340.c:sub_0803D06C`, `aos:c:src/code/code_08039340.c:sub_0803D18C`, `aos:c:src/code/code_08039340.c:sub_0803D408`, `aos:c:src/code/code_08039340.c:sub_0803E8BC`, `aos:c:src/code_0800B700.c:sub_0800C8C8`, `aos:c:src/code_08013960.c:GameModeGameOverUpdate`, `aos:c:src/code_08013960.c:sub_080142E8`.  
Local implementation: [`src/runtime/aos_soma.c`](../src/runtime/aos_soma.c), [`src/runtime/aos_game_over.c`](../src/runtime/aos_game_over.c), [`src/runtime/aos_game_over_graphics.c`](../src/runtime/aos_game_over_graphics.c), [`src/runtime/aos_game_lifecycle.c`](../src/runtime/aos_game_lifecycle.c), [`src/runtime/aos_title_preview.c`](../src/runtime/aos_title_preview.c), [`src/runtime/aria_runtime.c`](../src/runtime/aria_runtime.c), [`src/assets/aos/runtime_room.c`](../src/assets/aos/runtime_room.c), [`src/assets/aos/objects.c`](../src/assets/aos/objects.c).  
Tests: [`tests/test_aos_soma.c`](../tests/test_aos_soma.c), [`tests/test_aos_game_over.c`](../tests/test_aos_game_over.c), [`tests/test_aos_game_over_graphics.c`](../tests/test_aos_game_over_graphics.c), [`tests/test_aos_game_lifecycle.c`](../tests/test_aos_game_lifecycle.c), [`tests/test_fusion_assets_aos.c`](../tests/test_fusion_assets_aos.c).
Dependencies: `aos.startup.mode_dispatch`, `aos.title.input_stage8`.  
Known divergences: The palette-fade processor that applies sub_0803D18C / sub_0803CDF0 each frame is not decompiled: fades are modelled as their traced step (sub_0803E8BC) added once per frame until the target. The runtime shows the BG and OBJ fades with a colour modulation and a black veil, and the frontend the game-over fades with a veil (exact for one shared weight). The 0x42C & 0x200000 restart branch of sub_0800C8C8, the music (MUS_15, the mute) and the death particles of sub_0801B03C are not ported. The runtime hands over to the frontend process (--frontend, --game-over) rather than switching mode in one process, and no save is touched (the native game over returns to the Konami logo).  
Next action: Port the 0x42C & 0x200000 restart and the death particles, then the in-game pause and the save-room reload on continue.
